# Hazel's Grove side stories (S23–S31)

## S23. Three Visitors

*A Christmas Carol (Charles Dickens, 1843): Marley’s warning, the three spirits, Fezziwig’s ball, Belle, Fred’s guessing game, Ignorance and Want, the unmourned grave and the vow. Public domain; lifted lines from the 1843 text (Project Gutenberg #46), listed in the file header* · audience: main · where: A note under your door; then Corvin Vale’s cottage up the valley, and wherever the visitors walk · when: The morning after c9-close, on every chapter 9 route (the Collector partner route included). It runs over the three nights before the Long Night and closes when c10-open plays.

**Cast:** Briar (the Ghost of Christmas Past (A Christmas Carol); here the sun warden’s echo), Hush (the Ghost of Christmas Present (A Christmas Carol); here the moon warden’s echo), Aldous Vale (Jacob Marley’s ghost (A Christmas Carol); Corvin’s father from S13), Young Hazel (Fezziwig’s ball and Belle’s farewell (A Christmas Carol), in this story’s memories only), Corvin, at twenty-two (young Scrooge (A Christmas Carol))

### Step 1: Go up the valley to Corvin’s cottage at dusk.

Hint: Take the road past the bridge, where the frost starts. His is the square garden. · check: `visit:s23-cottage:dusk`

#### Scene `s23-note`

_The morning after the shard came home, there is a note on the mat inside your door._  
_Very good paper. A very neat hand, every letter standing up straight, as if somebody were checking._  
**Corvin Vale** _(think)_: “Something came last night. I’d like a witness. C.V.”  
_On the back, smaller, as if it were added at the door: “Dusk.”_  
_Next door, Fern is pretending to prune something over the fence, in December, when there is nothing to prune._  
**Fern** _(shock)_: Is that from him? Up the valley? Don’t go.  
**Fern** _(think)_: …You’re going. You’ve got the chin out. Take a scarf. Take two.  

#### Done `s23-dusk`

_Dusk, up the valley. Corvin’s garden is empty, and the gate stands open, the way he left it._  
_Nothing has come back. The rows are only lines of bare earth in the frost, very straight._  
_There is a light in one window. When you knock, the door opens before you’ve finished._  

### Step 2: Sit up with Corvin until midnight.

Hint: Take the second chair. Don’t light the stove: he’d rather you didn’t. · check: `sit:s23-second-chair:midnight`

#### Scene `s23-chairs`

_Inside, the cottage is clean and silent and nearly empty. The stove is laid, and not lit._  
_In front of it are two chairs, set square, a little apart. One has a cushion that has never been sat on._  
**Corvin Vale**: You came. Good. Sit. The one with the cushion. I bought it for visitors. There haven’t been any.  
**Corvin Vale**: The stove is laid. I don’t light it. I considered offering you tea. It was close.  
_He sits in the other, his left hand flat on his coat over the breast pocket. The pocket is empty._  
_He keeps his hand there anyway. Now and then he presses, as if to check._  
**Corvin Vale** _(think)_: Last night, at midnight, something knocked. Not at the front.  
**Corvin Vale**: At that one.  
_He nods at a second door in the back wall. It is nailed shut, and the nails have rusted into the frame._  
**Corvin Vale**: That door hasn’t opened in twenty-two years. It faces the valley. I didn’t care for the view.  
**Corvin Vale**: I didn’t answer. I sat here, and I felt nothing. I’d like that confirmed by somebody else.  
_Your Nubbin climbs onto his boots and settles there, the way it sits on yours. He looks down at it._  
_Corvin looks at the Nubbin on his boots for a long time. He does not move his feet._  
_His feet have gone to sleep, and he lets them._  
**Corvin Vale** _(think)_: It’s warm. I can see that it’s warm. I can’t feel that it is. Do you understand the difference?  

### Step 3: Wait for the first of the three.

Hint: Wherever you chose to be, stay there. One o’clock comes on its own. · check: `wait:s23-first-night:one-oclock`

#### Scene `s23-caller`

_Midnight. The clock strikes, and the nailed door doesn’t open, and someone comes in through it anyway._  
_Small and round, gold at the edges the way old memories are, and grey all through. You can see the nails through him._  
**Corvin Vale** _(shock)_: …Thimble.  
**Corvin Vale** _(soft)_: Hazel’s. One of hers. He was the first one I—  
_He doesn’t finish. Thimble crosses the floor without a sound and sits on Corvin’s other boot._  
_Then he goes to the window and touches the frosted glass: once, twice, three times. Three small prints._  
_He looks at you, in the second chair, as if making sure the witness is counted. Then at Corvin._  
**Corvin Vale** _(think)_: Three.  
**Corvin Vale**: And I’m to sit up for them.  
_When you look back at the window, Thimble has gone. The three prints are still there._  
_Corvin takes out a ledger and a pencil, and writes down the time. Under it, in the same square hand: “Nothing felt.”_  
**Corvin Vale**: Thank you. You’ve witnessed it. You can go home now.  
_He says it kindly, and he means it. It is the first thing he has asked of you that isn’t a price._  

**Choices**

- **Go out, and sit on his step instead.** — sets `{"s23-stay":"step"}`
    - You go out and sit on his front step in the frost, with your back to his door.
    - After a while the latch clicks behind you: not opening, just checking that you’re there.
    - Inside, the clock begins to strike one.
- **Go home, as he asks.** — sets `{"s23-stay":"home"}`
    - You go. The lane is white and silent, and your breath walks on ahead of you.
    - At the bend you look back. One window is lit, and inside, very faintly, a clock begins to strike one.
- **Tell him flatly you’re staying.** — sets `{"s23-stay":"insist"}`
    - **You**: I’m staying.
    - **Corvin Vale**: …I see.
    - He looks at you for a long moment, like a sum that won’t come out. Then he gets up and fills the kettle.
    - The clock begins to strike one.

### Step 4: Walk with Briar and Corvin into what was.

Hint: Keep close to her light. Visions don’t wait, but they don’t hurry either. · check: `walk:s23-briar:what-was`

#### Scene `s23-briar`

_One o’clock. A small warm light comes up the lane, bobbing, with a laugh in it somewhere._  
_It doesn’t matter where you were: lane, step or chair. When it comes in at the nailed door, you are in the room to meet it._  
_It is a Nubbin, or the shape of one, made of morning. Light springs up off the crown of her head like water from a spring._  
**Corvin Vale**: Who are you?  
**Briar** _(happy)_: Briar. What was.  
**Corvin Vale** _(think)_: Long ago?  
**Briar**: No. Your past.  
_She laughs, not unkindly: the laugh of someone who has just noticed how serious everybody is being._  
**Corvin Vale**: You’re the one in the song. The stream lantern. You’ve been gone longer than this cottage has stood.  
**Briar** _(happy)_: Gone. Yes. Up you get, Corvin Vale. You too, witness.  
_She takes his hand, and his hand doesn’t notice. Her other paw she holds out to you, and it is like holding a patch of sun._  
_The back wall of the cottage goes thin as paper, and then it isn’t there at all._  

### Step 5: Watch Lantern Night, twenty-two years ago.

Hint: Stay by the pond. When the fiddle starts, nobody minds where you stand. · check: `watch:s23-lantern-night:reel`

#### Scene `s23-lantern-night`

_Then it is summer, all at once: the Grove at dusk, twenty-two years ago, on Lantern Night._  
_The gate opens at a touch, like any gate. You have never once seen it do that._  
_A young man stands just inside it in a thin coat, as if he isn’t sure he’s allowed any further._  
_A woman comes down the path with a lantern in each hand, laughing at someone behind her. She isn’t old yet._  
**Young Hazel** _(happy)_: Corvin. Hold this. No, by the handle, you’ll have your eyebrows off.  
_She gives him her lantern, and he holds it as if it might go off. Nobody has ever handed him anything lit before._  
_Then there is a fiddle, and a reel on the grass by the pond, and the whole valley dancing it badly._  
_Corvin dances it worst. He goes left when everyone goes right, and treads on Hazel twice._  
_She laughs. Not at him: with him, until he laughs too, helplessly, bent over with his hands on his knees._  
**Briar** _(happy)_: Such a little thing. A field, some candles, a man with a fiddle.  
_Beside you, the older Corvin watches his younger self laugh. His own face doesn’t move._  
**Corvin Vale** _(think)_: It cost her nothing. Some candles. A field she already had.  
**Corvin Vale**: The happiness she gives, is quite as great as if it cost a fortune.  
_He says it flat, like a clerk reading back a sum. As if he has found it written down in someone else’s hand._  

#### Done `s23-reel`

_The reel comes round again. Briar tugs at your sleeve until you join in at the bottom of the set._  
_You learn it the way everyone did: wrong, then less wrong, then not caring. Young Hazel spins past without seeing you._  
_Corvin doesn’t dance. He stands where his younger self stood, by the gate, holding nothing._  

### Step 6: See the winter that ended it.

Hint: There is nothing to do but watch. That’s the hard part. · check: `watch:s23-winter:gate`

#### Scene `s23-cold-one`

_The music stops, and the light goes cold and blue. It is winter in the Grove, and very quiet._  
_Too quiet: the Nubbins sit in the frosted grass in a row, not touching. None of them looks up._  
_The smallest sits on young Corvin’s boot, the way yours sat on his tonight. It is Thimble, and the grey is coming up through him like damp through paper._  
_Young Corvin doesn’t look down. His hand is flat on his coat, over a pocket that isn’t empty yet._  
_Hazel is in the cottage doorway, with snow on her shoulders. She doesn’t shout; she is past shouting._  
**Young Hazel** _(sad)_: Another idol has displaced me.  
**Corvin, at twenty-two**: What idol?  
**Young Hazel** _(soft)_: A cold one.  
_That is all. She goes in and shuts the door, and the snow keeps coming._  
_Later, in the dark, the gate opens. That is all the vision shows: a gate, opening, and the dark._  
_Corvin claps his hand over Briar’s light, hard, the way you’d put out a candle. It shines straight through his fingers._  
**Briar** _(soft)_: Your lip is trembling. And what is that upon your cheek?  
_Nothing: there is nothing on his cheek. His face is perfectly dry._  
_That is the worst of it._  

#### Done `s23-dawn-one`

_Then it is the cottage again, and the clock says a quarter past two. Briar has gone._  
_The room smells of summer for a moment, and then it doesn’t._  
_Corvin opens the ledger and writes, in the square hand: “Briar, 1 to 2.15. Nothing felt.”_  
**Corvin Vale** _(think)_: Same time tomorrow. If you’re coming.  

### Step 7: Follow Hush into what is.

Hint: She only shows tonight. Everything you see is happening right now, somewhere in the valley. · check: `walk:s23-hush:what-is`

#### Scene `s23-hush`

_The second night. Corvin is in his chair before you arrive, with the ledger open on his knee._  
_Neither of you says anything for three hours. It isn’t comfortable, and it isn’t meant to be._  
_At one o’clock the cottage goes quiet. Not silent: quiet, as if every sound has been gently put away._  
_She is in the corner, though nobody saw her come. Tall for a Nubbin, moon-pale, wrapped in her own grey like a cloak._  
_It is the quietest thing you have ever seen. Even the clock seems to tick more carefully._  
**Corvin Vale** _(soft)_: Hush.  
**Corvin Vale** _(think)_: The old wall. The one who sat with the still ones.  
**Hush** _(soft)_: Look upon me!  
_She says it so quietly it is hardly more than a breath. Then nothing more._  
_He looks, and so do you. There isn’t much to see: a pale shape, a grey cloak, patience all the way down._  
_Then she turns to the nailed door, and it lets her through, and both of you after her, into tonight._  

### Step 8: See what is happening in Ember’s kitchen tonight.

Hint: Hush takes you in through the wall. Ember can’t see you. Don’t try to help. · check: `watch:s23-ember:stove`

#### Scene `s23-stove`

_Ember’s kitchen, tonight, by the light of one lamp. The stove is lit and the kettle is on, and nobody is drinking anything._  
_Ash lies by the stove, grey, facing the corner._  
_Ember puts a bowl down beside her. Ash doesn’t turn, and Ember doesn’t wait to see if she will._  
**Ember** _(angry)_: I hope he’s cold.  
**Ember** _(angry)_: I hope he’s sitting up there in that clean house, cold to the bone, and nobody comes.  
_In the dark of her kitchen, Corvin stands very straight and listens to himself being hoped about._  
_Ember stares at the stove for a long time._  
**Ember** _(soft)_: …No.  
**Ember** _(soft)_: I hope he’s cold exactly as long as it takes.  
_She doesn’t say as long as what takes. She gets up and puts another log on, which wasn’t needed._  
**Corvin Vale** _(think)_: That’s fair. That’s more than fair.  

#### Done `s23-ash`

**Ember** _(soft)_: Ash.  
_Nothing. Ash faces the corner._  
**Ember** _(soft)_: Ash, love.  
_Nothing. Ember says it again anyway, the way you’d leave a light on for someone._  

### Step 9: Look in on the lantern shed.

Hint: The Keepers are getting ready for the Long Night. They can’t see you. Just listen. · check: `watch:s23-lantern-shed:guessing-game`

#### Scene `s23-shed`

_The lantern shed, tonight, well past midnight. It is full of people, wire and the smell of lamp oil._  
_The Keepers are readying the lanterns for the Long Night. Nobody has gone home, or seems to have thought of it._  
**Sparks** _(happy)_: Right. Guessing game. I’m thinking of an animal. Sort of an animal.  
**Byte** _(think)_: Is it a badger? It’s always a badger.  
**Sparks** _(happy)_: Not a badger. It lives up the valley. It growls. It has extremely clean railings.  
**Pixel**: Is it a cold animal?  
**Sparks** _(happy)_: VERY cold. Never laughs. Thinks laughing is a waste of breath.  
**Fern**: Is it… Corvin?  
**Sparks** _(happy)_: YES! Fern gets a biscuit!  
_Everyone laughs. Among them, in the lamp smoke, Corvin nods slowly._  
**Corvin Vale** _(think)_: Accurate.  
_Fern stops laughing first. She turns her biscuit over in her hands._  
**Fern** _(soft)_: Somebody should take him soup. He’s up there on his own, with all that.  
**Sparks** _(think)_: …Bags not.  
**Fern** _(soft)_: No. I know. I’ll do it.  

#### Done `s23-pond-chair`

_Hush leads you both out, and not by the gate. She goes over the old wall by her own post, the way the quiet ones go._  
_By the pond there is an old chair with a cushion on it, flattened in the shape of someone who sat there for years._  
**Corvin Vale**: Whose is that?  
_Hush doesn’t answer._  
_You know whose, and so does he. He doesn’t ask again._  

### Step 10: Go with Hush to the Hollow.

Hint: Follow her past the falls. Touch nothing there: you are only seeing it. · check: `walk:s23-hush:hollow`

#### Scene `s23-root`

_Past the falls, where the paths stop. The Hollow, tonight, under a hard bright frost._  
_At the edge of a ring of old roots, under one root, alone, there is a very thin grey Nubbin._  
_It isn’t eating; there is nothing near it to not eat. It isn’t doing anything at all._  
_Far off between the trees, a lantern is moving. Away from you, slowly, stopping now and then._  
**Corvin Vale** _(think)_: Who keeps a lantern out here?  
_Hush doesn’t say._  
**Corvin Vale**: Will it live?  
_Hush only looks at the root._  
_In his own garden Corvin would have known exactly what to do with a thin one. He would have made it quiet._  

#### Done `s23-cloak`

_Then Hush opens her grey cloak._  
_Underneath, huddled against her, are two grey Nubbins. They are thinner than the one under the root, older, and very still._  
**Hush** _(soft)_: This one is Fear.  
**Hush** _(soft)_: This one is Hurry.  
_Corvin looks at the second one for a long time._  
**Corvin Vale** _(soft)_: I know that one.  
_At dawn she is gone, and the cottage is only the cottage._  
_He writes it down: “Hush, 1 to 7. Nothing felt.”_  

### Step 11: Look where Cinder points.

Hint: She won’t speak. Follow her paw, not her face. · check: `watch:s23-cinder:later-spring`

#### Scene `s23-cinder`

_The third night. The ledger is open on his knee before you’ve sat down._  
_At one o’clock the room goes red at the edges, like a page held too near a candle._  
_She doesn’t come in by any door. She is simply there, by the cold stove, as if she always had been._  
_A Nubbin-shape of embers, burning low and fierce, even now. She doesn’t look at either of you._  
**Corvin Vale** _(shock)_: …Cinder.  
_She says nothing. She will say nothing, all night._  
_She raises one paw, and points._  
_The Grove, in some later spring. Every Nubbin in it sits in the sun, quite still, quite safe._  
_Nothing is hungry or frightened, and nothing runs into the dark. Nobody has gone up the hill in years._  
_The gate opens by itself, and makes no sound at all._  
**Corvin Vale** _(think)_: Whose Grove is that?  
_Cinder doesn’t answer. She only keeps pointing, until he is the one who looks away._  

### Step 12: Walk the lane on a Lantern Night that hasn’t happened yet.

Hint: Keep to the verge. The children won’t see you. · check: `walk:s23-cinder:lane`

#### Scene `s23-rhyme`

_Then a lane at dusk, on a Lantern Night years from now, with lanterns on every gatepost and children skipping in the road._  
_They chant it the way children chant old things, flat and fast. You know the tune; one word is new._  
_“Corvin held them close and tight, / kept them warm one winter night;”_  
_“when the morning came to call, / they did not move at all.”_  
_The children shriek and scatter, laughing the way you laugh at something that can’t catch you any more._  
_Corvin stands in the road where they skipped, his lips moving. He is saying it over, checking it._  
**Corvin Vale** _(think)_: Close and tight. Winter night. Came to call. Not at all.  
**Corvin Vale**: They’ve got the words right.  
_A long pause._  
**Corvin Vale** _(soft)_: They’ve got me right, too.  

### Step 13: Go where Cinder points, the last time.

Hint: The high pass, then the hill. When he asks, the answer is yours, not hers. · check: `watch:s23-cinder:high-pass`

#### Scene `s23-pass`

_Then snow, and wind. The high pass above the valley, very far off, as if seen from the bottom of a well._  
_Up there, alone, someone sits hunched over a little light: a cold ember, barely red, cupped in two hands._  
_It is a man, in a good coat gone thin. It is Corvin, sitting where she sat, keeping what she kept._  
_Then the hill, at home, in some spring. Every flower is out: Briar’s yellow one, and the rest, leaning together._  
_There is no flower for him. Only a bare patch by the path, where the frost stays after everything else has thawed._  
_Corvin kneels by it. You hear his knees crack on the frozen ground._  
**Corvin Vale** _(sad)_: Where a man’s heading tells you where he’ll end up. If he keeps on.  
**Corvin Vale** _(sad)_: But he can turn off the road, and then the end moves. Say that’s what this is. Say that’s what you’re showing me.  
_Cinder looks at him a long time. Then she holds out a paw to him._  
_He takes it. It is warm: the only warm thing in three nights._  
_She doesn’t answer him; she can’t, or won’t. So he looks up at you instead._  

**Choices**

- **“It may be.”** — sets `{"s23-answer":"maybe"}`
    - **You**: It may be.
    - He nods, as if you’ve given him a figure he can work with.
- **“Only if you keep on.”** — sets `{"s23-answer":"keep-on"}`
    - **You**: Only if you keep on.
    - He nods slowly. Keeping on is the one thing he has always known how to do.
- **Say nothing. Sit down in the snow beside him.** — sets `{"s23-answer":"sat"}`
    - You don’t answer. You sit down in the snow next to him, close enough that your shoulders touch.
    - He doesn’t move away. You stay like that until the snow stops.

#### Done `s23-dawn-three`

_Dawn, in the cottage: the stove still unlit, the ledger open on his knee._  
_He writes: “Cinder, 1 to 8.” And then, after a long time, the same as every night: “Nothing felt.”_  
_But he underlines it, as if it were a figure he no longer trusts._  

### Step 14: Hear Corvin out, at his gate.

Hint: He’ll talk if you stand there long enough. Don’t hurry him. · check: `talk:corvin:s23-railings`

#### Scene `s23-drip`

_The morning before the Long Night. You walk up the valley one more time, because it seems wrong not to._  
_Something is different about the sound of Corvin’s garden. Then you have it: dripping._  
_His railings are dripping. All along the fence, in the low sun, the frost is coming off them one bead at a time._  
_Corvin is at his gate in his shirtsleeves, watching it, holding a pan with a lid on._  
**Corvin Vale** _(think)_: Somebody left soup on my step last night. In a pan with a fern scratched on the lid.  
**Corvin Vale**: I didn’t know what to do with it. So I ate it.  
**Corvin Vale**: First time since summer, the railings. The tin’s been out of my pocket four days. They’ve only just noticed.  
**Corvin Vale** _(think)_: The first night. Three in the morning. I came out, and you were asleep on my step in the frost.  
**Corvin Vale**: I put my coat over you. I didn’t decide to. My hands did it.  
_You remember waking under a coat that wasn’t yours. You thought Briar must have left it._  
**Corvin Vale** _(think)_: I don’t know what that was. I’m only reporting it.  

#### Complete `s23-complete`

_He goes in and comes back with the ledger. He reads the last three pages to himself, moving his lips._  
**Corvin Vale** _(think)_: Briar. Hush. Cinder. What was, what is, and what’s coming.  
**Corvin Vale**: I’ll keep all three. I’ll live in all of them at once, and I won’t shut the door on any of them again.  
**Corvin Vale**: I don’t feel a word of it. I’m saying it anyway. That’s allowed, isn’t it?  
**Corvin Vale** _(think)_: You said “it may be.” On the hill.  
**Corvin Vale** _(soft)_: That isn’t a promise. I’ve been made a great many promises. I’d rather have “it may be”.  
**Corvin Vale** _(think)_: Tonight. Where do people stand, at a vigil? I’ve never been to one. I’ve only ever been the reason for one.  
**You**: The gate sticks.  
**You**: But the lane’s anybody’s.  
_He thinks about that. Then he tears the three pages out of the ledger, folds them once, and gives them to you._  
**Corvin Vale**: For your records. And take the dressing gown off the back of the door, and the cap. You sat up three nights. Dress for it.  
_Behind him the railings drip, and drip, and the sound goes all the way down the valley._  

**Rewards**

- visitors (flag): Set when all three nights have played. A sixth S9 “family” condition; Corvin stands in the lane with a lantern during the vigil; adds an epilogue line.
- Corvin’s nightcap and dressing gown (cosmetic): A long grey nightcap and a heavy dressing gown from the back of Corvin’s door. For sitting up in. Your Keeper looks very awake in them.
- Lantern Night reel (emote) (cosmetic): The reel from young Hazel’s Lantern Night, danced badly with great commitment. Nearby Nubbins join in at the bottom of the set.
- “Three Nights” journal pages (item): Three pages torn from Corvin’s ledger, in his square hand: each visitor, the hours, and “Nothing felt”, the last one underlined.

Sets on completion: `{"visitors":true,"s23Done":true}`

**Notes:** THEME. Dickens wrote a cold man shown his life on three nights who wakes able to feel. Here the visitors come while Corvin still can’t feel anything, so he watches his whole life like a ledger (“Nothing felt”, every night), and it only lands at the Long Night, when the stone is unmade. The second step of his redemption: chapter 9, S23, chapter 10, S13, S9. You are the witness; the visitors do the persuading. SIGNATURE CHOICE. Q4 (s23-caller: step | home | insist, flag s23-stay) pays off in Q16, twelve quests later: s23-drip (step: he put his coat over you at three in the morning, “My hands did it”), s23-drip-home (he left the door unlatched on nights two and three, “Don’t read into it”), s23-drip-insist (two cups of tea every night, his never drunk). Q15 (s23-pass: maybe | keep-on | sat, flag s23-answer) shapes his closing reply: s23-complete, s23-complete-keep-on, s23-complete-sat. TIMING. Opens the morning after c9-close, on every chapter 9 route. Step 1 at dusk; steps 2–6 are night one, 7–10 night two, 11–13 night three, 14 the morning before the Long Night. A step’s scene plays the moment the previous check is met; its done scene when its own check is met. The nights are story nights: each advances on its checks, not on a clock (no timers). Corvin’s cottage scenes are always at night; S6 and other day stories can play by day. If c10-open plays before step 13’s check, the story closes unfinished: no rewards, and visitors stays unset. If step 13 is done, play s23-drip and s23-complete before c10-open (they are the morning before the Long Night). VARIANTS (SIDE_STORY_S23_VARIANTS). Collector route = soldNubbin && !boughtBack when S23 opens. (1) s23-note → s23-note-bargained (corvin = bargained), s23-note-forced (corvin = forced, not Collector), s23-note-collector (Collector; wins over the corvin variants). (2) s23-dusk → s23-dusk-bargained (bargained: the rows still full) or s23-dusk-forced (forced, Collector or not: the pens wrecked). Reconciled, Collector or not, keeps s23-dusk (the garden is empty, as c9-shard-reconciled leaves it). (3) s23-caller → s23-caller-aldous when s13Done. (4) s23-briar → s23-briar-collector (Collector). (5) s23-cold-one → s23-cold-one-aldous when s13Done (Thimble’s winter is cut, since he never came). (6) s23-stove → s23-stove-collector (Collector). (7) s23-ash → s23-ash-named when s6Done. (8) s23-root → s23-root-ear when s8Reunited. (9) s23-cloak → s23-cloak-collector (Collector). (10) s23-pass → s23-pass-collector (Collector). (11) s23-drip → s23-drip-home (s23-stay = home) or s23-drip-insist (insist); step keeps s23-drip. (12) s23-complete → s23-complete-keep-on or s23-complete-sat by s23-answer; maybe keeps s23-complete. ADD-ONS (also in VARIANTS). s23-coat-letter: straight after s23-note (any version) when millLetter = kept. s23-window: when s13Done, straight after the walk:s23-briar:what-was check, before s23-lantern-night (Q6: the boy at the mill window and Tobin’s lamp). s23-partner: on the Collector route, after the talk:corvin:s23-railings check, before s23-complete*. s23-letter-back: after s23-complete* when millLetter = kept. CAMPAIGN HOOKS (VARIANTS, exact places). c10-vigil-corvin: if visitors, after c10-vigil (after c10-vigil-wisp when that plays), before the timed vigil step. Narration only: Corvin in the lane beyond the gate with a lantern. Nobody goes to him or sends him off; he doesn’t try the latch; nothing is gated by it; it is not his arrival (that stays s9-dusk). c10-dawn-lane: if visitors and keptStone false, straight after c10-dawn, before the Moss scene (c10-moss-stays | c10-moss-fades | c10-moss-mourned). He went home at first light, so s9-dusk is still his first arrival. c10-kept-lane: if visitors and keptStone true, straight after c10-kept, before c10-close. Epilogue: end-epi-visitors (visitors, keptStone false, cinder unset), end-epi-visitors-cinder (visitors and cinder set: it replaces end-epi-visitors, with the red-flower line added), end-epi-visitors-kept (visitors and keptStone: replaces it). Exactly one plays, after end-epi-gate-* and before end-epi-mill-*, in every ending. S9. visitors is the sixth “family” condition (S9’s notes already count it). TIER 2. After s23-complete, Corvin’s railings stop frosting (art: wet, dripping, then plain iron), even before the stone is unmade. Fern did take the soup up (s23-drip); Ember gains s23-ember-soup in her talk pool from chapter 11. ART AND SOUND. Echoes (Thimble, Aldous, young Hazel, young Corvin) use S1’s memory treatment: gold filter, silhouette, no portrait. Briar: warm gold light springing from her crown. Hush: tall, moon-pale, grey cloak, all sound damped while she is on screen. Cinder: ember-red, the screen red at the edges, no voice SFX. Night two shows the Keepers as they are tonight; they can’t see you (no interaction). The Long Night’s present-day Keepers are unchanged. The ledger is a readable prop. The three window prints stay on Corvin’s glass for the rest of S23. CANON. Never show Hazel’s face or name in the Hollow; the moving lantern is hers, and the thin grey is the one she carries down at dawn (she finds it the next night). Nothing here says where Hazel is. The third rule is never named. Corvin never comes to the Grove gate: Hush brings the vision in over the old wall. The Collector route never buys anyone back; do not make the numbered Nubbin recruitable here. REWARDS. All given at s23-complete (the reel emote unlocks then too, though you learn it in s23-reel). SIM: (a) new flags visitors and s23Done (approved), local s23-stay and s23-answer; (b) the closing rule above (c10-open waits for s23-drip and s23-complete once step 13 is done); (c) variant and add-on selection as listed; (d) the railing art state after s23Done; (e) s23-ember-soup in Ember’s chapter 11 talk pool when s23Done.

## S24. The Adventure Book

*Up (Pixar, 2009): a list of places someone meant to go, a load of keepsakes too heavy to carry, a stowaway with badges, a tall striped bird and an old explorer who wanted proof. Two quoted lines; all other words original* · audience: all · where: The western hill by Hazel’s flower; then the carrier’s cart, the scree path beside the falls, the far ridge and the Sunrise Shelf · when: Post-game: the first spring after chapter 12, once the memorial flowers open (after the ending has played). Any ending.

**Cast:** Tilly (Russell, the badge-collecting stowaway (Up, Pixar, 2009), for this story only; reference only), Augusta Fairweather (Charles F. Muntz (Up, Pixar, 2009), reference only: an original character who is rescued and finds peace), Professor Delve

### Step 1: Sit with Nova by Hazel’s flower on the hill.

Hint: Nova is at the top of the western hill, by the newest flower. Walk up and sit down beside her. · check: `sit:s24-hill:hazels-flower`

#### Scene `s24-tea`

_Spring. The memorial flowers on the hill have opened all at once, as if somebody gave a signal._  
_Hazel’s is the untidy one near Briar’s. It still faces the Grove, very firmly._  
_Somebody comes over the far ridge in the early light, under a pack so big it looks like a walking hedge._  
_It is Nova. She comes straight up the hill path, and at the top she stops, which is new._  
**Nova**: Morning. Don’t get up. I was passing. I’m always passing.  
**Nova** _(think)_: I thought I’d sit here a minute and not pass.  
_She sits down by Hazel’s flower with her pack still on, like a tortoise that has decided to stay for a bit._  
_Your Nubbin runs to her, then back to you, then to her again, to check you are both still there._  
**Nova** _(soft)_: Is there tea? There’s usually tea. I usually say no.  
**You**: There’s tea.  
**Nova**: Then I’ll have some. I’d like to stay for tea.  
_Nobody in the valley can remember Nova staying for tea. Down by the hedge, Fern drops a whole tray of seedlings._  

#### Done `s24-tea-done`

_You bring the pot up the hill. Nova drinks two cups, and holds the third without drinking it, for the warmth._  
**Nova** _(soft)_: She asked me in every time. Every single time. I always said I couldn’t stop.  
**Nova** _(sad)_: I could have stopped.  
_She looks at the flower for a while. Then she undoes the top of her pack._  

### Step 2: Look at the first pages of Nova’s atlas.

Hint: Nova has the atlas open on her knees. Stay sitting beside her and look. · check: `inspect:s24-atlas:first-pages`

#### Scene `s24-pages`

_The atlas comes out wrapped in oilcloth. It is fat with maps and tickets and pressed leaves, and tied shut with a bootlace._  
**Nova** _(think)_: You’ve seen the middle. The stories. Everybody has. I don’t show anyone the front.  
_She unties the bootlace. The first pages are not maps: they are a list, in pencil, in a young hand that presses hard._  
_At the top, underlined twice: PLACES I’LL GO. Underneath, smaller and crosser: (and nobody is to stop me)._  
**Nova** _(soft)_: Hazel wrote that. She was nineteen. Before the Grove. Before Moss decided she’d do.  
_“A sea with no other side”; “a city with trams”; “somewhere it snows in June”; “a mountain nobody can pronounce”._  
_Every line has a tick beside it, in ink, in a different hand. Some of the ticks are very old, and some are not._  
**Nova**: Mine. Every tick. She asked me to come back and tell her about each one after. So I did.  
**Nova** _(soft)_: I was twelve. I came through on the carrier’s cart, between one place and the next one. It was raining.  
**Nova**: She gave me tea, which I refused, and this, which I didn’t. She said, “You go. One of us should.”  

#### Done `s24-pages-done`

_One line, at the very bottom, has no tick: “The Sunrise Shelf. See the whole valley at once. Take someone.”_  
**Nova** _(sad)_: It’s on the far ridge. A day’s walk. I’ve seen a sea with no other side, and I never went a day’s walk from her door.  
**Nova**: It says take someone. I never had anyone to take.  
**Nova** _(soft)_: …I might have someone now. If someone’s free.  

### Step 3: Fetch Hazel’s lantern from its hook by the cottage door.

Hint: It hangs on the hook by the cottage door. Tap it to take it down. · check: `collect:s24-hazels-lantern`

#### Scene `s24-lantern`

**Nova** _(think)_: One more thing. Can we take her lantern? And light it up there, at sunrise?  
**Nova** _(soft)_: So she sees it. From wherever she is, being difficult about something.  
_Hazel’s lantern hangs on the hook by the cottage door, where it has hung since somebody brought it down off the hill._  
_You haven’t lit it since. You dust it, and you tell yourself you are waiting for a good reason._  
_Your Nubbin sits under it and looks up, the way it looks up at the fruit trees._  
**Nova** _(think)_: Is that all right? It’s yours now. I can stop asking for things. I’m out of practice anyway.  

#### Done `s24-lantern-done`

_The lantern is lighter than you remember. It was nearly always her carrying it._  
**Nova** _(happy)_: Right. Now I need a carrier who doesn’t ask questions.  

### Step 4: Load Nova’s pack onto the carrier’s cart. Something under the tarp is giggling.

Hint: The carrier’s cart waits at the end of the lane. Lift the tarp at the back. · check: `find:s24-tilly:carrier-cart`

#### Scene `s24-stowaway`

_The carrier’s cart goes up the ridge road on Tuesdays, and takes anyone who can lift their own luggage._  
_Nova can just about lift hers. The cart’s springs go down with a groan._  
_Under the tarp at the back, something says “ow”._  
_Then it says, much more quietly, “Nobody heard that.”_  
_Sparks comes down the lane at a run, which is how Sparks comes down a lane, waving both arms._  
**Sparks** _(shock)_: Is she in there? She’s in there. Course she is. Sorry. SORRY. She’s my cousin.  
**Sparks**: Tilly. Nine. Calls Hazel “Gran” too. Everyone under ten does; it saves explaining. She heard “ridge” and went off like a cork.  
_You lift the tarp. A girl in big wellies glares up at you, with a sash across her chest covered in home-made badges._  
**Tilly** _(angry)_: This is a stowaway situation. You can’t send a stowaway back. That’s the law of the sea.  
**Nova** _(think)_: We’re not at sea.  
**Tilly**: It’s the law of carts, then. It’s the same law.  

#### Done `s24-badges`

**Tilly** _(think)_: I need a badge. I’ve got Knots (Mostly), Brave at the Dentist, Goose Handling, and an old tin one.  
**Tilly**: I need Helping Someone Sad. Somebody here is sad. I can tell. I’m very good at it.  
_She looks straight at Nova. Nova looks at the cart’s back wheel as if it has said something interesting._  
_In the top of Tilly’s left welly there is a folded square of paper, damp at one corner._  

**Choices**

- **Say nothing about the note.**
    - You say nothing about it. Tilly notices you saying nothing, and stands up a bit straighter in the cart.
    - **Tilly**: You can come on my adventure. You’re allowed.
- **Ask her if she’s all right.**
    - **Tilly**: I’m FINE. That’s private. It’s from Gran Hazel. It isn’t for opening. It’s for having.
    - She pushes it down out of sight. Then she pats the welly, once, to check.

### Step 5: Climb the scree path beside the falls. Nova won’t let anyone carry her pack.

Hint: Follow your Nubbin. It finds the dry stones; step where it steps. · check: `climb:s24-falls-scree`

#### Scene `s24-pack`

_The cart leaves you at the foot of the falls path, where the road gives up and the water takes over._  
_Nova gets her pack onto her back in three goes. It clanks, and it is the size of a small shed._  
**You**: What’s in it?  
**Nova** _(think)_: Things. Her things. Things she gave me.  
**Nova**: A tin cup. A scarf she knitted a sleeve too long. Every postcard I sent her, and every one she sent back.  
**Nova** _(happy)_: A shell I gave her, which she gave back, and I gave her again. Twice.  
_You offer to take a strap, and she says no. Tilly offers to take a strap, and she says no, more kindly._  
**Nova**: I’ll manage. I always manage. It’s only heavy when you think about it.  
_Fern has sent a paper bag of lantern buns. It sits on top of everything, the only light thing in there._  
_Your Nubbin is already halfway up the first stretch of scree, looking back to see why you are all so slow._  

#### Done `s24-falls-top`

_The path climbs beside the falls, not behind them, through spray and roar. The stones are wet and loose._  
_Your Nubbin finds the dry ones. It runs up, waits on each, runs back to you, and goes up again._  
_At the top Tilly climbs onto a boulder, spreads her arms at the whole wet sky, and yells at the top of her voice._  
**Tilly** _(happy)_: Adventure is out there!  
**Nova** _(soft)_: …It is, isn’t it.  
_Nova is red in the face and bent nearly double under the pack. She is also, very slightly, smiling._  

### Step 6: Something has stolen a lantern bun. Meet the thief without frightening it.

Hint: Choose: hold out a bun on your flat hand, or sit down on the path and let it come to you. · check: `choose:s24-tall-bird:feed-or-wait`

#### Scene `s24-bird`

_Above the falls the ridge goes up into cloud, and the cloud is full of birdsong you don’t know._  
_You stop to eat, and set Fern’s bag of lantern buns on a rock. Then it is a bag of lantern buns minus one._  
_Something enormous is standing in the mist three strides off, with a bun in its beak._  
_It is taller than Nova and striped like a humbug, with legs like stilts and a little crest that won’t lie flat._  
**Tilly** _(shock)_: Oh. Oh. Oh, he’s BEAUTIFUL.  
**Tilly**: He’s called Sir Reginald. That’s decided. Nobody else gets a go.  
_Sir Reginald swallows the bun whole. You can watch it go all the way down._  
_It looks at the bag, and then at you. It takes one step forward and two steps back, the way Sable used to._  
**Nova** _(soft)_: Don’t grab. Whatever you do. A thing like that, you only see once if you grab.  

**Choices**

- **Hold out a bun on your flat hand.** — sets `{"s24-bird":"fed"}`
    - You hold out a bun, flat on your palm, and keep your arm very still.
    - It takes a long time. Then the bird lowers its head and takes the bun so gently you hardly feel the beak.
    - Afterwards it follows you a few steps, hopefully, in case your hand does that again.
- **Sit down on the path and wait for it to come to you.** — sets `{"s24-bird":"waited"}`
    - You sit down on the wet path. Your Nubbin sits down beside you and keeps still.
    - After a long while the bird comes and stands over you both, and looks down at the top of your Nubbin’s head.
    - Then it bends and tidies a tuft of fur there with its beak, very carefully, as if it has been bothering it.

#### Done `s24-bird-done`

_Then something calls from higher up, a creaky two-note call. The bird turns its head, and is gone into the cloud._  
**Tilly** _(think)_: He’s got somewhere to be. You can tell.  
_Nova is standing very still, with her mouth open._  
**Nova** _(shock)_: That’s a— no. That can’t be. That’s out of a picture paper.  

### Step 7: Follow the bird’s tracks up to the camp in the cloud.

Hint: Three-toed tracks, very far apart. Your Nubbin will find the next one. · check: `visit:s24-fairweather-camp`

#### Scene `s24-tracks`

_The tracks are three-toed, enormous and very far apart. Your Nubbin runs from one to the next and sits in each to show you._  
**Tilly** _(happy)_: We’re tracking him. That’s a tracking badge. I’m getting two badges today.  
_Higher up, the tracks cross a lot of other tracks: boots, very old ones, going round and round in the same circles._  
_Then a tarpaulin in the cloud, roped to the rock. A washing line, a row of flags, and a great many notebooks under a tin lid._  
_And a very old woman with a telescope, a hat with a feather in it, and a voice like a door that wants oiling._  
**Augusta Fairweather** _(angry)_: Stand still. Stand STILL. You’ll frighten it off, and then you’ll say it was never here. They always do.  

#### Done `s24-camp`

**Augusta Fairweather** _(think)_: Augusta Fairweather. You won’t have heard of me. Nobody has, for fifty years.  
_Nova makes a noise like a kettle just before it gets going._  
**Nova** _(shock)_: THE Fairweather? From the picture papers? Hazel had your picture on her wall when she was a girl. She told me.  
**Augusta Fairweather**: Did she. Then she was one of four thousand children who believed me, before they were told not to.  
_Fairweather’s eye falls on Tilly’s sash, on the old tin badge, and stays there._  
**Augusta Fairweather** _(shock)_: That’s one of mine. The Young Explorers. Where did you get that?  
**Tilly**: Gran Hazel. It was hers when she was little. She gave it me. It’s my oldest one.  
**Augusta Fairweather** _(soft)_: …Of course it was. I heard somebody shout my old motto up at the falls today. I thought I’d dreamed it.  
**Nova** _(soft)_: She wrote her list because of you. All of it. Every place.  

### Step 8: Stay at the camp till dusk.

Hint: Wait by the tarpaulin as the light goes. Fairweather says lanterns frighten the bird, so keep Hazel’s unlit. · check: `watch:s24-ridge:dusk`

#### Scene `s24-cage`

**Augusta Fairweather** _(angry)_: I came down off this ridge with one striped feather and a story. The papers called me a liar in letters this high.  
**Augusta Fairweather** _(sad)_: So I came back up to prove it. I’ve been proving it ever since.  
_Behind the tarpaulin is a cage of bent ash and wire, beautifully made, and big enough for a bird taller than Nova._  
**Augusta Fairweather** _(think)_: For the bird. Your girl’s name for it is better than mine. I’ve called it Proof for fifty years.  
**Augusta Fairweather**: One night in that, down in the valley square where everyone can see. Then I can stop.  
**Nova** _(soft)_: Stop what?  
**Augusta Fairweather** _(angry)_: Being the liar on the ridge. Being up here. Being—  
_She doesn’t finish. She folds a net over her arm, carefully, the way you fold a shirt somebody ironed for you._  
**Tilly** _(angry)_: You can’t put Sir Reginald in a CAGE. That’s the worst rule I ever heard.  
**Augusta Fairweather** _(sad)_: I didn’t make the rule, child. The papers did.  
**Augusta Fairweather**: It comes down to drink at the spring above the camp at dusk. It always has. Tonight I shall be ready.  

**Choices**

- **Tell her the bird doesn’t owe anyone proof.**
    - **Augusta Fairweather**: Nor did I. They wanted it anyway.
- **Say nothing. Sit down near the cage.**
    - You sit down near the cage. Fairweather watches you do it, and something in her face goes tired.

#### Done `s24-net`

_Dusk. The bird comes down to the spring, all stripes and stilts, and bends to drink._  
_The net goes over it with a sound like a sail filling. Fairweather is faster than anyone that old has a right to be._  
_The bird bolts up the ridge into the dark, dragging net and old woman both. Tilly is after them before anyone can catch her collar._  
_Nova lunges. Her pack swings, slides on the wet rock, and goes towards the drop._  
_She grabs the pack. She grabs it with both arms._  
_When she looks up, Tilly is gone into the dark._  

### Step 9: Light Hazel’s lantern and give it to Nova.

Hint: Sit with her first. Then light the lantern and put it in her hands. · check: `give:s24-nova:hazels-lantern`

#### Scene `s24-rock`

_Nova sits down on the rock with the pack in her arms, and doesn’t move._  
_Your Nubbin is off up the ridge into the dark before you can call it. A moment later it comes back, calm, and sits at your feet._  
_Then, from high up the ridge, a whistle: two fingers, rising at the end. Hazel’s supper call._  
**Tilly** _(angry)_: I’M FINE. I’M WITH SIR REGINALD. NOBODY COME, YOU’LL SCARE HIM.  
**Nova** _(shock)_: She’s all right. She’s all right.  
**Nova** _(sad)_: I grabbed the pack. She was right there, and I grabbed the pack.  
_It is too dark for the ridge path now, and too steep to hurry on. The whistle comes again, cross and steady._  
_You sit down next to Nova, and you don’t say anything. You wait._  

#### Done `s24-light`

_You light Hazel’s lantern. It comes up small and gold, and the rock and the pack and Nova’s face come out of the dark._  
_You put it in her hands, and she holds it a long time. Then she sets the pack down, so she can hold it properly._  
**Nova** _(soft)_: I’ll read her list. I always read her list when it’s bad. It’s the only thing that works.  
_She opens the atlas from the wrong end. The back pages fall open in the lantern light._  

### Step 10: Stay with Nova. Let it take as long as it takes.

Hint: Don’t hurry her, and don’t move. There is no wrong amount of time. · check: `wait:s24-nova:as-long-as-it-takes`

#### Scene `s24-back-pages`

_The back pages are in pencil too, in an older hand than the list. It doesn’t press so hard now._  
**Nova** _(think)_: These aren’t mine. I never… I never turned to the back. Why would you turn to the back?  
**Nova**: “Nova came. Twelve, all knees. Gave her the book. She’ll never bring it back. Good.”  
**Nova**: “Nova came. Tea (refused).”  
_She laughs. It comes out wrong._  
**Nova** _(soft)_: “Nova came. Brought a shell. Tea (refused).” “Nova came. Took the shell back. Tea (refused).”  
**Nova**: She left my pack by her door while I walked the valley. She must have taken this out and written in it. Every time.  
_Pages of it: rain, frost, Fern’s trees, the weather, and who came to the gate, and who didn’t._  
**Nova** _(soft)_: “Sun. Moss sat on my foot all afternoon. Got nothing done. Best day this year.”  
**Nova**: “Nova came. Taller. Ticked off a sea. Tea (refused). Don’t mind. She’ll stop for tea when she’s ready.”  
**Nova** _(sad)_: “Nova came. I nearly told her. Didn’t. She’d have stayed, and hated it, and so would I.”  
_Nova can’t read the last one out. She turns the book round and holds the lantern so you can see._  
_It is the last thing written there, on her last visit before Hazel went to the Hollow._  
**Gran Hazel** _(soft)_: “Thanks for the adventure – now go have a new one!”  
_Nova cries. All of it, at once, with her whole face, the lantern shaking in her hands._  

#### Done `s24-crying`

_Your Nubbin climbs into her lap, on top of the book, and leans its whole weight against her._  
_You keep still. Up the ridge, every so often, the supper whistle comes, cross and steady, and nobody hurries anything._  
_It takes as long as it takes. Then a little longer._  
**Nova** _(soft)_: “Tea (refused).” Every time. She wrote it down every time.  

### Step 11: Help Nova build a cairn of Hazel’s things.

Hint: Flat stones at the bottom. Nova will hand you each thing. Take your time. · check: `build:s24-cairn`

#### Scene `s24-cairn`

_When she has finished, Nova wipes her face on her sleeve, both sides, thoroughly, like someone cleaning a window._  
**Nova** _(soft)_: She didn’t want me carrying her about. She wanted me to go and get rained on somewhere new.  
_She opens the pack and empties it onto the rock, carefully, one thing at a time._  
_A tin cup, a scarf a sleeve too long, a shell, and bundles of postcards in string, in two hands, going both ways._  
**Nova**: I’m leaving them here. Up here, where she never got to. On the way to the Shelf.  
_You build it together by lantern light, flat stones first. Every thing she puts in, she puts in properly._  
**Nova** _(think)_: She’d say I was being dramatic.  
**You**: She’d be right.  
**Nova** _(happy)_: She would. She’d be insufferable about it.  
_With the cairn nearly done, Nova stops, with the last two things in her lap: the tin cup and the scarf._  

**Choices**

- **“Keep the tin cup.”** — sets `{"s24-cairn":"cup"}`
    - **Nova**: For tea. Which I’ll have, from now on. Whenever it’s offered.
- **“Keep the scarf.”** — sets `{"s24-cairn":"scarf"}`
    - **Nova**: It’s too long. It was always too long. She said I’d grow into it. I didn’t.
    - She puts it round her neck anyway, twice, and there is still some left over.
- **“Leave them both. Keep the book.”** — sets `{"s24-cairn":"book"}`
    - **Nova**: Just the book. Yes. It’s the only one with her in it, talking.

#### Done `s24-cairn-done`

_The pack is empty. Nova picks it up with one finger, and it swings, and she laughs at it._  
**Nova**: Oh. Oh, that’s much better.  
_Up the ridge the whistle comes again. This time it sounds fed up._  
**Nova** _(happy)_: Right. Let’s go and fetch our stowaway.  

### Step 12: Follow Tilly’s whistle up the ridge by lantern light.

Hint: Walk towards the whistle. Keep the lantern low so it doesn’t dazzle anything. · check: `find:s24-tilly:nest`

#### Scene `s24-whistle`

_You go on up towards the whistle with the lantern held low, so it lights the path and not the sky._  
_Your Nubbin runs ahead into the dark, and back, and ahead, as if it is pulling you along on a string._  
_Nova’s empty pack slaps on her back. Every few steps she turns to check the cairn is still there, a pale heap in the dark._  
**Nova** _(think)_: It’s fine. It’s just stones. I know it’s just stones.  
_The whistle again, much closer, and very fed up._  
**Tilly** _(angry)_: I CAN SEE YOUR LANTERN. YOU’RE GOING THE LONG WAY.  

#### Done `s24-nest-fed`

_Under an overhang there is a nest the size of a cart wheel, made of heather and string and most of somebody’s washing line._  
_In it are four striped chicks like knitted tea cosies. Beside it sits Tilly, perfectly safe and extremely cross._  
_Her badge sash is wound round the smallest chick, badges and all, so it doesn’t get cold._  
**Tilly** _(angry)_: You took AGES. I’ve been whistling for about a hundred years.  
**Tilly**: Sir Reginald’s a mother. That’s allowed. She can still be Sir.  
_Fairweather’s cage stands open and empty at the edge of the overhang. Out in the dark, a creaky two-note call._  
_You remember the flat hand. You lay a trail of buns from the dark to the nest, one by one, and step back._  
_After a long while a striped head comes out of the dark, eating its way along your trail, bun by bun, all the way home._  
_She stands over her chicks and looks at you. You are fairly sure she remembers your hand._  
**Nova** _(soft)_: There. She came straight home.  

### Step 13: Free Fairweather’s boot from her net, one loop at a time.

Hint: Work from the outside in. Your Nubbin will sit on the net’s end so it can’t slide. · check: `puzzle:s24-net:untangle`

#### Scene `s24-edge`

_At the edge of the overhang, where the rock stops and the dark begins, somebody says, quite calmly, “I wonder if anyone might help.”_  
_Augusta Fairweather is sitting at the very edge with one boot wound up in her own net. The net is caught on a spike of rock._  
**Augusta Fairweather** _(think)_: Don’t hurry. Hurrying is how one gets into nets.  
**Augusta Fairweather**: I went after it in the dark. Fifty years of patience, thrown away in one evening. Rather a waste.  
_You and Nova kneel on either side of her, and set the lantern down where it lights the knots._  
**Nova** _(soft)_: One loop at a time. Like her scarf, when it went wrong.  

#### Done `s24-proof`

_Nobody hurries, and nobody falls. Your Nubbin sits on the end of the net so it can’t slide, and the last loop comes free._  
_Fairweather tries to stand, and can’t, and sits back against the rock. She is very tired, and very old._  
**Augusta Fairweather**: I shall just sit, I think. For a moment.  
_So she sits, and waits, and so do you all. The mother bird steps out from the nest and walks past her, close enough to touch._  
_Fairweather’s hand comes up off her knee and stays in the air a moment. Then it goes back down._  
**Augusta Fairweather** _(soft)_: That’ll do.  
**Augusta Fairweather** _(soft)_: That’s proof enough for me.  
_She takes an old striped feather out of her hatband, the one the papers laughed at, and puts it in your hand._  
**Augusta Fairweather** _(happy)_: I shan’t need it now. Go on. Go and see your sunrise. I’ve seen eighteen thousand. I’ll mind the children.  

### Step 14: Set Hazel’s lantern, lit, on the Sunrise Shelf as the sun comes up.

Hint: Put it on the lip of the rock, facing the Grove. Then wait for the sun. · check: `place:s24-hazels-lantern:sunrise-shelf`

#### Scene `s24-shelf`

_The Sunrise Shelf is ten minutes on from the nest: a flat lip of rock on the far ridge, with nothing in front of it but air._  
_You get there in the last of the dark. The valley below is one big blue shadow with nothing in it you can name._  
**Tilly** _(think)_: Is this it? It’s just dark. I was promised a whole valley.  
**Nova** _(soft)_: Wait. That’s the whole trick of it. You wait.  
_Nova gives you back Hazel’s lantern._  
**Nova** _(soft)_: You do it. She’d want it to be you. She’d pretend she wanted it to be anybody.  
_The sky over the far rim is going thin and pale, the way milk goes when you pour it into tea._  

#### Done `s24-lights`

_The sun comes over the far rim and runs down into the valley one place at a time, as if it is calling a register._  
_The Tooth, then the Circuit’s square roofs, then the falls, smoking white; the Hollow, where the paths stop; the tide pools, flashing._  
_Then the Grove: the pond, the cottage, the hill. All of it at once, small and bright, exactly as she wrote it._  
**Tilly** _(shock)_: Oh.  
**Nova** _(soft)_: There. The whole valley. At once.  
_Down in the Grove, four small lights are still burning, one at each post, as if somebody left them on for you._  
_You lift Hazel’s lantern as high as it will go. Nova puts her hand under your elbow, to help hold it up._  

### Step 15: Write the first line on the atlas’s last page.

Hint: Choose what to write, then write it with Hazel’s pencil stub. · check: `craft:s24-atlas:last-page`

#### Scene `s24-tick`

_Nova sits on the lip of the Shelf with the atlas open at the front, and a stub of pencil worn almost to nothing._  
**Nova** _(soft)_: This was hers. The one she wrote the list with. She gave it me with the book. I’ve never once used it.  
_She ticks the last line. It is a very small tick, and it takes her a long time._  
**Nova** _(happy)_: Take someone. Done. I took two, and a stowaway.  
_She pours tea from a flask into Hazel’s tin cup, drinks half, and hands you the rest._  
**Nova** _(soft)_: Tea. Accepted.  
_Then she ties the pencil stub to a bit of string, and hangs it round Tilly’s neck._  
**Nova**: Helping Someone Sad. You earned it. You were very annoying about it, but you earned it.  
**Tilly** _(soft)_: …It’s the best badge I’ve got.  
_Tilly holds it in her fist. With the other hand she checks the top of her welly, the way she always does, and leaves the note where it is._  
_Nova turns the atlas to its very last page, which is blank, and holds it out to you._  
**Nova** _(soft)_: Your turn.  

**Choices**

- **Write: “The Sunrise Shelf, again. Take someone.”** — sets `{"s24-page":"shelf"}`
    - **Nova**: Good. Somebody has to keep it in use.
- **Write: “Wherever Nova goes next. Tea after.”** — sets `{"s24-page":"tea"}`
    - **Nova**: Then you’ll have to come and find me. I’ll leave the kettle on. Probably.
- **Write: “Here. Every morning.”** — sets `{"s24-page":"here"}`
    - **Nova**: That’s the hardest one on any list. She managed it forty years.

#### Done `s24-written`

_You write it in pencil, with the stub, which Tilly lends you on the strict understanding that it is hers._  
_Then she borrows it straight back, and letters a badge for you on a bit of cereal box: HELPING SOMEONE SAD (ADVANCED)._  
**Tilly**: You were all right. For a grown-up.  
_Under your line on the last page there is room for a great many more._  

#### Complete `s24-home`

_You come down off the ridge in the afternoon, slowly, because Augusta Fairweather is coming too, on Nova’s arm._  
**Augusta Fairweather** _(think)_: Fifty years. I don’t suppose anybody has kept my room.  
_She takes the empty cottage at the foot of the falls path, for a week, she says. Nobody believes her, kindly._  
_That night you light Hazel’s lantern by the cottage table and open the atlas, to read her list again._  
_In the margins there is writing, in faint pencil. It wasn’t there before, or it was, and it needed this light._  
_It is on every page. Beside a boy and a dragon in a hollow: “I’d have fed the dragon.” Beside a building of endless counters: “Ha. Typical.”_  
_She read every story Nova ever brought her, and had something to say about all of them. Of course she did._  
_Nova stays three days, and has tea every afternoon. On the fourth she goes over the far ridge with a pack you could lift with a finger._  
_At the top she turns and waves, and doesn’t wait to see whether you wave back. She knows._  

**Rewards**

- Hazel’s margin notes (feature): Faint pencil notes in Hazel’s hand, one on every page of Nova’s atlas, written on Nova’s visits years ago. Tap one to read it.
- The atlas’s last page (feature): A blank last page in the atlas, for your own list. It starts with the line you wrote on the Sunrise Shelf.
- The Sunrise Shelf (feature): A viewpoint on the far ridge where you can see the whole valley at once. Best at dawn.
- Tilly’s pencil-stub badge (cosmetic): A circle of cereal box, lettered with Hazel’s pencil stub: HELPING SOMEONE SAD (ADVANCED). Worn on the coat.
- Ridge cairn (decor): A small cairn of flat ridge stones, like the one Nova built on the way to the Shelf. Nubbins like to sit on top of it.
- Striped-feather hat (cosmetic): A hat with Augusta Fairweather’s old striped feather in the band: the one the papers laughed at, fifty years ago.

Sets on completion: `{"s24Done":true}`

**Learning Grove swaps**

- `s24-falls-top#3` → The whole world’s up here! The WHOLE WORLD!
- `s24-back-pages#13` → “Thank you for every bit of it. Now off you go, and find somewhere new!”

**Notes:** THEME. Hazel’s list was the adventure she meant to have; the back of the book was the one she had. Nova learns that carrying Hazel’s things is not the same as carrying Hazel, and sets them down. QUESTION: what do we keep of someone, and what do we leave on the ridge? (s24-cairn; all three answers are right.) SIGNATURE CHOICE: s24-bird (feed or wait, step 6) pays off at the nest (step 12, six steps later): the done scene is s24-nest-fed or s24-nest-waited. OUTLINE MAP. Outline Q1–Q4 are steps 1–4; Q5 (the heavy pack) and Q6 (beside the falls) are merged into step 5; Q7–Q9 are steps 6–8; Q10 (the rest of the book) is split into steps 9 (the lantern) and 10 (the reading and the wait); Q11–Q14 are steps 11–14; step 15 (the last page) is the end of Q14. TIMING. The usual convention: a step’s scene plays when the previous check is met; its done plays when its own check is met. The whole story is one journey (morning, a day’s walk, a night, a dawn); once step 4 is met, steps 5–15 run without a break and the player cannot leave the ridge until step 14 is met (a back-out returns to the last step’s start, never fails). Steps 1–4 can be spread over days. CHECKS. sit:s24-hill:hazels-flower (sit beside Nova). inspect:s24-atlas:first-pages (open the atlas item at the front). collect:s24-hazels-lantern (take Hazel’s lantern decor off its hook; it is carried, and goes back on the hook at s24-home, lit). find:s24-tilly:carrier-cart (lift the tarp). climb:s24-falls-scree (a short scree climb: step on the stones your Nubbin sits on). choose:s24-tall-bird:feed-or-wait (met by either choice). visit:s24-fairweather-camp (follow the tracks). watch:s24-ridge:dusk (wait for dusk; a short sky fade). give:s24-nova:hazels-lantern (light it, then give it). wait:s24-nova:as-long-as-it-takes is a hold-still beat: no input for about 30 seconds of ambient sound (any tap resets it gently; no timer shown, no fail, no music). build:s24-cairn (place five flat stones and four things; any order works). find:s24-tilly:nest (walk towards the whistle). puzzle:s24-net:untangle (unpick four loops, outside in; cannot fail). place:s24-hazels-lantern:sunrise-shelf (set it on the lip, then a sunrise sky fade). craft:s24-atlas:last-page (write the chosen line). VARIANTS (all in SIDE_STORY_S24_VARIANTS). Scene and Choice have no condition fields, so every branch is its own scene. Replacements play instead of the named scene; inserts play in addition, at the named point. (1) STEP 1 SCENE, by walk. s24-tea is for walk = you, corvin or unset. walk = moss and keptStone false: s24-tea-moss (Moss came down in the spring “decided”, end-epi-walk-moss, and rides your shoulder for the whole story; staging only after this, with one insert at the Shelf). walk = moss and keptStone true: s24-tea-moss-stayed (his flower beside Hazel’s, as in end-epi-walk-moss-stayed; Nova sits between them). walk = sable: s24-tea-sable (Sable walks two steps ahead of you the whole way, waiting at every bend: staging, plus one insert at the Shelf). (1a) INSERT after the step 1 scene: s24-moss-kept, when keptStone is true and walk is not moss (Moss sits on the hill with his back to the Grove, as in c11-moss-kept and end-collector-kept). (2) STEP 3 SCENE: s24-lantern by default; with walk = corvin, s24-lantern-corvin (corvinGate = family, so it is Sunday and he is by the pond; the lantern hangs by the cottage door, as in end-epi-walk-corvin; he trims the wick: “Bring it back lit.”). The done scene s24-lantern-done is shared. (3) STEP 4 SCENE: s24-stowaway when s14Done is false (Sparks introduces Tilly, apologising); with s14Done, s24-stowaway-knows (Tilly knows you from Keeps & Critters). The done scene s24-badges is shared. (4) INSERTS between s24-badges (step 4 done) and s24-pack (step 5 scene), by the ending id: moss-sleeps → s24-bun-moss (Nova leaves a lantern bun on Moss’s flat stone by his flower); eternal-dawn → s24-eternal-watch (the Eternal watches the cart go). At most one plays. (5) STEP 6 SCENE, by the ending id: s24-bird for every ending but collector; collector with keptStone false → s24-bird-collector; collector with keptStone true → s24-bird-collector-kept (Tilly asks why your hands are cold). In both collector versions the bird will not come near you, and the payoff falls to Nova. Choice ids and the s24-bird flag are the same in all three. (6) STEP 12 DONE: s24-nest-fed (s24-bird = fed) or s24-nest-waited (waited); with the collector ending, s24-nest-fed-collector or s24-nest-waited-collector (Nova lays the trail or sits; you stand back). (7) STEP 14 DONE, by hazel and keptStone: s24-lights (hazel secret or unset: only the four posts answer); s24-lights-told (hazel = told: the valley has climbed Hazel’s hill with lanterns); with keptStone, s24-lights-kept and s24-lights-told-kept (the Long-Grass post only flickers). (7a) INSERTS straight after the step 14 done: walk = moss with keptStone false → s24-shelf-moss; walk = sable → s24-shelf-sable. (8) STEP 15 SCENE, by s24-cairn: s24-tick (cup), s24-tick-scarf, s24-tick-book. Same choices in all three. (9) COMPLETE: s24-home by default; with the ending eternal-dawn, s24-home-eternal (the Eternal is on the step). (9a) INSERT straight after the complete scene: walk = corvin → s24-home-corvin (the lantern comes back lit). (10) LATER: with s18Done, s24-lecture plays the first time the player visits the museum after s24Done (Fairweather’s lecture; Professor Delve hangs the plaque). Without s18Done, Fairweather is simply a resident. KEPT-STONE NUBBIN (line variants). With keptStone, the Grove’s Nubbins are stilled (end-collector-kept), so your Nubbin walks, it does not run. Replace these lines, in every scene that has them: “Your Nubbin runs to her, then back to you, then to her again, to check you are both still there.” → “Your Nubbin goes and sits by her, grey and quiet, and doesn’t move again until you do.” (every s24-tea* scene). s24-pack: “Your Nubbin is already halfway up the first stretch of scree, looking back to see why you are all so slow.” → “Your Nubbin waits at the foot of the scree, quite still, until you start. Then it climbs at your heel.” s24-falls-top: “Your Nubbin finds the dry ones. It runs up, waits on each, runs back to you, and goes up again.” → “Your Nubbin finds the dry ones, slowly, and waits on each until you catch up.” s24-tracks: “runs from one to the next” → “walks from one to the next”. s24-rock: “Your Nubbin is off up the ridge into the dark before you can call it. A moment later it comes back, calm, and sits at your feet.” → “Your Nubbin goes a little way up the ridge into the dark, slowly, and comes back calm, and sits at your feet.” s24-whistle: “Your Nubbin runs ahead into the dark, and back, and ahead, as if it is pulling you along on a string.” → “Your Nubbin walks ahead into the dark, slowly, and waits for the lantern at every turn.” WILD GROVE (letThemGo). No line here says the gate sticks; Hazel’s back pages mention “who came to the gate” in the past, before it came off its hinges, so no variant is needed. HAZEL’S MARGIN NOTES (Tier 2, data for the Atlas). From s24-home on (s24Done), every Atlas page shows one margin note in Hazel’s faint pencil beside the named quest’s scene. The Atlas reads s24Done read-only and never writes. In the fiction they were always there, written while Nova walked the valley, and Hazel’s lantern is what showed them; in play they simply show once s24Done is set (no need to light anything). Two notes are not S24’s and show earlier, once chapter 12’s finalChoice is done: A11’s last page (beside Galleons Lap) and A14’s Argos page (with Corvin’s “I came back. C.” under it when corvinGate = family). S24 leaves those two as they are and adds no second note to A11 or A14. The other twenty-nine, one per page (A18, A20 and A24–A31 are as their own outlines give them): A1 The Brothers’ Bargain, Q20 Equivalent Exchange: “He gave up the thing he was best at to bring his brother home. Cheap at the price. H.” A2 The Monkey and the Monk, Q5 Under the Mountain: “Five hundred years under a mountain and still cheeky. I’ve had Nubbins like that. H.” A3 Forty-Nine Days, Q12 The Dream: “His mother forgave him before he asked. They mostly do. That’s the hard part. H.” A4 The Waterfall Prince, Q2 The Climb: “Fell off that waterfall forty times and went up again. Slow hands. Well. Slow-ish. H.” A5 The Lion of Mali, Q4 The Iron Rod: “Seven years on the floor while they laughed. Then he stood up and bent iron. Never hurry a slow one. H.” A6 Seven Voyages, Q1 Two Sinbads: “Seven voyages, and he still came home to tell it to a porter. Somebody has to stay put and listen. I volunteer. H.” A7 The Marigold Bridge, Q12 Remember: “Somebody sang to Mamá Coco and she came back for a minute. Sing to your old ones. They’re still in there. H.” A8 The Twelve Tasks, Q9 The Place That Sends You Mad: “Ha. Typical. Try asking the parish council for a gate hinge. H.” A9 The Comet, Q11 The Cave: “She packed everything important into the cave. I’d have packed the kettle and the lot of you. H.” A10 The Heart of the Island, Q5 Setting Sail: “The grandmother goes first and tells her to go. That’s the right way round. Go on, then. H.” A12 The Mirror Shard, Q16 Gerda’s Tears: “Warm him up first. Tell him off after. In that order. H.” A13 The Wooden Boy, Q19 The Terrible Dog-fish: “Two years inside a fish, and he kept a light going for the boy. That’s a father. H.” A15 The Humbug, Q14 The Humbug: “A humbug, but he gave them what they had already. Most of us manage less. H.” A16 The Fox and the Rose, Q13 The Fox: “Sit a little nearer every day and say nothing. The fox and I would have got on. H.” A17 The Camphor Tree, Q10 The Seeds: “Plant it and wait. Nothing comes up. Wait anyway. That’s most of gardening and all of the rest. H.” A18 The Doll in the Pocket, beside the cellar scene: “Feed them first. Somebody’s mother said it before I did. H.” A19 The Dragon Who Fell, Q7 The Knife: “I’d have fed the dragon. Oh. He did. Good lad. H.” A20 The Weeping, beside the cave: “Everything wept but one. Don’t hate her for it. Feel sorry for her. H.” A21 The Flower Field, Q2 The Funeral: “Fifty years with him, and she wished she’d looked properly. Look at them properly. H.” A22 The Candle, Q14 The River: “Abuela sat down by the river and stopped being right. Took her long enough. Took me longer. H.” A23 Remember Who You Are, Q14 Look Harder: “He saw his father in the pool. They stay, you know. In the face. Usually the chin. H.” A24 The Law of the Jungle, beside Kotick: “Safe beach first. Then a well one. He found both. H.” A25 The Nothing, where its outline places it: “Never went. Read it twice. You lose a bit of yourself with every wish. I’d have printed that on the cover.” A26 The Wall of Uruk, beside the snake: “Lost the plant. Kept the wall. Good trade. H.” A27 The White-Haired Prince, beside the nest: “White hair at birth. I’d have kept him. H.” A28 The Box of Stories, where its outline places it: “Nova says here they tell you it isn’t true before they start. Best manners I ever heard of.” A29 The Honmoon, where its outline places it: “Never been. Nova says it’s all singing. I’d hum.” A30 The Word on the Brow, where its outline places it: “Clay, a word, and a man who forgot one Friday. I’d have forgotten every Friday.” A31 The Hero Twins, where its outline places it: “Two boys who beat Death by being cheeky to it. My kind of boys.” The two in s24-home (“I’d have fed the dragon.”, “Ha. Typical.”) are A19’s and A8’s, so they must match. Notes on teen pages (A1, A3, A4) show only where those pages can be opened. THE LAST PAGE. A new last page in the atlas item, blank but for the line chosen in step 15 (s24-page: shelf → “The Sunrise Shelf, again. Take someone.”; tea → “Wherever Nova goes next. Tea after.”; here → “Here. Every morning.”), in pencil. SIM. (a) Post-game seasons: the trigger needs the first spring after chapter 12; if post-game time has no seasons, offer S24 from the first post-game morning. (b) The ending id is read from the campaign state (CampaignState.ending, set when the ending plays). (c) The atlas item gains the margin-note layer and the last page (above). (d) The Sunrise Shelf is a new viewpoint section on the far ridge, reached by the falls path. (e) Augusta Fairweather becomes a resident NPC in the cottage at the foot of the falls path, with idle lines about the ridge; never about the bird’s nest’s whereabouts. (f) Ambient only, no reward: from the next spring on, the tall striped bird brings her chicks to the Grove pond for a few spring days (seen, never caught or fed by command). (g) If s10Done is false, the player may not own Hazel’s lantern decor (S10’s reward); stage the lantern on its hook anyway for this story, and leave it there afterwards as staging only. (h) The tall bird and her chicks are wild: no Nubbin or critter interactions, no capture, never a pet. STAGING. Hazel’s lantern is the one warm light from step 9 to step 14; the ridge is blue-grey. Tilly’s whistle is S14’s two-finger supper call SFX; it repeats about every 20 seconds from s24-rock until the nest. The cairn stays visible behind you on the climb to the nest. At the Shelf, the sunrise reaches places in the order the narration names them. Moss on your shoulder (walk = moss) dozes; Sable (walk = sable) keeps two steps ahead and waits at bends. REWARDS at s24-home: the margin notes and the last page (atlas features), the Sunrise Shelf viewpoint, the pencil-stub badge (coat cosmetic), the ridge cairn decoration and the striped-feather hat. Each is given once. LEARNING GROVE. Yes: both bands, to be written in the edu side-story files (map-reading and finding east for sunrise; why we don’t cage wild birds, and how nesting works; keeping a memory book for someone; old people were young once, with lists of their own). The two quoted lines use childLines here.

## S25. Real

*The Velveteen Rabbit, or How Toys Become Real (Margery Williams, 1922; public domain; Project Gutenberg #11757): the toy, the Boy, the Skin Horse and the nursery magic, retold in the valley* · audience: all · where: Hazel’s cottage: the loft, the window seat, the lawn and the long grass, and the shed · when: From chapter 3 (after c3-open) to chapter 8. Step 3 waits for c4-open, step 10 for c6-close, step 13 for c8-nova. Closes when chapter 9 opens.

**Cast:** Clank (Claptrap, the CL4P-TP unit (Borderlands), reference only)

### Step 1: Open the parcel in Hazel’s hand under the loft eaves.

Hint: It’s on the trunk under the little window. Pull the right end of the string; there is only one right end. · check: `inspect:s25-parcel`

#### Scene `s25-loft`

_Early-summer rain, steady and warm. The loft lamp has died, and Sparks says it’s the small, fiddly kind of broken._  
_Small and fiddly is Pixel’s department. Pixel is coming after lunch._  
_You go up the ladder first, to clear a space, and because you were never once allowed up there._  
_The loft smells of apples, dust and mothballs. Rain ticks on the slates a hand’s breadth above your head._  
_Hazel’s things are up here, in the order of someone who knew exactly where everything was and told nobody._  
_Seed trays, a broken umbrella labelled STILL GOOD, and forty years of Lantern Night programmes tied up with string._  
_Your Nubbin climbs up behind you, sneezes at the dust, and sets off to investigate a hatbox._  
_Under the eaves, on a trunk, is a parcel in brown paper, soft and gone pale where the sun from the little window has reached it for years._  
_On the front, in her hand: “For when they come back. (They’ll have grown. It won’t mind.)”_  
_The writing doesn’t lean. She wrote this a long time ago, before her words began to walk uphill._  
_The string is tied in her bow: the one that falls open if you pull the right end, and never if you pull the wrong one._  

#### Done `s25-name`

_Inside, in tissue: a knitted Nubbin, lumpy, in grey-brown wool. The bobble on top is a little crooked._  
_One button eye is sewn on tight. The other hangs on a long loose thread, as if it were looking at something far off._  
_Under it is a thin book with a rabbit on the cover, its spine mended twice with brown tape._  
_You remember, though not all at once. The smell comes first, wool and peppermint, then her kitchen, and being three, and holding it by the bobble._  
_She made it for you, and you took it everywhere for one summer. Then you went home and grew, and it stayed._  
_You called it something. You’re nearly sure you did._  

**Choices**

- **“Button.” For the eye.** — sets `{"s25-name":"button"}`
    - Button. You said it all that summer, mostly to the eye that was loose.
    - Your Nubbin looks at the toy’s loose eye, then checks its own two.
- **“Bobble.” For the bobble.** — sets `{"s25-name":"bobble"}`
    - Bobble. You carried it by the bobble everywhere, which is why the bobble is crooked.
    - Your Nubbin looks at the bobble, then pats the top of its own head, doubtfully.
- **“Mister.” Just Mister.** — sets `{"s25-name":"mister"}`
    - Mister. Hazel called the postman Mister, and at three you thought it was the most respectful word there was.
    - Your Nubbin stands up straighter, in case it is being inspected.

### Step 2: Help Pixel get the loft lamp working.

Hint: Hold the lamp steady while Pixel winds. Pass her the small cog, not the shiny one; the shiny one is hers. · check: `craft:s25-loft-lamp`

#### Scene `s25-lamp`

_After lunch Pixel comes up the ladder with a toolbox in her teeth and a biscuit tin under each arm._  
**Pixel**: Afternoon. Don’t touch the tins. Nothing in them is dangerous. Some of it is just enthusiastic.  
**Pixel** _(think)_: The lamp’s fine. It’s the winder. It’s lost the will to wind. I’ve brought it some friends.  
_Out of the tins come her bits: a clockwork moth, a brass fan the size of a thumbnail, a cog that turns itself when it’s warm._  
_Sparks and Fern come up the ladder too, because three people in a loft is an event._  
**Sparks** _(happy)_: Ooh. Does any of it explode?  
**Pixel**: Not on purpose.  
_The moth whirrs up off Pixel’s palm, flies one wobbly circle round the rafters, and lands on Fern’s head._  
**Fern** _(happy)_: Oh. Oh, it’s beautiful. I can’t keep a moth alive and you’ve gone and made one.  
_Your Nubbin chases the flashes off the brass fan across the floorboards, squeaking at every one._  
_On the trunk, where you put it down to make room, the knitted toy watches all of it with its one good eye._  

#### Done `s25-sill`

_The lamp comes on: a warm ring of light, and a small, satisfied tick every second, like something counting sheep._  
_Everyone goes down the ladder talking about the moth. You go last, with the toy and the book, and set them on the window seat for now._  
_For now turns into a week, and then a fortnight. The toy sits on the sill and gets the afternoon sun._  
_Nobody looks at it. You don’t either, though you mean to._  
_Out of the window, on top of the western hill, the little grey one is looking this way._  

### Step 3: Stay on the window seat until Moss has finished.

Hint: Don’t get up, and don’t tap anything else. He’s slow, and he’s nearly done. · check: `sit:s25-window-seat:moss`

#### Scene `s25-real`

_One morning the top of the western hill is empty._  
_Moss is on the outside ledge of the window seat, in the sun, by the open window. The toy is on the inside sill._  
_It is a long way down from the hill for him. He must have set out before it was light._  
**Moss**: Don’t mention it. I’m not here.  
_You sit on the window seat, because that is the rule, and you wait. A bee comes in, considers them both, and leaves._  
**Moss** _(happy)_: She knitted that in a week, with her feet on the fender. She dropped a stitch every night and said words I won’t repeat.  
**Moss** _(soft)_: You were three. You carried it by the bobble and you wouldn’t let anyone else hold it. Not even her.  
**Moss**: She read you that book on this seat, every night of one wet August. I sat on this ledge. I heard every word.  
**Moss**: The horse in it talks a great deal of sense, for a horse.  
_He turns, not to you, but to the toy._  
**Moss** _(soft)_: Real isn’t how you are made. It’s a thing that happens to you.  
**Moss** _(soft)_: When someone loves you for a long, long time, not just to play with, but REALLY loves you, then you become Real.  
_The toy, being knitted, takes this well._  

**Choices**

- **Ask him: does it hurt?** — sets `{"s25-ask":"hurt"}`
    - **You**: Does it hurt?
    - Moss doesn’t answer you. He answers the toy.
- **Ask him: does it happen all at once?** — sets `{"s25-ask":"when"}`
    - **You**: Does it happen all at once?
    - Moss doesn’t answer you. He answers the toy.
