- **Say nothing. Sit with them both.** — sets `{"s25-ask":"sit"}`
    - You say nothing. After a while Moss nods, very slightly, and goes on as if somebody had asked.

#### Done `s25-hurt`

**Moss** _(soft)_: Sometimes. When you are Real you don’t mind being hurt.  
**Moss**: It doesn’t happen all at once. You become. It takes a long time.  
**Moss** _(think)_: That’s why it doesn’t happen often to people who break easily, or have sharp edges, or who have to be carefully kept.  
_Then he climbs down off the ledge, one careful foot at a time, and sets off back to the hill. It will take him until supper._  
**Moss**: He has a good face, your toy. Don’t let him be carefully kept.  

### Step 4: Give your Nubbin something to hold.

Hint: Take the knitted toy off the window seat and put it in the basket. · check: `give:s25-toy:nubbin`

#### Scene `s25-storm`

_Later that week, Sable comes past the window seat on her way to nowhere in particular._  
_She stops. She looks up at the toy on the sill for a long time, with her ears flat._  
_Sharp edges, Moss said. She has them._  
_Then she lies down on the path under the window, a tail’s length from it, and goes to sleep. She doesn’t take it._  
_In the morning there is frost on the path where she lay, and none at all on the toy._  
_Then, at the end of a hot week, a storm. It comes over the hill at bedtime all at once, like a dropped tray._  
_Your Nubbin won’t settle. It goes from the basket to the hearth to the door and back, ears flat at every bang._  
_You look for the old sock it likes, and can’t find it. You warm a stone at the hearth, and it pushes the stone away._  
_Thunder, right overhead. The window seat lights up white, and the knitted toy on its sill with it._  

#### Done `s25-sleeps`

_You put the toy in the basket. Your Nubbin looks at it, then climbs in and curls all the way round it, nose to tail._  
_Thunder. It doesn’t even twitch._  
_In the morning it is still curled round the toy, which is damp at one ear where it was held all night._  
_From then on, the toy is your Nubbin’s._  

### Step 5: Find the toy before it gets too dark. Your Nubbin won’t sleep without it.

Hint: Take a lantern. Your Nubbin had it on the lawn by the pond after supper, so start there. · check: `find:s25-toy:lawn`

#### Scene `s25-everywhere`

_From then on the toy goes everywhere. It is carried by one ear, or the bobble, or not at all when it’s been dropped._  
_It goes to the pond, and comes back wet to the knees, if a toy has knees._  
_It goes to Sparks’s picnic and comes home with jam on it, like everybody else._  
_It has its bath when your Nubbin has its bath. It has views about cats, according to your Nubbin._  
_Then one dusk, at bedtime, the basket has your Nubbin in it, standing up, and nothing else._  
_It looks at you, and at the door. It does not lie down._  
_The toy is out in the Grove somewhere. It’s getting dark, and the dew is coming down._  

#### Done `s25-lantern`

_You find it face down in the grass by the pond, soaked with dew, its loose eye trailing._  
_Your Nubbin is still standing up in the basket when you get back, as if it never once doubted you._  
_It takes the toy in both arms, lies down, and is asleep before you’ve blown out the lantern._  

### Step 6: Go back to Fern’s for tea, with the toy, so she can apologise properly.

Hint: Let your Nubbin carry him in. It will insist. · check: `visit:fern:s25-apology`

#### Scene `s25-fern`

_You take your Nubbin round to Fern’s for tea, and the toy comes too. The toy always comes too._  
_Your Nubbin climbs onto Fern’s good chair and sets the toy upright beside it, as if it were company._  
**Fern**: Oh, not on the cushion, love. It’s only a toy. It can go on the floor with the shoes.  
_She reaches for it. Your Nubbin makes a sound you have never heard it make: a squeak so fierce the cups rattle._  
_Fern takes her hand back. Your Nubbin stands in front of the toy, very small and very large, and looks at you._  
**You**: He isn’t a toy. He’s REAL!  
_There is a short silence. In it, Fern looks at the toy properly for the first time._  
**Fern** _(think)_: …Right. Yes. I see. Then I owe him an apology. Possibly several.  
**Fern**: And I’m going to do it properly, because Hazel would have, and because your Nubbin is looking at me like that.  
**Fern**: Come round for tea, and bring him. I’m getting the good cups out.  

#### Done `s25-apology`

_Fern has the good cups out, a cushion on the good chair, and a very small cup on the cushion._  
**Fern**: Sir. I said you were only a toy. That was wrong, and rude, and in front of your friend. I apologise unreservedly.  
**Fern**: I am, as everybody knows, only a neighbour. You are clearly a great deal more than that.  
_Your Nubbin considers this. Then it sets him on the cushion, and accepts a biscuit on his behalf._  
**Fern** _(happy)_: Hazel made me do this once. With a cat. I was in the wrong that time too.  

### Step 7: Let the wild Nubbins have a look at the toy.

Hint: Stand back by Moss and keep still. Wild ones won’t come near a Keeper who’s moving. · check: `watch:s25-wild-nubbins:long-grass`

#### Scene `s25-grass`

_A hot, still afternoon. Your Nubbin carries the toy down to the edge of the long grass, where the wild ones live, to show him off._  
_Moss has come as far as the flat stone at the foot of the hill. He looks as though he’s been there for a century._  
**Moss**: I have not. I came down this morning. It only feels like a century, at my speed.  
_Your Nubbin sets the toy down in the grass, upright, facing outwards, and steps back._  
**Moss**: Go on, then. Let them look. They’ll be rude. They’re young.  
_The grass moves, here and there, with no wind in it._  

#### Done `s25-wild`

_Three wild Nubbins come out of the grass. They sniff the toy, bounce away, come back, and sniff him again._  
**Moss** _(think)_: That one says he can’t hop. That one says he smells of indoors.  
**Moss**: That one wants to know what he’s for. It doesn’t mean it unkindly. It really wants to know.  
_They lose interest all at once, the way young ones do, and are gone. Your Nubbin picks the toy up and holds him tighter._  
**Moss** _(happy)_: They think Real is hopping. I thought so too, at their age. I gave up hopping a long time ago.  

### Step 8: Find the right page in Hazel’s book.

Hint: The book is on the window seat. The page you want has the old horse on it. · check: `find:s25-book:page`

#### Scene `s25-clank`

_A wet afternoon. Moss is on the window ledge again; he says the hill is too damp for his knees._  
_You were not aware he had knees. You decide not to raise it._  
_Something squeaks up the path, whistling, and stops under the window. It’s Clank, with a watering can, in the rain._  
**Clank** _(happy)_: Announcement: I am watering the rain! It is going very well!  
**Clank** _(happy)_: Announcement: what is THAT? On the sill. Is it a Nubbin? Is it a new best friend?  
**You**: It’s a toy. Hazel knitted him. He’s a toy, mostly. It’s complicated.  
**Clank** _(think)_: Knitted. Somebody made him. Like Pixel made me.  
_Clank rolls closer, until his chimney nearly touches the glass. His whistle goes very quiet._  
**Clank** _(soft)_: Is there a book? There’s a book. Is there a me in it?  

#### Done `s25-page`

_There is a page about the clockwork toys in the nursery, and an old horse who has watched them come and go. You read it to him._  
**You**: “…and he knew that they were only toys, and would never turn into anything else.”  
_Clank’s whistle drops, all the way down, like a kettle taken off the heat._  
**Moss**: The horse was wrong about that. I’m older than the horse.  
_You pass it on, word for word. Clank’s chimney-light comes up one notch._  
**Clank** _(soft)_: Is Real the word? The one I couldn’t find?  
**Moss**: No. It lives next door to it. Keep looking.  
_You pass that on too. Clank thinks about it for so long that the rain stops._  
**Clank** _(soft)_: Next door. Announcement: I am going to look next door.  

### Step 9: Hold the toy steady while Clover darns his elbow.

Hint: Your Nubbin won’t let go of him, so hold them both. Clover is quick; it only feels slow. · check: `craft:s25-darn`

#### Scene `s25-shabby`

_High summer goes by, carried by one ear. The toy goes grey at the seams, and his bobble is loved clean off._  
_Clover comes by with a basket of wool, sees him, and puts her hand over her mouth._  
**Clover** _(sad)_: Oh, look at him. Look at his poor seams. He’s gone right through at the elbow, if that’s an elbow.  
**Clover**: I could knit you a new one. Same pattern. I’ve got wool in the exact grey. I keep it for… well. I keep it.  
_Your Nubbin looks at the new wool. Then it sits down on the old toy, firmly, and looks at nothing._  
_Up on the window ledge, Moss makes a small dry sound that might be a laugh._  
**Moss** _(soft)_: …when you are Real shabbiness doesn’t matter.  
_Clover can’t hear him, and doesn’t need to. She looks at your Nubbin sitting on its toy, and puts the wool away._  
**Clover** _(soft)_: No. You’re right. Nobody’s getting a new one.  
**Clover**: But I’ll mend his elbow, if he’ll let me. In grey. So he doesn’t feel he has to match.  

#### Done `s25-darned`

_Clover darns his elbow in grey, in small neat stitches, with your Nubbin watching every one._  
_She knots it, bites the thread, and gives him back. Your Nubbin checks the elbow all over, and decides to allow it._  
**Clover** _(happy)_: There. Mended isn’t the same as new. It’s better. Hazel told me that over a teacup. I didn’t believe her either.  

### Step 10: Sit up with your Nubbin through the night.

Hint: Stay by the basket, and give it water when it wakes. Clover will look in. · check: `sit:s25-basket:night`

#### Scene `s25-chill`

_A few days after the rain on the hill, your Nubbin sneezes at breakfast. Then again._  
_It is warm to the touch and droopy, and it doesn’t want its plum._  
_You have your boots on to fetch somebody before you know you’re doing it. Clover is already at the gate._  
**Clover** _(soft)_: It’s a chill. Only a chill. I’m saying it first, so you don’t have to wonder.  
**Clover**: After a wet week they all get them. I’ve seen a hundred. Warm, quiet, water little and often, and somebody to sit up with it.  
**Clover** _(happy)_: It’ll be right as rain by morning. Well. Right as the day after rain.  
_Your Nubbin climbs into its basket with the toy, curls round him, and shuts its eyes._  

#### Done `s25-better`

_Clover looks in at midnight with a cup of something, and again at two, and at four, passing by each time, she says._  
_By morning your Nubbin is cool and hungry and cross about it. It eats two plums and half of yours._  
**Clover** _(happy)_: Told you. Right as the day after rain.  
**Clover**: Now. Everything it slept on gets boiled. Hazel’s rule, and her mother’s, and frankly mine.  

### Step 11: Carry the rag bundle out to the shed.

Hint: The rag bundle goes by the shed door for the rag cart, whatever you decided about the toy. · check: `carry:s25-bundle:shed`

#### Scene `s25-bundle`

_The copper in the wash-house is lit by ten. In go the blankets, the basket cushion, and the old sock, which turns up at last._  
_Then Clover holds her hand out for the toy, and stops._  
**Clover** _(sad)_: Oh. Oh, no. Not him. Old wool in a hot copper comes out the size of a walnut. Felted solid. I did it to a jumper once.  
**Clover**: What we can’t boil goes out with the rags, by the shed. The rag cart takes it. That’s how Hazel always did it.  
**Clover** _(think)_: Or. Cold water, by hand, a whole evening, and you never wring him. He’d come out smaller. But he’d come out.  
_Your Nubbin is better, and asleep in the sun on the step, with nothing in its arms for the first time since the storm._  
_The toy is in your hands. He is grey at the seams, darned at one elbow, and his loose eye turns slowly on its thread._  

**Choices**

- **Let him go with the bundle.** — sets `{"s25-toy":"gone"}`
    - You put him on top of the rag bundle, the right way up, with his good eye to the sky.
    - **Clover**: That’s a kind way to do it. Hazel always put the soft things on top, too.
- **Wash him yourself, by hand, in cold water.** — sets `{"s25-toy":"kept"}`
    - Clover fills the tin bath from the pump, sits on the step, and tells you what not to do, which is most things.
    - **Clover**: Squeeze. Don’t wring. Don’t rush him. You’ll want to rush him. Don’t.

#### Done `s25-shed`

_You carry the bundle out and set it by the shed door, the toy on top. The sun goes down behind the hill._  
_Your Nubbin, asleep on the step, doesn’t wake. You don’t wake it._  

### Step 12: See the night through, and go and look in the morning.

Hint: Stay at the window seat until it’s light. · check: `wait:s25-bundle:morning`

#### Scene `s25-dew`

_That night you can’t sleep. You sit on the window seat in the dark, where he used to sit, and look out at the shed._  
_The dew comes down. The bundle by the shed door goes silver at the edges._  
_A drop of it runs off his button eye, slowly, and falls into the wet grass._  
_Where it lands, something small and pale lifts out of the grass: a light, no bigger than a thimble._  
_It rises the way Moss says the first ones came up out of the spring: not quickly, as if it had been asked._  
_It goes low across the lawn, past the pond, and into the long grass, and is gone among the wild ones._  
_You don’t go out, and you don’t know why not. It seems like something you aren’t meant to follow._  

#### Done `s25-gap`

_In the morning there is a gap in the top of the rag bundle, the shape of a small lumpy Nubbin. Nothing else is disturbed._  
_In the wet grass by the shed is a button on a long loose thread. You put it in your pocket._  
_After breakfast your Nubbin goes to the edge of the long grass and sits there a while. Then it comes back and eats its plum._  
_It doesn’t look sad. It looks like somebody who has just waved somebody off._  
_Up on the hill, Moss is looking at the long grass. He doesn’t say anything all day._  

### Step 13: Tell Moss about the warm water behind the falls.

Hint: Sit on the window seat beside his ledge. He’ll listen. · check: `talk:moss:s25-wellspring`

#### Scene `s25-falls`

_You come home from the Hollow with the notebook inside your coat. It’s late, and the cottage smells of cold ashes._  
_Moss is on the window ledge. He doesn’t ask about the notebook, and you don’t tell him yet._  
_On the window seat, where the toy used to sit, there is a patch of faded cushion the shape of a small lumpy Nubbin._  
_There is one thing you do want to tell him._  
_You went along the ledge behind the falls today. You put your hand on the rock, and the rock was warm._  

#### Done `s25-twice`

**You**: There’s a pool behind the falls. Warm. Steaming a bit. Lit from underneath, by nothing.  
**Moss** _(soft)_: The Wellspring. Yes.  
**Moss** _(think)_: The first ones came up out of the warm water as small lights. Nobody ever told me where the lights came from.  
_He looks out across the dark lawn, past the pond, to the long grass._  
**Moss** _(soft)_: I’ve a guess.  
_He doesn’t say what it is. You think you know where he’s looking._  

#### Complete `s25-complete`

_He asks you to write it down, so you do: a new page in the journal, on the window seat, by the last of the lamplight._  
_At the top, in capitals: HOW TOYS BECOME REAL._  
_Underneath, what Moss said, as near as you can get it._  
**Moss** _(soft)_: Twice round, and loved the whole way, and settled in itself.  
**You**: What does that mean?  
**Moss**: It means what it says. I am old, and it is late, and the hill is a long way up.  
_He climbs down off the ledge and sets off into the dark. It will take him until morning, and he says he likes it._  
_The page says nothing more. You leave the next one blank, in case._  

**Rewards**

- Hazel’s knitted toy (item): A lumpy knitted Nubbin your Nubbin carries everywhere and sleeps curled round. Kept for good if you washed him by hand (s25-toy = kept).
- Velveteen coat colour (cosmetic): A rare soft grey-brown coat, faintly knitted-looking. In spring, either a Velveteen wild Nubbin sits at the edge of the long grass, or Fern and Clover knit the toy’s pattern into a coat for your Nubbin.
- Button-eye charm (cosmetic): One brown button on a thread, worn by your Nubbin: the toy’s loose eye, found in the grass by the shed, or Clover’s spare from her tin, so they match.
- “How Toys Become Real” journal page (feature): Moss’s words, in your hand. “Twice round, and loved the whole way, and settled in itself.” It says nothing more.

Sets on completion: `{"s25Done":true}`

**Learning Grove swaps**

- `s25-real#10` → Real isn’t in the stitches. It grows on you slowly, the way moss grows on a wall.
- `s25-real#11` → Somebody has to love you for ages and ages, properly, and not just for games. That’s how it starts.
- `s25-hurt#0` → Sometimes it does. But once you’re Real, a little hurt doesn’t frighten you so much.
- `s25-hurt#1` → It isn’t quick. It’s slow, like a tree getting tall. You hardly see it happening.
- `s25-hurt#2` → It takes longer for anyone with sharp edges, or anyone kept up on a high shelf where nobody can hug them.
- `s25-fern#5` → He’s not just a toy! He’s Real, to us!
- `s25-page#1` → “…and the clockwork toys were sure they would stay clockwork for ever, and never be anything more.”
- `s25-page-found#1` → “…and the clockwork toys were sure they would stay clockwork for ever, and never be anything more.”
- `s25-shabby#6` → Once you’re Real, the worn-out bits stop mattering. They’re just where the love went.

**Notes:** THEME. Real is not how you are made; it is what being loved for a long time does to you. It does not happen to things that have to be carefully kept (Moss, s25-hurt), which is the Grove’s “safe is not the same as well” in a nursery. The lore underneath is the Eternals: a Nubbin loved enough stops fading. S25 hints at that and nothing more. It never says the toy became a Nubbin, and it is never a route to eternalRaised. SIGNATURE CHOICES. (1) s25-name in step 1 (button | bobble | mister) pays off twelve steps later in the spring coda: the wild one turns its head at the name, or the name is cross-stitched inside the coat collar. (2) s25-toy in step 11 (gone | kept) decides step 11’s done, steps 12 and 13, the coda and the idle lines. Moss frames it in step 3 (“Don’t let him be carefully kept”) and answers it in step 12 (“It’s the better ending, mostly”). Neither branch is the sad one. RUNNING THREADS. The wild ones say he can’t hop (step 7); in the let-go coda the Velveteen wild one hops twice, to show you. The narration calls the toy “it” until you say “He isn’t a toy” at Fern’s (step 6), and “he” from then on. Clover darns in grey “so he doesn’t feel he has to match” (her c3-close blanket line), and in the kept coda the coat is knitted to match him. MOSS. Steps 1–2 can play in chapter 3, before Moss first speaks in c4-open, so there he is only “the little grey one” on the hill (s25-sill), never named and never speaking. If c4-open has already played when step 2’s done fires, play s25-sill-late (he is named, still silent). Step 3 waits for c4-open. Only you hear Moss: Clank, Byte, Fern and Clover never do, and you pass his words on. He walks to the cottage window ledge and back (slowly; the text says so). Give him no affection from this story beyond ordinary petting (his chapter 10 threshold is fixed). TIMING. Opens after c3-open (the loft lamp has died; step 1 starts the next time the player enters the cottage). Steps 1–2 from chapter 3. Step 3’s scene waits until c4-open has played, then plays the next morning the player is at the cottage. Steps 4–9 are summer, one per day or so, in order. Step 10’s scene waits until c6-close has played, then a few in-game days, so it never lands on the day of Fossil’s farewell. Steps 11–12 follow it directly (the next day and that night). Step 13’s scene waits until c8-nova has played (chapter 8’s walk home), then plays that night at the cottage. If chapter 9 opens with the story unfinished, it closes: no rewards, no coda, s25Done unset. The toy, if your Nubbin already has it, stays as scenery on its bed. YOUR NUBBIN. “Your Nubbin” is the Nubbin the toy is given to in step 4 (the youngest in the Grove at that moment, not Sable, not Moss). Store it as the toy’s holder. If it leaves the Grove before step 10, the holder becomes your next youngest; the text never names it. VARIANTS (all in SIDE_STORY_S25_VARIANTS). Step 2 done: s25-sill-late replaces s25-sill if c4-open has played. Step 4 scene: s25-storm-nosable replaces s25-storm if Sable’s trust is under 20 or Sable is not in the Grove (read only; this story never changes her trust). Step 8: with s21Done, s25-clank and its done s25-page play, and s25-page-found replaces s25-page if a13-clank-word is true (Atlas save). Without s21Done, s25-byte replaces s25-clank and s25-page-byte replaces s25-page. If S21 is still open (started, not done) and chapter 6 hasn’t opened, hold step 8’s scene until S21 closes or chapter 6 opens, then read s21Done. Step 8 also waits until c4-close has played (Byte, who may come instead of Clank, is first met at Lantern Night). Step 11 done: s25-shed is for gone; s25-shed-kept for kept. Step 12: s25-dew and s25-gap are for gone; s25-line and s25-line-dry replace them for kept. Step 13: s25-falls and s25-twice are for gone; s25-falls-kept and s25-twice-kept replace them for kept. SPRING CODA. In the first spring after the campaign’s ending (post-game, when the memorial flowers open), the first time the player is in the Grove by day, play s25-coda-wild-<s25-name> (gone) or s25-coda-coat-<s25-name> (kept). It plays whatever the ending, and Moss is not in it, so it works if he has faded. It needs s25Done. IDLE LINES (Tier 2). After s25Done, the first time the player taps Moss (while he is in the world) play s25-moss-kept or s25-moss-gone, once. If s21Done and s25Done, the first time the player taps Clank play s25-clank-next-door, once. CHECKS. inspect:s25-parcel (tap the parcel in the loft); craft:s25-loft-lamp (a hold-steady and pass-the-cog beat with Pixel); sit:s25-window-seat:moss (sit, no input, about 20 s); give:s25-toy:nubbin (carry the toy from the sill to the basket during the storm); find:s25-toy:lawn (a short lantern search at dusk; the toy is always by the pond); visit:fern:s25-apology (go to Fern’s with the toy); watch:s25-wild-nubbins:long-grass (stand still near Moss while three wild Nubbins approach); find:s25-book:page (turn the book’s pages to the horse); craft:s25-darn (hold steady while Clover darns); sit:s25-basket:night (sit by the basket; offer water twice; Clover looks in); carry:s25-bundle:shed (carry the rag bundle to the shed door); wait:s25-bundle:morning (stay at the window seat until dawn; in the kept branch the toy lies on a towel by the range); talk:moss:s25-wellspring (tap Moss on the ledge). No check can fail. STAGING. The window seat is downstairs in the front room, under a low window facing the lawn and the western hill; the toy sits on its inside sill, Moss on the outside ledge, with the window open between them. Storm (step 4): one scripted night of rain and thunder, cosmetic only. The chill (step 10) is narration only: no sim illness, no stat, mood, affection or lifespan change, no risk. The light in s25-dew is the same small pale light as the Wellspring art in c4-tale and c8-ledge, thimble-sized, rising slowly; play it with no music. REWARDS. The knitted toy becomes a carryable from step 4 (your Nubbin holds it; it sleeps curled round it); in the gone branch it leaves at s25-gap, in the kept branch it stays for good. The button-eye charm is given at s25-gap (the loose eye) or s25-line-dry (Clover’s spare). The journal page is given at s25-complete. The Velveteen coat colour arrives in the spring coda: a Velveteen wild Nubbin at the edge of the long grass (gone), or the knitted coat as a cosmetic for your Nubbin (kept). SIM: (1) A carryable toy item that a Nubbin holds and sleeps with (new item behaviour). (2) A new rare coat colour, “Velveteen”, available only through this story. (3) Gone branch: in the coda spring, spawn one wild Nubbin with the Velveteen coat that lingers at the edge of the long grass; it follows the ordinary stray rules and can be invited in like any stray. It is an ordinary child Nubbin: zero rebirths, no stat bonus, no Eternal flag, no special evolution. (4) Kept branch: a knitted Velveteen coat cosmetic for the toy’s holder. (5) Read a13-clank-word from the Atlas save: true once A13’s “Clank’s word” scene has played (which needs s21Done when A13 is played). If A13’s script names it differently, map it here. (6) Read Sable’s trust and presence at step 4 only. (7) A held-scene rule for step 8 while S21 is open (above). (8) No change to rebirth, evolution, alignment, the Eternal rule, Moss’s affection threshold or Sable’s trust. REWARDS ARE ONE-TIME. Nothing repeats daily or per visit. LEARNING GROVE. The outline wants a version (caring for things we love and mending them; how knitting works; why we wash bedding after someone is ill; love makes old things precious). The childLines above cover the lifted lines; the edu versions are a separate job.

## S26. The Walled Garden

*The Secret Garden (Frances Hodgson Burnett, 1911; public domain): the locked garden, the robin and the key, “a bit of earth”, and things coming alive slowly. Adapted so the child is never cured; the garden changes to fit her.* · audience: all · where: The Old Wall lantern post on the north side of the Grove; then Old Tobin’s cottage, and the walled garden behind the ivy · when: Opens in chapter 6, after c6-close (Fossil’s farewell). Steps 1–8 from then; step 9 from chapter 7; step 10 by the end of chapter 8. Pauses in chapters 9–10. Steps 11–13 in chapter 11, after c11-open. Closes when chapter 12 begins.

**Cast:** Linnet (Mary Lennox and Colin Craven, The Secret Garden (Frances Hodgson Burnett, 1911)), Old Tobin (The Secret Garden’s household that keeps Colin indoors for his own good (theme only); the Grove’s own Old Tobin from S13), Linnet’s mum, The Thrums

### Step 1: Find out what the robin on the old wall post wants from you.

Hint: It wants feeding, obviously. Hold out a crumb by the old wall post and keep still. · check: `feed:s26-robin`

#### Scene `s26-robin`

_Late summer. The rain has stopped at last, and Fossil’s flower is a few days old on the hill._  
_Dusty hasn’t been down from the cliff since._  
_A robin has taken the old wall lantern post. It sits on the top like a lid._  
_After that, wherever you go, it goes: the pond, the woodpile, the cottage step. It never comes closer than a spade’s length._  
_It looks at you the way a landlord looks at a tenant._  
**Fern** _(think)_: It’s decided you owe it something. Robins do that. One followed me for a whole spring over a raisin.  
**Fern**: I never found out which raisin.  
_Your Nubbin sits down under the post and stares up at it. The robin stares back, and neither of them blinks first._  

#### Done `s26-robin-fed`

_You hold out a crumb of one of Fern’s lantern buns, which have never once come out. The robin considers it, and you, and the crumb again._  
_Then it takes it off your palm, quick as a pinch, and goes back to the post._  
_It hops from the post onto the ivy, and looks at you over its shoulder._  
_Then it sets off down the lane, and your Nubbin follows it. You follow your Nubbin._  

### Step 2: Say hello to the girl at Old Tobin’s gate.

Hint: Tobin’s cottage is at the bottom of the lane, by the polished lamp post. Let her set the pace. · check: `talk:s26-linnet`

#### Scene `s26-linnet`

_The robin leads you down the lane to a cottage with a lamp post outside it, polished like a medal._  
_An old man in a lamplighter’s coat is getting a wheelchair over the doorstep. The girl in it is telling him how, very loudly._  
**Linnet** _(angry)_: No. Back wheels first. Grandad. Back. Wheels. First.  
**Old Tobin** _(think)_: I know which wheels, love. I’ve been doing wheels since before…  
_He gets it over the step. She takes the rims herself the moment it lands, and spins round to look at you._  
**Old Tobin** _(happy)_: Hazel’s grandchild! You’ve her chin. I’m Tobin. I lit the lamps in this valley for fifty-one years.  
**Old Tobin**: This is Linnet. My granddaughter. Here till winter, while her mum works the coast boats.  
**Linnet** _(angry)_: He means I’m kept here till winter.  
**Old Tobin** _(soft)_: She’s eleven. She’s very… she’s eleven.  
_Linnet looks up the lane, and down it, and at the hill behind, and at the hill behind that._  
**Linnet**: Nothing in this valley is flat. Did you know that? Nothing. Somebody should be told.  
**Old Tobin** _(soft)_: Mind yourself on the lane, love.  
**Linnet**: There. That. That’s the other thing everybody says.  

**Choices**

- **Agree. Whoever built this valley did it badly.** — sets `{"s26-hello":"agree"}`
    - **Linnet**: Thank you. Finally. Somebody with eyes.
    - It is very nearly a smile. She puts it away quickly, before anyone can use it against her.
- **Ask if she’d like a push up the lane.** — sets `{"s26-hello":"asked"}`
    - **Linnet**: No.
    - **You**: All right.
    - She looks at you, surprised that “no” was allowed to be the end of it.
    - **Linnet**: …You asked, though. Nobody asks. They just grab the handles.
- **Say nothing. Wait and see which way she goes.** — sets `{"s26-hello":"walk"}`
    - She goes up the lane, hard, the rims slapping her palms. You walk beside her at her pace, which is faster than yours.
    - **Linnet**: You’re not going to tell me to mind myself?
    - **You**: Do you need telling?
    - **Linnet**: No.

#### Done `s26-linnet-robin`

_The robin lands on the lamp post, right above Linnet’s head._  
**Linnet**: That’s following you. Is it yours?  
**You**: I think I’m its.  
**Linnet**: Where does it live?  
**You**: On the old wall, at the top of the Grove. In the ivy, mostly.  
**Linnet** _(think)_: Is the lane flat? To the old wall?  
_You think about it. It is, almost: the only lane in the valley that is._  
**Linnet**: Then show me. Tomorrow. Before Grandad thinks of a reason.  

### Step 3: Follow the robin along the old wall with Linnet.

Hint: Watch where the robin scratches. Leave the ground round the lantern post alone; you know why. · check: `find:s26-door`

#### Scene `s26-key`

_Next morning Linnet is waiting at the top of the lane: early, cross, and slightly out of breath. Tobin is not with her._  
**Linnet**: He thinks I’m reading. I am. I’m reading the valley.  
_The old wall runs along the north side of the Grove, tumbled and deep in ivy. The robin goes ahead, post to bush to stone._  
_Near the lantern post it drops onto a fresh molehill and scratches, as if it has lost something and is cross about it._  
_Something in the earth goes clink._  
**Linnet**: Dig. Go on. It’s pointing.  
_You kneel, and lift out an iron key, black with age, its bow as wide as your palm._  
_It has been in the ground a very long time. The robin watches you hold it, as if you’d better not drop it._  
_The lantern post beside you is warm under your hand, the way the three good posts always are._  
_You know from Moss’s tale what sleeps under this one. You put the earth back with your hands, and press it flat._  
**Linnet**: What? What was that face?  
**You**: Nothing. Something old. It’s asleep.  
**Linnet** _(think)_: Everything here is old and asleep.  

#### Done `s26-door`

_Then the wind comes along the wall, and lifts the ivy like a curtain._  
_Under it, exactly at the height of Linnet’s eyes, is a round iron knob._  
**Linnet** _(happy)_: There. There! You lot stand too tall to see anything.  
_You hold back the ivy and find the edge of a door, and under the knob, a keyhole._  
_The key goes in as if it has been waiting. It takes both your hands to turn it, and then Linnet’s as well._  

### Step 4: Fetch Dusty to the hidden garden. They’ll know if anything in it is alive.

Hint: Dusty is up on their cliff. Bring them along the old wall to the ivy door. · check: `escort:dusty:s26-garden`

#### Scene `s26-inside`

_The door opens inwards, slowly, scraping a fan in a hundred years of leaves._  
_Three stone steps lead down from the doorway into a garden walled on all four sides._  
_Everything in it is grey: grey tangles, grey canes, bare roses climbing the walls and falling off them again. In the middle, a stone bench._  
**Linnet** _(soft)_: It’s so quiet in here.  
**Linnet** _(soft)_: I bet nobody’s said a single word in here for a hundred years.  
_Nothing moves. Even the robin lands without a sound._  
**Linnet**: …Is it dead? All of it?  
**You**: I don’t know. I know someone who would.  
**Linnet** _(think)_: Then fetch them. I’ll wait here. I’m very good at the tops of things.  
_She folds her arms in the doorway at the top of the three steps, and guards a garden nobody has wanted for a hundred years._  

#### Done `s26-wick`

_Dusty comes with a spade over one shoulder, stops in the doorway, and doesn’t say anything for a while._  
**Dusty** _(soft)_: After Fossil, I said I’d go and dig something up. I went. I stood on the cliff with the spade for an hour.  
**Dusty**: First time it hasn’t worked.  
_They go down the steps, take a grey cane in one hand, and scratch it gently with a thumbnail. Under the grey it is green: wet, bright green._  
**Dusty** _(happy)_: Wick. Old valley word. It means alive, and meaning it.  
**Dusty**: This one’s wick. That one. Not that one, that’s firewood. But most of this lot has only been asleep.  
**Linnet**: Then wake it up.  
**Dusty** _(soft)_: …Yeah.  
**Dusty** _(soft)_: Yeah. I want to dig this. I didn’t think I’d want to dig anything for a bit.  

### Step 5: Decide, with Linnet, how she gets down the three steps.

Hint: Ask her first. It’s her garden as much as anyone’s. · check: `choose:s26-steps`

#### Scene `s26-steps`

_Dusty comes back up, earth to the elbow. Linnet is still at the top of the three steps._  
_Three steps: steep, worn in the middle, a little slippery with moss. Built by somebody who walked._  
**Linnet** _(angry)_: Well. That’s that, then. Somebody built three stupid steps and walked off.  
_Sparks comes along the wall with a coil of wire for the old wall lantern. She sees the open door, and stops._  
**Sparks** _(happy)_: Ooh. I didn’t know that was there. NOBODY knew that was there!  
**Sparks** _(happy)_: Want me to lift you down? Chair and all. I lift generators. You’re lighter than a generator.  
_Dusty looks at the steps, and then at the spade, measuring something._  
**Dusty**: Or we dig them out into a slope. You and me. A few evenings’ work.  
_Everyone looks at you. You look at Linnet._  
**You**: What do you want?  
**Linnet**: I don’t mind being lifted.  
_Her face minds. Her face minds very much._  

**Choices**

- **Dig the steps into a ramp. Dusty and you, three evenings.** — sets `{"s26-steps":"ramp"}`
    - **Linnet**: Three evenings. Of watching you two dig.
    - **Linnet**: …Fine. Somebody has to tell you where.
    - **Sparks**: Shout if you change your mind. I’m only ever a shout away. A loud shout.
    - Sparks leaves her coil of wire at the top, just in case. Linnet sits beside it and looks down at the garden she can’t get into yet.
- **Ask Sparks to lift Linnet and her chair down. Now.** — sets `{"s26-steps":"lift"}`
    - **Sparks**: Right! How do you want it? Tell me everything. I break things when I guess.
    - **Linnet**: Front and back, not the sides. Don’t tip me back. I’m not a wheelbarrow.
    - Sparks does exactly as she’s told, which nobody has ever seen before. Linnet’s wheels touch the garden path.
    - **Linnet**: Thank you.
    - She says it carefully, like a coin she has had to count out. Then she is off among the roses.

### Step 6: Wait in the garden at dusk and catch whoever has been pruning the roses.

Hint: Sit on the stone bench as the light goes, and keep still. Watch the top of the far wall. · check: `wait:s26-garden:dusk`

#### Scene `s26-pruned`

_The garden wakes slowly, the way a cat does: one leg at a time, and complaining._  
_Dusty clears the dead wood, and you cart it out. Your Nubbin and the robin supervise from the stone bench._  
_Then Linnet spots a rose by the far wall that has been cut back. Badly, but not long ago._  
**Linnet** _(think)_: Look. Somebody’s been in here. That cut’s from this summer. The wood’s still pale.  
**Dusty** _(think)_: She’s right. And whoever did it held the shears upside down.  
**Linnet**: So somebody’s got another key. Or somebody comes over the top.  
_All three of you look up at the top of the wall at the same moment, the way people do in plays._  
_Nobody is there. On the coping there is a snapped rose twig, and a flattened patch of moss._  
**Linnet**: Dusk, then. Nobody prunes in daylight unless they want to be caught.  

#### Done `s26-moss-wall`

_Dusk. A blackbird says its last word._  
_Then something small and green and very slow comes over the top of the far wall._  
_It has a rose twig in its mouth, held crossways, like a dog with a stick. It is Moss._  
**Moss** _(shock)_: …  
_He takes the twig out of his mouth with great dignity, as if he had meant to be seen with it._  
**Moss** _(think)_: Good evening. I was pruning.  
**Linnet** _(angry)_: You were doing it upside down.  
**Moss**: I have hands the size of buttons, child. With shears, everything is upside down.  
**Linnet** _(shock)_: …You talk.  
**Moss**: When there is something worth the wear.  
**Dusty** _(shock)_: Moss. How long have you been coming in here?  
**Moss** _(soft)_: Once a year. On this day, as it happens. For longer than any of you have been alive. Put together.  
**Linnet** _(angry)_: So I wasn’t the first to say anything in here.  
**Moss**: You were the first who expected an answer. I only talk to the roses. They never keep up their end.  

### Step 7: Help Linnet choose her bit of earth.

Hint: Walk the garden and stop at each corner so she can see it. She’ll know it when she sees it; your job is to agree. · check: `explore:s26-garden`

#### Scene `s26-earth-ask`

_Moss is on the wall again before anyone else, pretending he has always been there._  
_Linnet watches him for a long time. She is not used to wanting things out loud, and it shows._  
**Linnet** _(think)_: Moss. It’s your garden, isn’t it? Or it’s somebody’s, and you look after it.  
**Moss**: It was somebody’s. I cut the roses once a year. That isn’t the same as looking after.  
**Linnet** _(soft)_: Could I have some of it? The earth, I mean. Not much. Just a bit that’s mine.  
**Moss** _(think)_: A bit of earth. And what would you want with it?  
**Linnet** _(soft)_: Put things in it. Seeds. Make them grow. Watch them wake up, and know it was me.  
_Moss is quiet for so long that Dusty stops digging to look._  
**Moss** _(soft)_: Have all the earth you like. Nobody has wanted any of it for a very long time.  
**Moss** _(soft)_: You put me in mind of someone. She liked earth too, and anything that came up out of it.  
**Moss** _(soft)_: Go on, then. Find the bit you want, and have it. Then wake it up.  

#### Done `s26-earth-pick`

_You walk the garden for her twice, wall to wall, stopping at every corner so she can see it. She turns most of it down on sight._  
**Linnet** _(think)_: Too shady. Too stony. Too near the door; people would tread on it. What do you think?  

**Choices**

- **The corner by the south wall. The sun stays there longest.** — sets `{"s26-plot":"sun"}`
    - **Linnet**: Obviously. I was testing you.
    - She marks it out with four sticks and a length of string, perfectly straight.
- **Under the oldest rose, where Dusty found the green.** — sets `{"s26-plot":"rose"}`
    - **Linnet**: Next to the wick one. Yes. Then it’s got company.
    - She marks it out with four sticks and a length of string, perfectly straight.
- **Wherever you like. It’s your bit.** — sets `{"s26-plot":"hers"}`
    - **Linnet**: …Nobody’s ever said that. About anything.
    - She takes the sunny corner by the south wall, and marks it out with four sticks and a length of string, perfectly straight.

### Step 8: Sit on the stone bench with Moss and hear the garden’s story.

Hint: The bench in the middle of the garden. Sit down, and let him take his time. · check: `sit:s26-hush-bench`

#### Scene `s26-bench`

_That evening the light goes long and gold across the garden, and Moss comes down off the wall at last, the slow way._  
_He climbs onto the stone bench and sits, as if he has sat there before. He has._  
**Moss** _(soft)_: It was Hush’s. The bench, the roses, the wall. All of it.  
**Linnet**: Who’s Hush?  
**Moss** _(soft)_: A friend of mine. The quiet one. She kept the griefs nobody else had room for.  
_Linnet puts her brake on, and Dusty sits down on the path. You sit on the end of the bench, and wait._  

#### Done `s26-hush`

**Moss** _(sad)_: Briar wanted the stilled ones up on the hill, in the light, where the valley could see them. Hush said the wind would have them.  
**Moss**: So she carried them in here herself, one at a time, all that winter. She was not big. It took her till the thaw.  
**Moss**: Then she planted roses up every wall. Not for herself. A still thing can still be looked at, she said, and should have something back.  
**Moss** _(soft)_: The last of them went in the second winter, here, with the roses bare above it. She said bare was still something.  
**Moss** _(soft)_: You heard the rest at Lantern Night. The door, and the key. I will not tell that twice in one year.  
_Nobody says anything. The robin turns round twice on the back of the bench, and settles._  
**Linnet** _(soft)_: Not even Hazel knew? About in here?  
**Moss** _(soft)_: Hazel had enough doors.  
_Then Moss looks down at the patch of bare earth beside the bench. He looks at it for a long time, the way he looks at the hill._  
**Moss** _(soft)_: If I ever have a flower, I’d like a bit of it here.  
**Moss** _(think)_: Don’t go telling people. They’d make a speech.  
_Linnet sets a white pebble on the bare patch to mark it, and says nothing at all. For Linnet, that is a speech._  

### Step 9: Plant the autumn bulbs in Linnet’s raised beds.

Hint: The bulb sack is inside the door. Your Nubbin will carry some. Some. · check: `place:s26-bulbs:raised-beds`

#### Scene `s26-beds`

_Autumn: the valley goes gold, then brown. For the first time in a hundred years, the garden has people in it most days._  
_Dusty builds raised beds along the south wall: deep wooden boxes at exactly the height of Linnet’s hands._  
**Dusty** _(happy)_: Measured twice. Measured you, I mean. Your arms. Tell me if it’s wrong. It won’t be.  
**Linnet** _(soft)_: It’s not wrong.  
**Linnet**: Right. Dusty: compost. You: bulbs. The Nubbin: carry. The robin: look important. Moss: stop helping.  
**Moss**: I haven’t touched anything.  
**Linnet**: You were about to.  
_Linnet runs the garden like a ship. Nobody minds: she is good at it, and nearly always right._  
_Tobin thinks she spends her afternoons at the library van. The library van comes on Thursdays._  
**Linnet** _(think)_: What goes in my bed first? You choose. I’ll overrule you if it’s wrong.  

**Choices**

- **Snowdrops. They come up first, before anyone thinks it’s worth it.** — sets `{"s26-bulbs":"snowdrops"}`
    - **Linnet**: First. Yes. Before anybody’s ready for them.
- **Crocuses. Purple ones. Loud.** — sets `{"s26-bulbs":"crocus"}`
    - **Linnet**: Loud. Good. This garden’s been quiet long enough.
- **Some of everything, and see what wins.** — sets `{"s26-bulbs":"mix"}`
    - **Linnet**: A race. I’ll win, obviously. My bulbs will, I mean.

#### Done `s26-cheeks`

_Your Nubbin carries the bulbs very seriously, two at a time, one in each cheek._  
_It plants about half of them. The other half it hides all over the garden, in places it seems to choose by feel._  
**Linnet** _(think)_: Your Nubbin has no sense of order whatsoever.  
_She watches it bury one under the bench, pat it flat, and look round to see if anyone noticed._  
**Linnet** _(soft)_: …I like it.  

### Step 10: Bring Tobin into the garden instead of hiding it from him.

Hint: Don’t send him away. Get him onto Hush’s bench, and let the garden do the rest. · check: `escort:s26-tobin:hush-bench`

#### Scene `s26-tobin-finds`

_It ends on a Thursday, when the library van comes and Linnet isn’t at it._  
_Tobin finds the ivy door by following her wheel tracks. He comes through it the way you would come into a burning house._  
**Old Tobin** _(shock)_: Linnet! Away from that— off that— is that a SPADE?  
**Old Tobin** _(angry)_: She’ll catch her death out here. She’ll tip. That wall could come down on her!  
_Linnet goes sour all at once, like milk in thunder. The whole busy, bossy afternoon goes out of her face._  
**Linnet** _(angry)_: Everybody keeps me. Nobody lets me.  
**Linnet** _(sad)_: I’ve been kept in every room in this valley. This is the first place anyone’s let me do anything.  
**Old Tobin** _(sad)_: I only want you safe, love.  
_Up on the wall, Moss shifts, as if a word he knows very well has been said in front of him._  
_You could send Tobin home and hide the door again. Linnet looks at you as if she thinks you might._  

**Choices**

- **Ask Tobin to sit on Hush’s bench. Just for a minute.** — sets `{"s26-tobin":"asked"}`
    - **Old Tobin**: Sit? I… well. A minute. One.
    - He sits. The stone is warm from the afternoon, and he looks very surprised by that.
- **Tell Linnet to show him round. It’s her garden too.** — sets `{"s26-tobin":"shown"}`
    - **Linnet**: …Fine. Grandad. Come here. No, here. Look at this.
    - She shows him everything: the wick canes, the raised beds, the pebble by the bench. She is cross the whole way round, and very thorough.
    - At the end he sits down on Hush’s bench, because his legs have decided for him.

#### Done `s26-tobin-bench`

**Old Tobin** _(happy)_: This puts me in mind of a story. There was a winter, the year of the Three Hats, when…  
**Old Tobin** _(think)_: …when the snow came up to the lamp posts, and I went round lighting them from underneath, and…  
_He stops, and looks at the roses. Then he looks at Linnet, up to her elbows in a raised bed, and not tipping._  
**Old Tobin** _(soft)_: Where was I.  
_He doesn’t find his place. He sits on, and stops arguing, and watches her work until it’s dark._  
_On the way out, at the door, he puts his hand flat on the old wall._  
**Old Tobin** _(soft)_: My old gaffer used to say: put a rose where your worry sits, and the worry has to go and sit somewhere else.  
**Old Tobin**: He was a terrible gardener. He was right about that, though.  

### Step 11: Find Linnet in the walled garden.

Hint: Follow her wheel tracks in the snow, along the old wall to the ivy door. · check: `find:s26-linnet:snow`

#### Scene `s26-snow`

_The Long Night is over. The days after it are short and white, and the valley is snowed in to the hedge tops._  
_Tobin comes up the lane at a sort of run, in two scarves and the wrong boots._  
**Old Tobin** _(shock)_: She’s gone out. First light. The chair tracks go up the lane and I can’t keep up with them.  
**Old Tobin** _(sad)_: I know where. I know exactly where. It’s my knees, in this.  
**Old Tobin** _(soft)_: Will you go and find her? Not fetch her. Find her.  
_You notice the difference. So, from his face, does he._  

#### Done `s26-snowdrops`

_Her tracks go along the old wall, through the open door and straight down the ramp: two neat lines in the snow._  
_Linnet is by Hush’s bench in three blankets, with the robin on her knee like a hot coal._  
**Linnet** _(happy)_: You took your time. Look. LOOK.  
_Snowdrops. All round Hush’s bench, white heads bowed, as if they came up to listen._  
_Some are in Linnet’s bed, in a dead straight row. The rest are everywhere: under the bench, in the path, one right in the middle of the ramp._  
**Linnet** _(happy)_: Your Nubbin’s. It can’t count and it can’t plant, and they all came up anyway.  
**Linnet** _(soft)_: I came in on my own. Nearly in the dark. Nobody had to come and do anything.  
_She says it lightly, like a fact about the weather._  

### Step 12: Walk Linnet’s mum up to the walled garden.

Hint: Along the old wall to the ivy door. Don’t tell her anything on the way. Let her hear it. · check: `walk:s26-mum:old-wall`

#### Scene `s26-mum`

_A few days later, a woman comes up the valley road with a kitbag and a coat that smells of the sea._  
_She has Linnet’s chin exactly, and Linnet’s way of looking at a hill as if it owes her money._  
**Linnet’s mum** _(think)_: I’m Linnet’s mum. The boats are laid up for the winter. Dad says she’s never at home. Dad says she’s up here.  
**Linnet’s mum** _(shock)_: Dad also says she’s been gardening. My Linnet once posted a houseplant out of a window because it was looking at her.  
**Linnet’s mum** _(soft)_: Is she all right? Really?  
**You**: Come and see.  

#### Done `s26-race`

_Halfway along the old wall she stops. From behind the ivy comes a noise like a kettle boiling over._  
_Laughing. Linnet, and Dusty, and somebody shrieking “NOT the snowdrops”, which is also Linnet._  
**Linnet’s mum** _(soft)_: …That’s her.  
_She lifts the ivy. Linnet is just inside, at the top of the ramp, and when she sees her mum her face does something enormous._  
**Linnet** _(happy)_: Mum! Race you. To the bench. GO.  
_Her mum drops the kitbag and runs. Linnet goes down the ramp and along the path like a hare, the rims singing._  
_Linnet wins by a length. It would have been two if she hadn’t looked back to gloat._  
_Nobody says “brave”, or anything like it. Dusty has been warned._  
**Linnet** _(happy)_: It fits me now. The garden. It didn’t, and now it does.  

### Step 13: Decide what happens to the garden key.

Hint: There’s no wrong answer. The door stays open whichever you choose. · check: `choose:s26-key`

#### Scene `s26-key-choice`

_That evening, when the others have gone in for tea, there is only you and Linnet and the robin, and the key._  
_It hangs on a nail inside the door, where it has hung since the morning you dug it up._  
**Linnet** _(happy)_: Mum says we’ll come every summer. Grandad says every summer and Christmas. Grandad’s outnumbered.  
**Linnet**: So. The key. It was Hush’s, and then the ground’s, and then the robin’s, and then yours.  
**Linnet** _(think)_: Somebody has to decide. Not me. I’d only make it about me.  
_She holds it out on her open palm. It is black and heavy and older than anybody._  

**Choices**

- **Give it to Linnet. She asked for the earth first.** — sets `{"s26-key":"linnet"}`
    - **Linnet**: Me? I just said… oh, you’re impossible.
    - She closes her hand round it. Then she goes very quiet.
    - **Linnet**: Then I’m keeping it so nobody can ever lock it. That’s what it’s for now.
- **Hang it on the old wall post, for anyone who wants to come in.** — sets `{"s26-key":"post"}`
    - You hang it on the hook under the old wall lantern. The post is warm against your knuckles.
    - **Linnet**: Anyone? Even people who are rubbish at gardening?
    - **Linnet**: …Good. They can be taught.
- **Leave it in the door. It’s never going to be locked again.** — sets `{"s26-key":"door"}`
    - You put it in the keyhole, turn it once to open, and leave it there.
    - **Linnet**: Somebody will steal it.
    - **You**: Then they’ll have the key to a door that doesn’t lock.
    - **Linnet**: …Ha.

#### Complete `s26-complete`

_Winter sun comes over the old wall and lies along the beds. The garden is still mostly bare, but it is not grey._  
_Dusty is hanging the ivy on a rail over the door, so it swings like a curtain. The robin is supervising._  
_Linnet wears the key on a bootlace round her neck. It is far too big and bangs on her chest when she races, and she races anyway._  
**Dusty** _(happy)_: The stone from the old steps is still in a heap. I’m making you a bench out of it, like Hush’s. For the Grove.  
**Dusty** _(soft)_: And this. I wore it every day Fossil and I went digging. It’s yours now. You dig like someone who means it.  
_It is a gardener’s apron, earth worked into every seam. Linnet crowns you with snowdrops, and says you can’t take them off until tea._  
_Linnet’s mum sits on Hush’s bench with Tobin, and they argue about which of them she gets her chin from._  
**Linnet** _(happy)_: Me. I get it from me.  
_On the way out you look back: the bench, the beds, the white pebble by the bare patch, and the snowdrops in the path._  
_The robin has decided it lives with you now, in a general sort of way. The door stays open behind you._  

**Rewards**

- hushGarden (flag): Hush’s garden is open again. In chapter 12 Hazel stops at the old wall; in Moss Sleeps his flower’s seed goes over the wall; every ending gains a garden line.
- The Walled Garden (feature): A new section behind the Old Wall lantern: Hush’s roses, Linnet’s raised beds, the ramp and Hush’s bench. Plants grow with the seasons on their own; nothing needs watering. Linnet visits every summer.
- The robin (cosmetic): A robin petpet that follows your Keeper about at a spade’s length, as if you owe it something. It never says a word.
- Gardener’s apron (cosmetic): Dusty’s old digging apron, earth worked into every seam. Nubbins bring you things to plant when you wear it.
- Snowdrop crown (cosmetic): A ring of snowdrops Linnet made for you. It never wilts, and you’re still not allowed to take it off before tea.
- Hush’s bench (decor): A stone bench for your Grove, made by Dusty from the old garden steps. Grey Nubbins like to sit on it in the sun.

Sets on completion: `{"hushGarden":true,"s26Done":true}`

**Learning Grove swaps**

- `s26-inside#3` → Shh. Listen. Nothing’s making a sound. Not one thing.
- `s26-inside#4` → It’s like the whole garden’s holding its breath.
- `s26-wick#4` → Green under the grey. It’s alive. It’s only been asleep.
- `s26-earth-ask#4` → Moss. Please could one little corner be mine? Just to look after?
- `s26-earth-ask#6` → I want to plant things. And look after them. And see them come up.
- `s26-earth-ask#8` → Take any corner you like. This garden has been waiting for someone.
- `s26-earth-ask#9` → You remind me of my friend Hush. She loved digging, and watching things grow.
- `s26-earth-ask#10` → Pick your corner. Then help it grow.

**Notes:** THEME. Safe is not the same as well, told from the child’s side: Linnet is kept, not let. The garden Hush grew for the still ones becomes a garden for someone who will not sit still in it. Slow hands: a ramp is three evenings’ work. BURNETT, CHANGED ON PURPOSE. No cure by willpower. Linnet stays as she is; the garden is changed to fit her. TIMING. Opens after c6-close (late summer). Step scenes play when the previous check is met, as in the other side-story files. Steps 1–8 run from chapter 6; step 9 waits for chapter 7 (autumn); step 10 must be done by the end of chapter 8 (the journal hint in chapter 8 says the garden won’t wait for winter). If chapter 9 opens before step 10 is done, the story closes unfinished: no rewards, hushGarden unset. The story pauses through chapters 9–10 (no scenes, no journal nudges). Step 11’s scene plays on the first visit to the Grove after c11-open; steps 12 and 13 follow within the few days of chapter 11. If chapter 12 begins before step 13’s check, the story closes unfinished and hushGarden stays unset. SIGNATURE CHOICE. Step 5 (s26-steps: ramp | lift) is paid off in step 11, six outline quests later. PRELUDES (SIM: play a variant immediately before a step’s own scene). With s26-steps = ramp, play s26-ramp-1 before s26-pruned, s26-ramp-2 before s26-earth-ask and s26-ramp-3 before s26-bench. With lift, play s26-lift-1, s26-lift-2 and s26-lift-3 in the same places. These are the outline’s “three evening scenes”: story beats, never a repeating task. Steps 6 and 7 are written to work whether Linnet is at the top of the steps or down in the garden. By step 8 she is down on both routes. OTHER VARIANTS. If s12Done when step 3 completes, play s26-door-thrums in place of s26-door. If s13Done when step 10 completes, play s26-tobin-bench-cellar in place of s26-tobin-bench. With s26-steps = lift, play s26-snowdrops-lifted in place of s26-snowdrops (step 11 done). Complete: s26-complete for s26-key = linnet, s26-complete-post for post, s26-complete-door for door. CAMPAIGN HOOKS (hushGarden; all in SIDE_STORY_S26_VARIANTS). (1) c12-wall-garden: in c12-open, after line 8 (“Hazel walks the Grove. Slowly. She touches each lantern post…”) and before line 9 (“At the fourth post, the long grass…”), play c12-wall-garden, then resume c12-open at line 9. Narration only: Hazel never speaks in it. (2) end-moss-sleeps-garden: in the Moss Sleeps ending, straight after end-moss-sleeps and before the epilogue variants. (3) end-epi-garden: in every ending except The Collector, after the other epilogue variants (S2, S9, S10, S13, S19, and S23 if wired) and before end-<id>-last. In The Collector, end-epi-garden-cold plays there instead. MOSS. Steps 6–8 need him, and he is always in the world in chapters 6–8. Steps 11–13 and the complete scene never use him, so they hold on every route. The bare patch by Hush’s bench, marked with Linnet’s white pebble, stays bare in the garden art until end-moss-sleeps-garden (where his seed is promised to it). Moss returns to S1’s Hush memory in s26-hush from a new angle (how the still ones came in, and why the roses); he never tells a tale twice in the same words, and he points to Lantern Night for the door and the key instead of telling that again. It agrees with S1: the last still one faded in winter by this wall. Hush faded before Hazel came; Moss has come over the wall once a year, on the day she faded, and told nobody, Hazel included. TOBIN. This is always the player’s first meeting with Tobin (S13 opens in chapter 7), so he gets his one-line introduction in s26-linnet. LINNET (art and movement). Eleven; her own dark green wheelchair, which she propels herself, fast on the flat. Nobody pushes it or lifts her without asking. Sparks lifts “front and back, never the sides”, as Linnet directs. Animate her racing, spinning to face people, and working the raised beds from her chair. Brake on when she settles (s26-bench). Check every movement, the ramp gradient and the snow beat with wheelchair users. ART. Walled garden behind the ivy door on the north side: ivy that lifts in the wind (later hung on a rail), three worn stone steps that become a long, shallow ramp (ramp state persists), grey canes that green over the seasons, Hush’s stone bench in the middle, raised beds on the south wall, the white pebble, snow in chapter 11, snowdrops scattered where the Nubbin hid bulbs (one on the ramp). Robin: perches on posts, bench and Linnet’s knee; never speaks. Your Nubbin: bulging cheeks in step 9. SIM: new flags hushGarden (bool) and s26Done; the prelude rule above; ramp and snow art states; the walled garden section (seasonal growth, no watering, no timers); the robin petpet; Linnet as a summer visitor in the garden after the campaign (talk only, no chores); Tobin gains garden tales (added to his S13 tale pool if S13 is done, or told at his cottage gate if not). The c12 insertion point and the three ending scenes above.

## S27. Some Nubbin

*Charlotte’s Web (E. B. White, 1952): a spider who saves a small life with words written in her web, and dies in her season. Adapted as an original Grove story; two short approved quotes.* · audience: all · where: Corvin’s Circuit: the old lantern shed, the runners’ pens and the finishing straight (the spring scene is in the Grove) · when: Opens in chapter 5, on the first visit to the Circuit after c5-close (and c5-sold, if it plays). The Show waits for chapter 7; the egg sac for wisp or chapter 8. Closes before chapter 9 opens.

**Cast:** Spindle (Charlotte A. Cavatica (Charlotte’s Web, E. B. White); she writes and never talks), Hob (Lurvy, the hired man (Charlotte’s Web), loosely: Corvin’s stable lad), Mr Tull (An original character: the dealer from over the pass (the book’s threat, made a businessman)), Wisp (Templeton the rat (Charlotte’s Web), in this story only: the hoarder who carries the egg sac), Clank (Claptrap (Borderlands), reference only)

### Step 1: Fetch a coil of wire for Sparks from the Circuit’s old lantern shed.

Hint: The shed is behind the finishing straight. Bottom drawer of the cabinet under the window. · check: `find:s27-wire-coil`

#### Scene `s27-wire`

_High summer at the Circuit. The sand is hot enough to fry on, and the white railings are furred with frost._  
_Sparks is on her back under the start lamp, with a screwdriver in her teeth._  
**Sparks** _(angry)_: Start lamp’s gone again. I need wire. Copper, thin, a coil about so big.  
**Sparks**: There’s a cabinet of mine in the old lantern shed, behind the straight. Bottom drawer. Be a love.  
**Sparks** _(think)_: He doesn’t like people in there. He’s never been in there himself, mind. Not once in eleven years.  
**Sparks**: It’s the only place up here with dust in it. I love it. Don’t tell him.  
_Your Nubbin climbs into your coat pocket for the walk. It doesn’t like the cold rails either._  

#### Done `s27-evening`

_The lantern shed smells of paraffin and warm wood. Dust turns slowly in the light from one small window._  
_Under the window stands Sparks’s cabinet, every drawer labelled in her square capitals: FUSES, WICKS, SPARE, EVENING LAMPS._  
_You find the wire. When you stand up, there is a web across the corner of the window that wasn’t there a moment ago._  
_It isn’t a new web; you just hadn’t looked. And in the middle of it, very small, is a word._  
_EVENING._  
_A grey-brown spider no bigger than a fingernail sits beside it. She lifts two front legs at you, politely._  
_It is eleven in the morning. You find, to your own surprise, that you lift a hand back._  

### Step 2: Ask the stable lad about the little grey runner at the end of the row.

Hint: Hob does the buckets by the pens. He’s the only one up here who whistles. · check: `talk:s27-tam`

#### Scene `s27-row`

_Through the shed window, past the spider and her word, you can see the runners’ pens._  
_One pen to each runner, in a straight row, every rail white with frost. Every runner grey, and none of them moving._  
_At the very end of the row is the smallest. It is the only one facing the wrong way._  
_It isn’t looking at anything in particular. It is just looking out, down the valley, the way you might look at weather coming._  
_There is a number painted on its side in neat white paint. 9._  
_Outside, a lad with two buckets goes along the pens, whistling. He stops whistling at the end of the row._  

#### Done `s27-tam`

**Hob** _(think)_: That’s Nine. Smallest we’ve got. Last every time. Not slow, exactly. It just doesn’t see the point.  
**Hob**: I’m Hob. I do the buckets. I’m not meant to whistle. I forget.  
_He fills Nine’s trough, and Nine doesn’t look round. Hob stands there a moment longer than the trough needs._  
**Hob** _(sad)_: Autumn Show’s at the first frost that isn’t his. Anything that doesn’t place goes over the pass.  
**Hob**: There’s a dealer comes for them. Mr Tull. He’s all right, as dealers go. They don’t come back, is all.  
**Hob** _(sad)_: Nine’s never placed. Not once.  
_In the shed window behind you, the spider has gone very still in the middle of her web._  

### Step 3: Sit down by the shed window, and wait with the spider.

Hint: Sit on the upturned crate by the window and don’t do anything. It takes as long as it takes. · check: `sit:s27-shed-window`

#### Scene `s27-wait`

_The next time you come up to the Circuit, the first thing you do is go to the shed. You didn’t decide to._  
_EVENING has gone. In its place, in the middle of the web, is a new word._  
_WAIT._  
_The spider sits at the edge of the web with her legs drawn in. She isn’t looking at you; she is thinking._  
_You know that kind of thinking. You’ve watched someone in your family do it on a cottage step for hours._  
_There is an upturned crate under the window. It is exactly the right height for sitting next to someone._  

#### Done `s27-spindle`

_You sit, and the spider thinks, and the dust turns in the light. Your Nubbin falls asleep in your pocket._  
_After a long time, the door bangs open, and Sparks comes in to find out where her wire went._  
**Sparks** _(shock)_: There you are. I’ve been holding a lamp together with my— oh.  
_She looks at you, and the crate, and the web, and the word. She sits down on the floor without being asked._  
**Sparks** _(soft)_: She writes. Course she writes. She’s been living on my drawer labels for years. Look at her, the little clerk.  
**Sparks**: She wants a name. Anyone who can spell wants a name. Spindle. All legs and she winds things. Spindle.  
_The spider considers this. Then she lifts two legs, politely, once at Sparks and once at you._  
**Sparks** _(happy)_: That’s a yes. That’s definitely a yes. I’m going to cry and then I’m going to fix the start lamp.  

### Step 4: Ask Fern, Clover, Luna and Byte for one word each, and bring them back to Spindle.

Hint: Fern is in her orchard, Clover by the pond, Luna in her night garden and Byte wherever there is a clipboard. · check: `collect:s27-words:4`

#### Scene `s27-labels`

_Over the next few days Spindle practises. FUSES appears in the web, and then WICKS, and then SPARE._  
_They are very good, and the S’s in particular are beautiful. They are also no use to anybody._  
**Sparks**: That’s the trouble with learning off a cabinet. She can tell you where everything is, but not how anyone feels.  
_Spindle spins a short line, and then cuts it, and then sits looking at the gap where a word ought to go._  
_Then she looks through the window at the end of the row, and back at you, and lifts one leg. Just one._  
**Sparks** _(think)_: She wants words. Proper ones. And she can’t exactly go down the valley asking.  
**Sparks**: Ask round. Everyone’s got one word they’d save for something that mattered. Bring them up here.  

#### Done `s27-words`

_Fern is up a ladder in her orchard, losing an argument with a plum tree._  
**Fern**: A word? For a spider? Oh, I’ve got one. Somebody’s. As in, this one is somebody’s.  
**Fern** _(sad)_: When Bramble went, people said “it’s only a Nubbin.” She wasn’t only. She was mine. Somebody’s.  
_Clover listens with her head on one side, the way she listens to the shy ones._  
**Clover** _(soft)_: Warm. You can’t make a grey one not grey. But you can make it warm. Warm is allowed.  
_Luna is in her night garden at noon, asleep in a deckchair. She answers without opening her eyes._  
**Luna** _(soft)_: Home. Everything in my garden grows towards it in the dark. Even the moonflowers. Especially them.  
_Byte takes the question very seriously, and goes away, and comes back with a sheet of paper._  
**Byte** _(think)_: “Statistically above average.” It’s accurate. It’s kind. It’s the nicest thing I know how to say about anybody.  
_You read the four words to the shed window. Spindle listens to each, legs folded, exactly like a judge._  
_At Byte’s she goes very still for a long moment. Then she points one leg, delicately, at the length of the rail._  
**Sparks** _(happy)_: She’s saying it won’t fit. I’d tell him gently. He’s sensitive about his adjectives.  
_Spindle spins three short threads and pulls them tight, the way someone squares a pile of letters on a desk._  
_Then she does something she hasn’t done before. She points at you, and then at the end of the row._  
**Sparks** _(think)_: She’s asking what it’s called. Nine isn’t a name. Nine’s a number. You can’t save a number.  
_The little grey runner is still looking down the valley. It has never had a name to look up at._  

**Choices**

- **“Tansy.” Small, yellow, and very hard to get rid of.** — sets `{"s27-nine-name":"Tansy"}`
    - **Sparks**: Tansy. Grows in the cracks where nobody planted it. Oh, that’s good. That’s really good.
    - Spindle writes it once, small, in the corner of the window, to practise. She gets it right first time.
- **“Button.” Because it’s small, and it holds things together.** — sets `{"s27-nine-name":"Button"}`
    - **Sparks**: Button. You’re soft. I like that. Never let anyone at this Circuit find out.
    - Spindle writes it once, small, in the corner of the window, to practise. She gets it right first time.
- **Give it a name of your own.** — sets `{"s27-nine-name":""}`
    - You think about it properly, the way you’d name anything you meant to keep. Then you say it out loud.
    - **Sparks**: …Yeah. That’s its name. You can tell. It sounds like it was always waiting there.
    - Spindle writes it once, small, in the corner of the window, to practise. She gets it right first time.

### Step 5: Be at the pens at first light, to see what Spindle wrote in the night.

Hint: Come up the valley before dawn. Stand by the shed: the frost shows the silk only for the first hour. · check: `watch:s27-pen-railing:dawn`

#### Scene `s27-first-night`

_At dusk Spindle comes to the edge of the web and waits, quite plainly, to be carried._  
_You hold out a matchbox. She walks into it the way a duchess gets into a carriage._  
_You carry her across the yard in the blue light to the last pen in the row, and open the box on the rail._  
_The rail is so cold it smokes. Spindle steps out onto it without a moment’s fuss._  
_Inside the pen the little grey runner watches the spider. It is the first thing you have seen it watch._  
_You go home. It is the hardest part, it turns out: going home and leaving someone to work._  

#### Done `s27-first-word`

_First light. Your Nubbin keeps watch with you from your pocket, nose out, and your breath hangs in the air._  
_The Circuit’s summer frost has come down in the night as it always does. Every rail is white._  
_And on the rail of the last pen, every thread of Spindle’s web has caught the frost, and stands out white as chalk._  
_It isn’t a number. It is a name, in letters the height of your hand: its name._  
_Fern has come up the valley with you to see. Fern reads it out first, loudly, and then again, louder._  
**Fern** _(happy)_: That’s its NAME. Look. It’s got a NAME. Everybody, it’s got a—  
_Inside the pen, the little grey runner turns round. For the first time, it is facing the right way._  
_Corvin’s runners have numbers. This one has a name, and now everybody knows it._  

### Step 6: Corvin wants a word with you by the pens.

Hint: He’s at the end of the row, by the rail with the name on it. · check: `talk:corvin:s27-pens`

#### Scene `s27-odd-things`

_Corvin comes out of the house at seven, as he always does, with his ledger under his arm._  
_He stops at the end of the row. He looks at the rail for a long time._  
**Corvin Vale** _(think)_: Frost does odd things.  
_He writes something in the ledger, and goes in._  
_By noon there are people at the Circuit fence who have never come up the valley before._  
_Two children from the lane, holding hands. A man with a bicycle, who says he was only passing, and stays all afternoon._  
_By then the frost is long gone from the rail. They have come to look at a small grey runner whose name they know._  
_Corvin comes out again and looks at the fence. Then he looks across the yard, at you._  

#### Done `s27-was-it-you`

_He walks over without hurrying. His hands are bare and blue at the knuckles._  
**Corvin Vale**: Hazel’s heir. A straight question, and I’d like a straight answer, since she always gave me one.  
**Corvin Vale**: Somebody wrote on my rail in the night. I don’t care for things written on my rails. Was it you?  
_Behind you, through the shed window, you can feel a very small person holding very still._  

**Choices**

- **“Yes. It was me.”** — sets `{"s27-truth":"shield"}`
    - **You**: Yes. It was me.
    - **Corvin Vale**: In frost. At four in the morning. In letters a hand high, in a spider’s silk.
    - **You**: I’ve got very steady hands.
    - Corvin looks at you for a long moment, the way he looks at a runner that has started late.
    - **Corvin Vale**: Hazel used to lie like that. Badly, and always for somebody else. I’ll be watching my rails, then.
- **“Ask the frost.”** — sets `{"s27-truth":"frost"}`
    - **You**: Ask the frost. It’s yours, isn’t it? It’s been on your rails all summer.
    - Corvin’s hand goes to his breast pocket, and stops halfway, and comes down again.
    - **Corvin Vale**: …Frost doesn’t spell.
    - **You**: This frost does.
    - He looks past you, at the shed he has never once been into. He doesn’t say anything else; he doesn’t need to.
- **Say nothing. Just wait.** — sets `{"s27-truth":"silent"}`
    - You say nothing, and Corvin waits. You have learned waiting from better people than him, and he can tell.
    - After a while he stops looking at you, and looks past you, at the shed window, for a long time.
    - **Corvin Vale**: Hm.
    - He goes back to the house. He doesn’t write anything in the ledger this time.

### Step 7: At dusk, carry Spindle to the main railing by the finishing straight.

Hint: Bring the matchbox. The main railing runs along the straight, opposite the stands. · check: `carry:s27-spindle:main-railing`

#### Scene `s27-second-night`

_Spindle has been working on the second word all day. You can tell from the look of the web: well used, a little ragged._  
_She points one leg, quite firmly, past the pens and out at the finishing straight._  
**Sparks** _(shock)_: The main rail? With the stands right there? She’s not shy, is she. I love her. I’m terrified.  
_Somewhere in the house a lamp is lit in an upstairs window. It stays lit._  
_At dusk, Spindle walks into her matchbox. You put it in your inside pocket, against your shirt, where it’s warm._  
_You cross the yard to the straight in the last of the light, as if you were only going for a walk._  

#### Done `s27-somebodys`

_Dawn. All along the main rail, opposite the empty stands, in white: SOMEBODY’S._  
_Fern’s word. Fern, at the fence, has both hands over her mouth._  
_That morning a cart comes over the pass a week early. Mr Tull gets down and walks along the rail, reading it._  
**Mr Tull** _(think)_: Mr Vale. I heard you had a runner with a name. I didn’t think you held with names.  
**Corvin Vale**: I don’t. It’s the frost. It does odd things up here.  
**Mr Tull**: So I see. It’s a lot of odd, for one rail.  
_He doesn’t stay. He climbs back onto his cart and looks at the fence, and the people at it, and frowns all the way up the pass._  
_By noon, the little grey runner has been moved out of the row to a pen behind the house, where the public can’t see._  

### Step 8: Carry Spindle in Sparks’s toolbox to the inside pen behind the house.

Hint: Walk slowly past the house. Nobody stops someone carrying a toolbox. · check: `carry:s27-spindle:inside-pen`

#### Scene `s27-toolbox`

**Sparks** _(angry)_: Behind the house? That’s the coldest pen up here. The tap’s frozen in August. He’s hiding it.  
**Sparks**: Right. Nobody looks twice at a toolbox. Toolboxes are invisible. I’ve walked into the judges’ tent with one.  
_She empties the top tray of her toolbox and lines it with a clean rag, which is the first clean rag you have ever seen her own._  
_Spindle walks in. She settles between a spool of fuse wire and a very small spanner, as if she has always travelled this way._  
_You go past the house at dusk with the toolbox swinging. The lamp in the upstairs window is still lit._  
_Nobody stops you. Sparks was right: nobody looks twice at a toolbox._  

#### Done `s27-warm`

_Behind the house the frost is thickest. The inside pen’s rail is so cold your breath crackles on it._  
_At first light, across it, the third word: WARM._  
_Corvin comes round the corner of the house in his shirtsleeves, as if he had been waiting up._  
_He stands at the rail. Then he lays his bare hand flat on the word._  
_You wait for the frost to melt under his palm. It doesn’t, not a single letter._  
_He takes his hand away and looks at it. Then he touches his breast pocket, once, and goes back inside._  

### Step 9: Go up to the Circuit for the Autumn Show.

Hint: The Show is on the first autumn morning. Spindle rides in your hat brim. Don’t bend down suddenly. · check: `watch:s27-autumn-show`

#### Scene `s27-ready`

_The first autumn dawn. The frost in the Grove is real frost now, and the leaves have started to let go._  
_Clover comes up the valley with you, and helps you brush the little grey runner through the rail of its pen._  
**Clover** _(soft)_: Gently. Grey ones are thin-skinned. Long strokes, and stop when it leans in. That means more.  
_It leans in. Clover stops brushing and lets it._  
_Byte is at the rail with his clipboard. His newest stopwatch has a very small web across the strap, and in it, one word: ABOVE._  
**Byte** _(think)_: It isn’t accurate. There’s no data. I’m keeping it.  
**Byte**: I’ve worked out its chances of placing. To three decimal places.  
**Byte** _(sad)_: I’m not going to tell you the number.  
_Spindle will not be left in the shed. She makes this extremely clear by walking up your sleeve._  
_In the end she rides in the brim of your hat, which is the best seat at the Circuit, and she knows it._  

#### Done `s27-show`

_The Autumn Show. Flags, a brass band of three, and more people at the fence than the Circuit has ever had._  
_Most of them aren’t here for the racing. They are here to see a small grey runner with a name._  
_Corvin’s champion, the big grey Number One, wins as it always does. It doesn’t look at the crowd; it never has._  
_The little grey runner comes last. It isn’t slow, exactly: it spends the whole race looking at the people who came._  
_It doesn’t place. The judge pins the three rosettes on three grey runners who don’t look down at them._  
_Mr Tull’s cart is waiting by the gate. He is eating a sandwich, and looks as if he wishes he weren’t there._  
_In your hat brim, Spindle sits very still. Then she taps the brim twice, which you have learned means: tonight._  

### Step 10: Hold the lantern low for Spindle while she writes her last word.

Hint: Stay by the finishing straight and keep the light steady. She’ll tell you when she’s done. · check: `wait:s27-spindle:home-word`

#### Scene `s27-home-word`

_That night Spindle doesn’t walk into the matchbox. She walks onto your hand, and stays there._  
_You carry her to the very start of the finishing straight. She looks down the whole length of the rail._  
_Then she starts: not one letter, but one stroke of one letter, from the top rail to the bottom._  
_It is going to be the biggest word she has ever written. It is going to take all night._  
_You hold the lantern low, so she can see, and walk beside her down the rail, a step at a time._  
_Your Nubbin falls asleep in your pocket, and wakes, and sleeps again. The lantern hisses._  

#### Done `s27-midnight`

_At midnight the house door opens. You shutter the lantern and step back into the shed doorway, out of the light._  
_Corvin comes down the straight in his coat, with a lamp. He is looking for something, and he finds it._  
_Halfway along the rail, in the lamplight, a grey-brown spider is halfway through a letter._  
_He stands over her for a long time. Then he lifts his hand, the cold one, over the web._  
_He holds it there, and you don’t breathe. Spindle doesn’t stop working, not for a single thread._  
_He lowers his hand._  
**Corvin Vale** _(soft)_: Spell it right, then.
