**Marina** _(soft)_: Scared? Of this? Don’t be daft. Nobody could be scared of this.  
_She is holding your sleeve, tight._  
**Marina** _(soft)_: …I am, though. I am. And I don’t mind a bit. Isn’t that strange?  
_None of you looks up. It seems very important, all at once, to look only at Portly and the grass and your own feet._  
_Then a small wind comes off the water, warm, and goes over the island once, and through your hair, and away._  
_…_  
_You are standing on an island at dawn, and you can’t think why you’re both so quiet._  
**Marina** _(happy)_: Well, look at that. There he is. Fast asleep, the little beggar.  
_Whatever there was to remember, you have been kindly let off remembering it. Your Nubbin yawns, and its light is just a light._  
_Marina lifts Portly into the boat, still asleep, and wraps him in her scarf. He doesn’t wake._  

#### Done `s29-ford`

_The sun is up when you come round the bend to the ford. The old otter is still on his stone, just as you left him, and so is Dusty._  
_Portly wakes all at once, sees where he is, and goes over the side in one wriggle._  
_Two brown heads in the shallows, close together, turning round and round each other. The old otter makes no sound at all._  
**Dusty** _(soft)_: Oh. Oh, look at them.  
_Marina is wiping her face with her sleeve, again and again. It doesn’t seem to be working._  
**Marina** _(soft)_: Don’t know what’s the matter with me. Must’ve been the early start.  
_Before they go, Portly comes back up the bank. You put the blue bead down on the stone for him._  
_He looks at it, and at you. Then he noses it back to your boot, puts a wet pebble down beside it, and goes._  
**Dusty** _(soft)_: He’s giving it back. That’s a thank-you, that is.  
**Marina** _(happy)_: That’s him saying he’s done with it. Same thing, with otters.  
**Dusty**: Where was he, in the end?  
**Marina** _(think)_: On the island. Asleep in the grass. That’s all.  
_She says it as if there was something else, a moment ago, that she has put down somewhere and can’t think where._  
_The old otter looks at you once from the water. Then the two of them go off downstream, under the bank, and the river closes over._  

### Step 14: Get to Marina’s boathouse before she goes.

Hint: The boathouse is past the tide pools, where the river meets the sea. Dusty is already running; go with them. · check: `find:marina:s29-boathouse`

#### Scene `s29-seafarer`

_Autumn. The leaves are turning, and one morning the swallows have gone without saying goodbye._  
_A man comes up the valley road with a pack on his back: brown as a walnut, a gold ring in one ear, eyes creased from too much water._  
**The seafarer** _(happy)_: Afternoon. Is this the river that comes down off the falls? I’ve followed it all the way up from the sea.  
_Marina is mending nets on the bank. He sits down beside her to rest his feet, and doesn’t get up again until the light goes._  
**The seafarer** _(soft)_: South of here the sea’s the colour of a bottle. The harbours have boats painted every colour you can name, and some you can’t.  
**The seafarer**: Nights on deck, you lie on your back and the stars swing over the mast like a lamp on a chain.  
**The seafarer**: There’s a town on a hill where the whole street is steps, and every step’s a shop, and every shop sells something you’ve never heard of.  
**The seafarer**: I’ve been everywhere you can get to by water. And there’s always more water. That’s the beauty of it.  
_Marina doesn’t say anything. She has stopped mending, and the net lies in her lap with the needle stuck in it._  
**The seafarer** _(happy)_: Well. I’ll be on. There’s a ship at the river mouth at the end of the week, and tides don’t wait.  
_He goes on down to the coast. Marina watches him the whole way, until he’s a speck, and then until he isn’t._  

#### Done `s29-packing`

_At dusk Dusty comes running up to your gate, out of breath. Together you get to the boathouse just as the lamps come on._  
_Marina is moving about quickly and quietly, folding jumpers into a bag on the bed, and not looking at either of you._  
_Her eyes have gone grey-green, like the sea in a picture. She looks straight through you, at something a long way south._  
**Marina** _(soft)_: I’m only going to have a look. A season. Two. Down the coast, and then on. I’ve never been anywhere.  
**Marina**: All my life, the same river. Up to the weir and back. Up to the weir and back.  
**Dusty** _(sad)_: You love the river! You told me. You said there was nothing half so—  
_She doesn’t stop folding._  

**Choices**

- **Hold on to her, Dusty’s way, and don’t let go.** — sets `{"s29-wayfarer":"held"}`
    - Dusty sits down on the bag. You take one of Marina’s arms, and Dusty takes the other.
    - **Marina**: Let go. Let GO. I mean it. You don’t understand, either of you.
    - She pulls, and you hold. She pulls harder, and you hold, and Dusty holds, and nobody says anything.
    - Then all at once she sits down on the floor between the two of you, and the grey-green goes out of her eyes.
    - **Marina**: …What was I doing? What was I doing with that bag?
- **Sit down beside her. Don’t say anything.** — sets `{"s29-wayfarer":"sat"}`
    - You sit down on the end of her bed, next to the bag, and say nothing at all.
    - Marina folds another jumper, and another. Then she stops with one half folded, and looks at you sitting there.
    - Dusty sits down on Marina’s other side. The three of you sit while the light goes, and nobody lights the lamp.
    - **Marina**: …Hazel used to do that. Just sit. It used to drive me spare.
    - After a long time, she puts the jumper down.
- **Put a pencil in her hand. Ask her to draw you the tide pools.** — sets `{"s29-wayfarer":"pencil"}`
    - You find a pencil on the windowsill and the brown paper off a parcel, and put them both in her hands.
    - **You**: Draw me the tide pools. Before you go. I’ll never remember them right.
    - **Marina**: Now? It’s getting dark. I’ll do it when I… fine. Fine. Quickly.
    - She draws the top pool, then the next, then the one with the anemone that never shuts and the crab under the green stone.
    - By the bottom pool she is drawing slowly, and her eyes are her own colour again. She has forgotten the bag.
    - **Marina**: There’s one I’ve never drawn. Hang on. Pass me another sheet.

### Step 15: Haul Marina’s boat up the bank for the winter, under the willows.

Hint: Logs under the keel for rollers, and all three of you. She goes upside down, so the rain runs off. · check: `carry:s29-boat:willows`

#### Scene `s29-laidup`

_A grey, blowy morning near the end of autumn. The willows are dropping their leaves into the river by the handful._  
**Marina** _(think)_: Time she came out, before the hard frosts. You don’t leave a boat in over winter. Not one you love.  
_You lay three round logs on the bank for rollers. Dusty takes the bow rope; you and Marina push from the stern._  
**Dusty**: Nobody told me boats were this heavy when they’re not floating. It feels like a trick.  
**Marina**: Heave. And heave. Mind your toes on the rollers.  
_Your Nubbin rides in the bow all the way up the bank and refuses to get out, so that it is still, technically, boating._  

#### Complete `s29-complete`

_Under the willows you turn her over, gently, and lay her keel-up on the trestles where Marina was painting her in the summer._  
_The three of you stand and look at her. She looks like a beetle having a rest._  
**Marina** _(think)_: I was cross with you both for a whole day after. Did you notice? I slammed doors.  
**Dusty**: We noticed. The boathouse has one door. You slammed it a lot.  
**Marina** _(soft)_: Thank you. For not letting go. I’d have hated it, I think: somewhere far off and warm, wishing I was cold here.  
**Marina** _(happy)_: Here. Take the rug. It’s got gherkin on it. It’ll always have gherkin on it.  
**Marina** _(think)_: Her little sister’s in the boathouse: the flat one, for winter water. Take her out whenever you like. Vests on.  
**Dusty** _(soft)_: Same time next summer?  
**Marina** _(happy)_: Before. March, if it’s mild. You get the vests down from the loft and smell them. That’s the first day of the year.  
**Marina** _(soft)_: Rule six. Come back. That one’s for all of you.  
_Down at the ford a brown head comes up, looks at the three of you for a while, and goes under without a ripple._  

**Rewards**

- Marina’s boat: rowing (feature): Row on the stream below the Grove and on the river, with a Nubbin in the bow. From the line’s end you row her flat little winter sister instead, till spring.
- The river section (feature): The river from the backwater to the weir opens: the otter family lives there, Granny Brock’s green door is in the Wild Wood, and Dusty’s cliff window is lit again.
- Cork vest (cosmetic): A tiny cork vest Marina cut down from an old one. Your Nubbin wears it with enormous pride, on water and off it.
- Willow-pattern picnic rug (decor): Marina’s blue-and-white picnic rug, for the Grove. It has gherkin on it. It will always have gherkin on it.
- Portly’s gift, and a pebble (item): The bead, the ribbon or the bell you gave Portly, given back at the ford with a wet river pebble beside it. A keepsake; it does nothing at all.

Sets on completion: `{"s29Done":true}`

**Learning Grove swaps**

- `s29-digging#4` → Oh, crumbs! Oh, rubble!
- `s29-digging#7` → That’s it. No more digging. Not today!
- `s29-boats#6` → Honestly? Pottering about in a boat is the best thing there is. Nothing else comes close.
- `s29-picnic#3` → Wait! Stop! Nobody could eat all that!
- `s29-wildwood#6` → And then Dusty started seeing things in the dark.
- `s29-packing#5` → You love the river! You told me. You said it was the best thing there is!

**Notes:** FOR FUN (Tier 2). Sets s29Done and two local flags: s29-gift (step 4: bead | ribbon | bell) and s29-wayfarer (step 14: held | sat | pencil). Writes no campaign flag. Reads s4Done, s5Done and the chapter (see VARIANTS). THEME. Homely comfort and ancient awe: friendship, homesickness, the dark wood and the lit window, and a dawn nobody is allowed to keep. Comedy where Grahame is comic (the boat, the capsize, the gherkin); grave where he is grave (Dulce Domum, the island). SIGNATURE CHOICE. Step 4’s gift for Portly pays off eight steps later in step 12 (how you find his trail at night) and again in step 13 (what he gives back at the ford, with a pebble). Step 14’s choice decides the complete scene. Marina stays in every branch (bible conflict 9). The "sat" branch acts out Hazel’s third rule without quoting it, and Marina names Hazel’s habit, not the rule. TIMING. A step’s scene plays when the previous step’s check is met; its done scene plays when its own check is met. Gates: steps 1–4 from c4-close (chapter 4, and on into chapter 5 if the player is slow). Step 5 waits for chapter 5 (after c5-open: frost in August) and then plays the next dusk the player is in the Grove; steps 5–7 run as one night and the next morning. Step 8 waits for c6-close (after Fossil’s farewell); if c6-close has already played when step 7 ends, it plays the next morning. Steps 9–10 follow on the same evening as step 8. Step 11 plays two in-game days after step 10. Steps 12–13 run as one night and the dawn after. Step 14 waits for chapter 7 (c7-open) and then the next afternoon; step 15 plays the morning after step 14. Closes when chapter 8 opens (Marina has the ledge path to guard): if the line is unfinished then, it closes unfinished, with no rewards beyond the cork vest and the gift keepsake if those were already given. Never during S4’s sluice night (s4-sluice). VARIANTS (in SIDE_STORY_S29_VARIANTS): step 6 done: s29-kitchen-after replaces s29-kitchen if c6-open (Fossil’s farewell) has played; only Dusty’s listening line changes, and Fossil is never named in either. step 8 scene: s29-low-river-full replaces s29-low-river if s4Done (the river is up since S4 opened the sluice). step 10 done: s29-supper-walk replaces s29-supper if s5Done (Dusty remembers the walk you took with Fossil). step 12 done: s29-night is for s29-gift = bead; s29-night-ribbon and s29-night-bell replace it for the others. step 13 done: s29-ford is for bead; s29-ford-ribbon and s29-ford-bell replace it for the others. complete: s29-complete is for s29-wayfarer = held; s29-complete-sat and s29-complete-pencil replace it. s29-brock-knock is an ambient scene after the line: tap Granny Brock’s green door in the Wild Wood by day (by night the door does not answer). It can play any number of times and gives nothing. STAGING. Step 5’s middle (s29-wildwood lines 4–9) is a cutaway of Dusty alone, told in the past tense as Dusty’s own later telling: dim the frame, no player sprite. The faces are eye-glints at the edge of the lantern radius, never sprites, and gone the moment the camera centres on them; the pattering is a soft SFX bed. Nothing in the Wood attacks or touches anyone. In s29-boats#6, set "nothing" (the first one) in italics if the dialogue box supports emphasis; it is italic in Grahame. The Lantern Song lines in the supper scenes are sung (children’s voices, the campaign melody, one verse only, no refrain). THE ISLAND (s29-island). Light: pre-dawn pearl going to gold. The hollow is just flattened grass. Show no figure, shadow, footprint, hoof, horn, pipe or face, and play no melody: the "music" is a breathy reed-and-water texture that stops when the wind comes. Never let the camera look up. Your Nubbin’s ball glows sky-colour, then returns to normal after the wind. The journal records only “Found Portly asleep on the island.” The island is never landable outside this scene: afterwards the boat glides past it. CANON. Marina = the Water Rat, Dusty (they/them) = the Mole, Granny Brock (a person) = the Badger, the seafarer (a person) = the Sea Rat. The otters never speak and are not speakers: Portly and the old otter act only through narration. Your Nubbin never speaks. Fossil is never named before step 10, so the chapter 5 scenes work whether or not S5 has introduced her; from step 10 (after chapter 6) she is named, gently, by Dusty only. Marina’s line about the island spring (“same water as behind the falls, my gran reckoned”) is a hint and nothing more: it adds nothing to canon and must never be followed up. The cold in the Wild Wood and the low river are noticed, never explained (chapter 7 explains the Stillness). Corvin does not appear. SAFETY. Every water scene has Marina and everyone in cork vests; the capsize is comic and nobody is in danger. Portly’s bell is on a loose loop made to slip off, and he shrugs it off at the ford. CHECKS. carry:s29-boat:stream and :willows use Carry with a second carrier (Marina). explore:s29-river:backwater is a ride-along (Marina rows; the player can tap to point at fish, herons and the backwater). collect:s29-picnic:river is a short gather in the shallows: basket, rug, two oars (gherkins drift off to the otters and can’t be caught). give:s29-portly:gift is met by the choice in s29-lessons. find:s29-dusty:wild-wood and find:s29-brock-door:wild-wood are lantern-radius searches with no fail state: the lantern dims slowly but never goes out, and standing still makes Dusty’s muttering louder. walk:s29-culvert:fields is a short walk in the dark following Granny Brock’s lantern. minigame:s29-rowing:weir and :night use the new rowing game (SIM). talk:marina:s29-dusk-path is met by reaching Marina at the bend. collect:s29-kindling:cliff-house is a dig through the wet top of the woodpile. watch:s29-old-otter:ford is a hold-still beat (~15 seconds, any tap resets it gently, no fail). deliver:s29-portly:ford is a downstream row with no steering errors possible. find:marina:s29-boathouse is a walk to the boathouse with Dusty. SIM: (1) a rowing mini-game: two-button or two-thumb strokes, the boat yaws if one side pulls harder, and the player faces backwards (the view rotates to show where you are going on request). Courses: weir (with a carry-over-the-shallows segment when s4Done is false) and night (lantern radius; the step 12 cue is a visual glint for bead or ribbon and a positional sound for bell). No fail state; a capsize only happens in step 3’s scene. (2) Marina’s boat as a usable feature on the stream and river after step 4 (the summer boat), then her flat winter boat from the boathouse after s29-complete. A Nubbin can be set to ride in the bow. (3) The river section map (backwater, ford, weir, slipway, island seen only) and the otter family as ambient wildlife that never comes within reach. (4) Granny Brock’s door in the Wild Wood (s29-brock-knock). (5) Dusty’s home location moves back to the cliff house after s29-supper (window lit at night). (6) Reading the chapter for the kitchen variant. REWARDS. The cork vest is given in s29-lessons (step 4); Portly’s gift and pebble at the ford (step 13 done; its label and sprite follow s29-gift); the rowing feature after step 4 (summer boat) and the winter boat at s29-complete; the river section and the rug at s29-complete.

## S30. What Sad Is For

*Inside Out (Pixar, 2015) and Inside Out 2 (Pixar, 2024): a Joy that keeps a girl so happy she can’t ask for help, the discovery that sadness tells the people who love you where to find you, and a worry that keeps everyone too safe. A light nod; two short quotes, everything else original* · audience: all · where: Your gate, then Clover’s cottage down the lane (the deep kitchen windowsill, the shed, the rain butt) · when: Chapter 6, the morning after c6-close (Fossil’s farewell). Steps 1–6 in chapter 6; step 7 after c7-stillness; steps 8–9 in chapter 7’s first frost week; steps 10–14 in chapter 8. Closes when chapter 9 opens.

**Cast:** Dimple (Joy (Inside Out, Pixar 2015), a light nod only; an original Nubbin of Clover’s), Drizzle (Sadness (Inside Out, Pixar 2015), a light nod only; an original Nubbin of Clover’s), Fidget (Anxiety (Inside Out 2, Pixar 2024), a light nod only; an original Nubbin of Clover’s)

### Step 1: Go down the lane to Clover’s cottage for a cup of tea, and meet her Nubbins.

Hint: Clover’s is the cottage past Fern’s with the deep kitchen windowsill and the rain butt by the door. · check: `visit:s30-clover-cottage`

#### Scene `s30-smile`

_The morning after the rain, the whole valley drips. Somebody is rattling your gate, trying to push it without lifting._  
_It’s Clover. A small gold Nubbin is bouncing on her shoulder like a kettle just before it boils._  
**Clover** _(happy)_: I know, I know. Lift and push. I never remember till I’m standing here pushing.  
**Clover**: This is Dimple. One of mine. She’s smiled since the hill. Since Dusty’s Fossil. Smiled all night. Smiled in her sleep.  
_Dimple beams at you. She beams at the gatepost, and at a slug on the gatepost, with exactly the same beam._  
**Clover** _(happy)_: At least somebody’s cheerful.  
_Behind Clover, on the wet path, four more Nubbins sit in a damp heap, as if somebody had dropped them there and walked on._  
**Clover** _(sad)_: That’s the rest. Drizzle, Bristle, Flinch, and her ladyship, who only eats red things. They’ve taken it hard.  
**Clover**: Fossil sat on my kitchen sill every digging day, you know. Years. Dusty dropped her off on the way to the cliff.  
**Clover** _(think)_: Come down for tea. I want somebody to look at Dimple who isn’t me. I keep looking at her and thinking: good. Good.  
_Your Nubbin has come to the gate. It studies Dimple’s smile for a long moment, and then, carefully, puts one on._  

#### Done `s30-sill`

_Clover’s kitchen is warm and loud and full of steam. Bristle is stamping on a saucepan lid; Flinch has jumped into the bread bin._  
**Clover** _(happy)_: Hazel said my kitchen was like the inside of somebody’s head. Everybody shouting, nobody in charge.  
_The windowsill is deep enough to sit in. On it is a flat old cushion with a dent in the middle, the shape of somebody small and round._  
**Clover** _(soft)_: Fossil’s. Dusty won’t take it back, and I can’t bring myself to plump it.  
_Dimple has filled the dent. Every toy she owns is in it: a cotton reel, a pine cone, a sock and half a wooden peg, piled up high._  
_Drizzle climbs the curtain, very slowly, and lays one pale-blue hand flat in the last gap in the dent._  
_Dimple is there at once. She takes Drizzle by the wrist and pulls her off the sill, still smiling._  
_Dimple beams at her, and goes back to patting the pile flat._  
_Then you see the ball above Dimple’s head. It has gone pale and thin, like a soap bubble that has been out too long._  
**Clover** _(happy)_: See? Not a cloud on her. She’s my good one, this week.  
_Clover is looking at the smile. She isn’t looking up._  

**Choices**

- **Point out Dimple’s ball to Clover.**
    - **Clover**: Balls flicker. It’s been a big week for everybody. Look at that face, though.
    - She means it kindly. She goes on looking at the face.
- **Say nothing yet. Keep watching.**
    - You drink your tea and watch the ball, and the ball doesn’t change.
    - Dimple sees you looking, and smiles harder.

### Step 2: Go out to the rain butt and see what Dimple is doing to Drizzle.

Hint: The yard is round the back of Clover’s. Go quietly; Flinch is asleep in the bucket by the door. · check: `watch:s30-acorn-ring`

#### Scene `s30-acorns`

_Next afternoon you’re back at Clover’s, holding a skein of wool while she winds it. It is drizzling, which Drizzle would love._  
**Clover** _(think)_: Sparks came by. Sparks says what Dimple needs is a party. Sparks says that about most things. Broken legs. Tax.  
**Clover**: She might be right, mind. She’s got streamers. She’s got a cake tin shaped like a cake.  
_Through the window, something is happening in the yard, by the rain butt._  
_Dimple is herding Drizzle into the corner between the butt and the wall. She does it the way you herd a hen: arms wide, a lot of bouncing._  
**Clover** _(happy)_: Look at them, playing. Go and see, if you like. I’ll wind.  

#### Done `s30-ring`

_Drizzle sits in the corner under the drip from the rain butt. Round her, Dimple has laid a ring of acorns, nose to tail, very neat._  
_Drizzle puts one foot over the acorns. Dimple bounces over and nudges her back in, smiling, quick and kind and firm._  
_Drizzle tries again, and again: nudge, nudge. Each time, Dimple adds another acorn to the ring._  
_Your Nubbin, who has followed you out, starts fetching acorns too, to help. It doesn’t know what for._  
_Drizzle looks at you over the ring. She doesn’t look upset; she looks like someone waiting to be let out of a room._  
_Drizzle says nothing. She lifts one foot over the acorns again, and waits to see what you do._  

**Choices**

- **Step over the acorns and lift Drizzle out.** — sets `{"s30-ring":"broke"}`
    - You lift her out. She is cool and heavy, like a stone from a stream.
    - She goes straight back to the drip from the butt and sits under it, outside the ring, with her face turned up.
    - Dimple stares at the gap in the ring. Then she fills it, very fast, with nobody inside, and bounces off indoors.
- **Sit down on the wet grass outside the ring, next to Drizzle.** — sets `{"s30-ring":"sat"}`
    - You sit down outside the acorns. Drizzle shuffles to the edge of the ring and leans against the inside of it, towards you.
    - Dimple brings more acorns. She builds a second ring round the two of you, and smiles the whole while.
    - When it is done she looks at it, and at you, as if she can’t remember what it was for. Then she bounces off indoors.
- **Leave it. She’s trying so hard. Go and help with Sparks’s party plan.** — sets `{"s30-ring":"helped"}`
    - You leave the ring be. Dimple is trying so hard; it seems unkind to spoil it.
    - Sparks is at Clover’s gate with a crate of streamers and a face full of plans. You go and hold the other end of things.
    - Behind you, Drizzle watches you go. She doesn’t try the acorns again.

### Step 3: Run Dimple’s Bounce Party in Clover’s yard: the streamer race, the hat toss and the cake.

Hint: Three party games, one after another. Nobody can lose, Sparks says, which mainly means Sparks can’t. · check: `minigame:s30-bounce-party`

#### Scene `s30-party-ask`

**Sparks** _(happy)_: PARTY. Saturday. Clover’s yard. Dimple’s the guest of honour and attendance is compulsory and fun.  
**Sparks**: Streamers. Races. Bunting. A cake shaped like a cake. Took me all night. It’s a sponge. It looks like a sponge.  
**Sparks** _(happy)_: Nothing cheers a Nubbin up like being cheered at. That’s science. Byte, tell them it’s science.  
**Byte** _(think)_: It isn’t science. I’m coming, though. I’m bringing a chart.  
**Ember**: I got dragged. I’m here under protest. Where’s the cake.  
_By Saturday, Clover’s yard is bunting from the shed to the rain butt. Every Nubbin in the lane has turned up, because there is cake._  
_Dimple is in the middle of it in a paper hat, bouncing so high that her hat keeps landing on other people._  

#### Done `s30-party`

_Dimple wins the streamer race and the hat toss. She bounces round the cake eleven times before anyone can cut it._  
_Your Nubbin copies every bounce, a beat behind. By the end it is worn out, and Dimple is not._  
**Sparks** _(happy)_: LOOK AT HER GO. That’s a happy Nubbin. That’s a party working.  
**Ember** _(angry)_: That’s not happy. That’s busy.  
**Sparks** _(shock)_: …What?  
**Ember** _(soft)_: I know busy. I was busy for a whole year after Ash. Cleaned the house twice a day. Nobody could get a word in.  
**Ember**: She’s furious, that one. Watch her land. She stamps every landing. She just hasn’t found who to shout at.  
**Byte** _(think)_: Two hundred and twelve smiles an hour. I counted. And her ball hasn’t gone above a two all afternoon.  
**Byte**: Out of ten. On my scale. I made a scale.  
**Byte** _(angry)_: The face and the ball disagree. And balls can’t be wrong. They haven’t got the muscles for it.  
_He seems very annoyed about this, as if Dimple has done it on purpose to spoil his numbers._  

### Step 4: Watch Dimple with each of her five favourite things.

Hint: The sill and the bedtime hum are at Clover’s, the puddles in her yard, Sparks’s racers at the pond, and Byte wherever Byte is. · check: `watch:s30-dimple:five-things`

#### Scene `s30-list`

_Next morning Clover is on your step with a list. It is written on the back of a seed packet and underlined in three places._  
**Clover** _(think)_: Ember’s wrong. She’s fine. But I’ve written down her favourite things, so we can check. Just to show Ember.  
**Clover**: One, the windowsill. Two, stamping puddles with Drizzle. Three, chasing Sparks’s fast ones round the pond.  
**Clover** _(happy)_: Four, Byte’s shoelaces. She pulls them undone. He pretends to mind.  
**Clover** _(soft)_: Five, the hum at bedtime. I hum, she sways. Every night since she hatched.  
**Clover**: If she’s doing those, she’s fine. Five out of five. That’s fine, isn’t it?  

#### Done `s30-five`

_The sill: Dimple won’t climb up. She sits underneath it and smiles at the skirting board._  
_The puddles: Drizzle stamps one, hopefully. Dimple smiles at the splash and doesn’t move._  
_Sparks’s fast ones go tearing round the pond three times, slowing down each time they pass her. She doesn’t chase._  
_At bedtime Clover hums. Dimple lies on her back in her basket and smiles at the ceiling, quite still._  
_Last, Byte, on Clover’s step with his boots stuck out. Dimple comes and looks at his laces for a long time._  
_Then she smiles at them, and goes back indoors._  
**Byte** _(sad)_: …She didn’t pull them.  
**Byte** _(sad)_: I’ve been tying them in single knots. For weeks. So she could get them undone. I didn’t tell anyone that.  
**Clover** _(sad)_: Nought out of five.  
_Clover sits down next to Byte on the step, and neither of them says anything for a while._  

### Step 5: Wait by Dusty’s hole by the stream and see what happens.

Hint: Don’t dig and don’t call them out. Stand at the edge for a while. · check: `wait:s30-dusty-hole`

#### Scene `s30-hole`

_By the stream there is a hole that wasn’t there yesterday. It is very deep, it is getting deeper, and it is singing._  
_That’s Dusty, at the bottom of it, singing a digging song very loudly with a lot of extra verses._  
**Dusty** _(happy)_: Morning! Lovely morning. Grand day for a hole. I’m not digging for anything. That’s what makes it relaxing.  
**Dusty** _(happy)_: Look at that soil. Look at that worm. Hello, worm. Everything’s fine, worm.  
_Dimple has followed you from Clover’s. She takes one look at the hole, dives in, and starts scooping earth out with both hands._  
_Dusty digs faster, so Dimple digs faster. They grin at each other across the hole like two people trying to win an argument._  
_Your Nubbin starts flinging soil about on the bank, to be part of it. Most of it lands on you._  
**Dusty** _(happy)_: See? She gets it. Dig. Keep your hands busy and the rest of you can’t catch up.  

#### Done `s30-drizzle`

_Then Drizzle comes down the bank, slowly, the way she does everything. She sits at the edge of the hole and says nothing at all._  
_Dusty’s spade stops. Dimple goes on scooping for a while on her own, and then she stops too._  
**Dusty** _(sad)_: …She used to sit like that. Fossil. On the edge, while I dug. Just there.  
**Dusty** _(sad)_: I dug her out with a spoon, you know. Two days. Twenty years and a bit. I was still counting the bit.  
_Dusty sits down in the bottom of the hole with the spade across their knees. Then they cry, properly, for the first time since the hill._  
_Drizzle climbs down and leans on them, without patting or fussing. She leans with the whole of her weight, like a sandbag._  
_…_  
_After a long time Dusty wipes their face with the back of a muddy wrist, which makes it worse._  
**Dusty** _(soft)_: I’m okay now.  
_Up on the bank, Dimple is watching. Her smile slips at one corner; she puts it back, and it slips again._  

### Step 6: Go up the hill path with Luna at three in the morning.

Hint: Set out after midnight, without a lantern. Luna says the moon is enough, and Dimple would see a light. · check: `walk:s30-hill-path:night`

#### Scene `s30-luna`

_Dawn. Somebody knocks very quietly, which in this valley means Luna, on her way to bed._  
**Luna** _(think)_: Sorry. My evening, your morning. I wouldn’t, only it’s three nights now.  
**Luna**: I work the hill at night; the dew’s best at three. Three nights running, at three, Clover’s gold one has been on the hill path.  
**Luna**: She goes up towards Fossil’s flower. Halfway, she stops, and turns round, and goes home.  
**Luna** _(sad)_: She doesn’t bounce up there. That’s what got me. I didn’t know she could walk.  
**Luna** _(soft)_: Things look like what they are, at night. Nobody’s watching, so nobody bothers. Come up with me tonight.  

#### Done `s30-path`

_Three in the morning, and the hill is grey and silver: wet grass, a fingernail of moon, and the smell of Luna’s night stocks._  
_Halfway up the path, there she is. Dimple, walking slowly uphill, on her own._  
_She isn’t smiling. Her face is just a face, and the ball above it is darker than any ball you have seen that wasn’t Sable’s._  
**Luna** _(soft)_: That’s where she stops. Every night.  
_Dimple stops and looks up the hill towards the flower, which is too far off to see. Then she turns round, and sees you._  
_For a second her face does nothing at all._  
_Then the smile goes on like a lamp, and she bolts down the hill past you, and home._  
**Luna** _(soft)_: Did you see her face? Before?  
**Luna** _(sad)_: That’s her. That’s the one who needs finding. The other one’s for us.  

### Step 7: Look closely at Hush’s flat stone in the old wall.

Hint: It’s low down by the wall lantern, under the ivy. Brush the frost off with your sleeve. · check: `inspect:s30-hush-stone`

#### Scene `s30-wall`

_The frosts have come, and the Grove is white at dawn round the fourth post. Moss, after his long talk, has not gone back up the hill._  
_You find him at the old wall, by Hush’s lantern post, with frost on his back that he hasn’t bothered to shake off._  
**Moss** _(think)_: Clover’s gold one. Luna told me. Luna tells me things at dawn, when I am too tired to argue.  
**Moss**: Hush. The quiet one. You may have heard she kept the griefs. Nobody told you how. She sat.  
**Moss** _(sad)_: When the White Year stilled half the valley, the rest of us went at the snow with shovels. Hush went and sat where the still ones could see her.  
**Moss**: She never tried to wake one. If one shivered, she took it for a conversation. That was her job, and nobody gave it her.  
**Moss** _(soft)_: People thought that made her a sad thing. They were wrong about which part was sad.  
**Moss**: Low in the wall. The flat stone, by your foot. Look at it properly. She meant it to be looked at.  

#### Done `s30-stone`

_You brush the frost off. The stone is covered in marks scratched fine as hair: rings and lines, hundreds of them, edge to edge._  
**Moss** _(soft)_: A ring for a joy kept. A line for a grief kept. That was her way of counting.  
_There are a few plain rings, and a few plain lines. Nearly all the rest are both: a ring with a line through it._  
**Moss**: Most things are both. She knew that before any of us.  
**Moss** _(happy)_: Briar thought Hush was gloomy. Hush thought Briar was loud. They were never more than a field apart, those two.  
**You**: What did she think sad was for?  
**Moss** _(soft)_: Sad is a lantern, she said. It shows people where you are.  
**Moss** _(sad)_: A Nubbin smiling on the outside is a Nubbin in the dark with its lantern out. Nobody can find it. It thinks that is a kindness.  
_Moss lays one small hand flat on the stone, over the marks, the way you would warm your hand on a mug._  

### Step 8: Come back to Clover’s at dusk and see how Fidget is settling in.

Hint: Dusk, Clover said. The frost comes down fast now; get there before the yard goes white. · check: `watch:s30-shed:dusk`

#### Scene `s30-fidget`

_The first frost week. Clover sends Bristle up the lane to fetch you, which Bristle does by stamping on your doormat until you come._  
_In Clover’s kitchen, in a nest of tea towels by the range, an egg is rocking. Then it is in pieces, and something orange is running._  
**Clover** _(happy)_: Oh! Oh, look at her. She hasn’t stopped since she cracked. Not once.  
_The new one is very small and very orange. She runs to the door, then the window, then under the table, then the door again._  
_She checks every Nubbin in the kitchen, one by one, as if she were counting them. Then she checks them all again._  
**Clover**: Fidget. That’s her name. She named herself, really. Look at her go.  
**Clover** _(happy)_: Finally, someone who fusses more than me.  
_Dimple bounces over to welcome her, beaming. Fidget counts Dimple twice._  

#### Done `s30-shed`

_At dusk the frost comes down over the yard like a cloth over a cage. Fidget goes very still for one second, and then she gets to work._  
_She herds the whole household into the shed: Bristle, Flinch, the fussy one, Drizzle, Dimple. She pushes from behind with her head._  
_Then she drags the slop bucket across the yard, an inch at a time, and shoves it hard against the shed door._  
_Fidget stands guard at the bucket, stiff as a peg, eyes wide, listening to the dark._  
**Clover** _(sad)_: Every night since the frost. In they go, and the bucket.  
_From inside the shed comes Dimple, louder than ever: bounce, bounce, bounce off the walls, a jolly, frantic thudding._  
_Nothing else. The others have gone quiet in there, the way pot plants go in a cupboard._  
**Clover** _(soft)_: She just wants them safe. I know how she feels. I’ve felt it every night of my life.  
**Clover** _(think)_: Safe is not the same as well.  
_Clover hears herself say it. She sits down, quite suddenly, on the bucket._  
**Clover** _(sad)_: That’s Moss’s. And I said it like it was mine. Well. It is now.  

### Step 9: Open the shed without frightening Fidget.

Hint: Fidget needs to see the door shut. Hang the blanket across the open doorway, stand the bucket on its hem, and leave a gap underneath. · check: `puzzle:s30-shed-door`

#### Scene `s30-blanket-ask`

**Clover** _(sad)_: I can’t just kick the bucket over. She’d go to pieces. She’s a week old and she’s doing her best.  
**Clover**: And I can’t leave them in there. Listen to Dimple. Listen to the others not.  
_Fidget sits by the bucket, guarding it. Her ball is whirling so fast it looks like a little orange wheel._  
_Your Nubbin sits down beside her, not sure why. It looks hard at the bucket too, to be helpful._  
**Clover** _(think)_: Help me open it without her knowing it’s open. You’re better at that sort of thing than me. Hazel was.  
_There’s a blanket frozen stiff on Clover’s washing line. There’s the bucket, and the shed door, which opens outwards._  

#### Done `s30-blanket`

_You open the door while Fidget is counting something else, and hang the stiff blanket across the doorway like a curtain._  
_You stand the bucket on one corner of the hem. Underneath, there is a gap a Nubbin could push through without trying._  
_Fidget comes back, inspects the blanket and the bucket, and pushes the bucket one inch further. Shut; she sits down._  
_A minute later Drizzle’s nose comes out under the hem. Then the fussy one, then Flinch, who jumps at the blanket, and then doesn’t._  
_They go in and out as they please all evening. Fidget counts them every time, and seems to find the number acceptable._  
**Clover** _(happy)_: Look at that. Shut, and open. Both. Hazel would have called that a fudge.  
**Clover** _(soft)_: She liked a fudge. She said most of the good things are fudges.  
_Dimple doesn’t come out. When you look under the hem, she is sitting at the back of the dark shed, smiling at nobody._  

### Step 10: Follow Dimple’s trail and find her.

Hint: She took her toys on Fossil’s cushion, and she is dropping them as she goes. Follow the toys. · check: `find:s30-dimple-trail`

#### Scene `s30-gone`

_Early autumn. Clover is at your gate before it is light, in her dressing gown and wellies, with Drizzle tucked under one arm._  
**Clover** _(shock)_: She’s gone. Dimple. Her basket was cold when I got up.  
**Clover** _(sad)_: She’s taken nothing but the cushion off the sill. Fossil’s cushion, toys and all.  
**Clover**: What does she want with it? She’s done nothing but pile rubbish on it for weeks.  
_Drizzle wriggles out from under Clover’s arm, drops to the path, and sets off down the lane on her own, slow and certain._  
**Clover** _(shock)_: Drizzle? Drizzle! She never goes anywhere on her own. She hates hurrying.  
_Dusty is coming up the lane with a spade on one shoulder, early as always. One look at Clover, and they put the spade down._  
**Dusty** _(soft)_: She’s going back to where the happy was. I would. I’ve been trying to all summer.  
_You go after Drizzle, all three of you, down the lane in the half dark._  

#### Done `s30-trail`

_Under Clover’s window there is a cotton reel in the frost. At the end of the lane, a pine cone; at the stile, half a wooden peg._  
_One toy at a time, the trail runs out of the lane and east along the stream, towards the striped cliff._  
**Dusty** _(sad)_: The cliff. She came with us once or twice. Rode on Fossil, mostly, which Fossil allowed. Nobody else ever got to.  
_At the foot of the cliff the rock is striped like a cake. Where it meets the ground, there is a hollow, dug down and in._  
**Dusty** _(soft)_: That’s it. The spoon hole. Where I dug her out. Two days, with a spoon.  
_Dimple is down in it, on Fossil’s cushion. She is scraping at the back of the hollow with both hands, as if there might be another egg._  
_The hollow is only waist-deep on you, but on a Nubbin the lip is a cliff. She can’t climb out, and she hasn’t tried._  
_Dimple says nothing. She scrapes, and scrapes, and smiles at the rock._  

### Step 11: Stay at the spoon hole until Dimple stops.

Hint: Don’t call to her and don’t pull her out. Stay where you are; this takes as long as it takes. · check: `sit:s30-spoon-hole`

#### Scene `s30-hollow`

_Before anybody else can move, Drizzle walks to the lip of the hollow and slides down into it, slowly, unasked._  
_She sits close to Dimple: not touching, but close. She says nothing at all._  
_Dimple turns, and the smile goes on like a lamp. She bounces, and offers Drizzle a pebble, a root, a handful of dust._  
_Drizzle doesn’t take any of it. She stays._  
**Clover** _(shock)_: Shall I go down? I should go down. I’m her— I should—  
**Dusty** _(soft)_: Leave them a minute. That one knows what she’s doing. Look at her.  
_Up at the rim you sit down in the frosty grass, because standing feels like hurrying somebody._  

#### Done `s30-stopped`

_And then Dimple stops, in the middle of a bounce, almost. She sits down hard on Fossil’s cushion, and the smile goes._  
_Not off, like a lamp. It goes the way frost goes off a window, from the edges in, until there is only her face._  
_Dimple says nothing. She looks at her own hands as if she has only just found them._  
_Up at the rim, Clover puts both hands over her mouth._  

### Step 12: Stay at the rim while Clover tells you how Dimple and Fossil met.

Hint: Just listen. The hollow will tell you when it’s over. · check: `wait:s30-spoon-hole:clover-story`

#### Scene `s30-thunder`

_Clover sits down at the rim. She doesn’t take her eyes off the hollow._  
**Clover** _(soft)_: Do you know how they met? Dimple and Fossil? I never told anyone. It didn’t seem like a story.  
**Clover**: Dimple hatched in a thunderstorm, under my kitchen table. First night of her life, and the sky fell in.  
**Clover** _(sad)_: She wailed. Hours. I tried everything. Milk, humming, the lot. Nothing.  
**Clover**: And Fossil came down off the sill. She never came down off the sill. She walked across my kitchen floor and sat beside her.  
**Clover**: Didn’t lick her. Didn’t fuss. Just sat, the whole storm, till it blew over. I made three pots of tea and drank none of them.  
**Clover** _(happy)_: After that they shared the sill. Every digging day, for years. Gold one and sand one in the window, like two loaves.  
**Dusty** _(soft)_: I never knew that. Twenty years, and I never knew that.  
**Clover** _(soft)_: Her best thing started on her worst day.  

#### Done `s30-blue-gold`

_Down in the hollow, Dimple’s ball goes blue. Not pale: deep blue, like the sky an hour after sunset._  
_Then she cries the way Nubbins cry. A thin, high whistle like wind across a bottle, and a shaking from her ears to her feet._  
_She leans sideways until she is leaning on somebody, and stays there, and whistles._  
_Nobody moves. Up at the rim Clover is crying too, without a sound, and Dusty has a hand on her back._  
_…_  
_When it stops, Dimple’s ball is still blue. And it is gold as well, the two colours turning slowly round each other._  
**Dusty** _(shock)_: I’ve never seen that. Every Nubbin in this valley, twenty years, and I have never once seen a ball do that.  
**Clover** _(soft)_: Neither have I.  
_Dimple looks up at the rim, and doesn’t smile. She looks at you all like somebody who has been found._  

### Step 13: Haul the basket up out of the spoon hole.

Hint: Pull steady, hand over hand. Jerk it and the basket spins, and Dimple will give you a look. · check: `carry:s30-basket:spoon-hole-rim`

#### Scene `s30-rope`

**Dusty** _(happy)_: Right. Basket.  
_Dusty has a coil of rope over one shoulder and a log basket on the end of it. Nobody knows where Dusty keeps rope, but they always have some._  
**Dusty**: Never go near a hole without a rope. First thing Hazel ever told me. Second thing was, give that spoon back.  
_Anybody with long enough legs climbs out. The rest go in the basket: Dimple with Fossil’s cushion, and anyone small who sat with her._  
**Clover** _(shock)_: Gently. Gently! She’s had a morning.  
**Dusty** _(soft)_: Two on the rope. You pull, I’ll count. I’m good at counting. I count bits.  

#### Done `s30-rim`

_The basket comes up over the lip. Clover has Dimple out of it before it has stopped swinging._  
_She cries all over her: into her fur, on the cushion, on her ears. Dimple lets her, ball blue and gold, quite still for once._  
**Clover** _(sad)_: Look at me. I’m worse than she is. I’m a disgrace. Somebody give me a hanky.  
_Dusty gives Clover a sock. It’s clean, mostly._  
**Clover** _(happy)_: Crying helps me slow down and obsess over the weight of life’s problems.  
_She laughs at herself, and blows her nose on the sock, and laughs harder. Dimple watches her face the whole time._  
**Dusty** _(soft)_: You can keep the sock.  

### Step 14: Sit with Dimple by Fossil’s flower.

Hint: Sit down near her on the hilltop and stay. You’ll know when it’s time to get up. · check: `sit:s30-fossils-flower`

#### Scene `s30-hill`

_That afternoon Dimple walks up the hill. Not on anyone’s shoulder: up the path, past the halfway place, slowly, on her own feet._  
_Drizzle walks beside her, slower still. Dusty walks behind with Fossil’s cushion under one arm, and doesn’t hurry anybody._  
_At the top is Fossil’s flower, sandstone-coloured, a little bent by the frost. Dimple sits down in front of it._  
_Drizzle sits on one side of her. Dusty sits on the other, cross-legged, and sets the cushion in the grass, dent up._  
_Nobody does anything. The wind goes over, and down in the valley somebody is burning leaves._  
_Your Nubbin, who has copied Dimple all autumn, comes and sits down too. It copies the sitting perfectly._  

#### Done `s30-fidget-hill`

_Then a noise comes up the path: small feet, very fast, and a thin worried squeak. Fidget._  
_She has woken in the kitchen and found everybody gone. She has come all the way up the hill alone, checking every bush._  
_At the top she spins round and round in the grass, looking for everybody at once. Her ball is whirling like a top._  
_Fidget skids to a stop in front of you, every whisker out, and says nothing at all._  
_Clover comes puffing up the path behind her, too late, in one welly._  
_Before anybody else can move, Dimple gets up. She goes to Fidget and sits down right beside her, and stays._  
_She doesn’t smile at her, or bounce. She sits, the way she was sat with._  
_Fidget spins slower, and slower. Then she stops, and leans on Dimple, and her ball settles to a small warm orange._  
**Clover** _(soft)_: Where did she learn that?  
**Dusty** _(soft)_: Under your kitchen table. In a thunderstorm.  

#### Complete `s30-complete`

_On the way home you stop at the old wall. Clover holds the paper flat; you rub over Hush’s stone with a stub of charcoal from her range._  
_The marks come up black and fine under your hand: rings, and lines, and rings with lines through them._  
**Clover** _(soft)_: Dimple ought to have a mark of her own. On her page. A proper one.  
_In the clean corner of the paper, you draw it for her: a ring with a line through it._  
_Beside it, small and close, you draw Drizzle’s: a plain ring, just touching._  
**Clover** _(soft)_: That’s the both one. That’s her now.  
_That night you write it up properly. Hush’s stone, and a page for each of your own Nubbins, ringed and lined from what you remember._  
_In the morning there is a parcel on your step: a pale-blue rain hat, Nubbin-sized, and Fossil’s cushion, plumped for the first time in years._  
_The note says: “Drizzle’s spare hat; she has three. And the cushion: Fossil would want it sat on, not kept. —C”_  
_Under it, in loud capitals: “ALSO A RIBBON, BLUE AND GOLD. I DYED IT TWICE TO GET IT RIGHT. —SPARKS”_  

**Rewards**

- Hush’s memory page (feature): A new journal page for each of your Nubbins, laid out like Hush’s stone: a ring for a joy kept, a line for a grief kept, a ring with a line for both. Filled in from moments already in your save. A keepsake to read, never a task.
- Pale-blue rain hat (cosmetic): Drizzle’s spare rain hat, Nubbin-sized. A Nubbin wearing it turns its face up whenever it rains.
- Fossil’s windowsill cushion (decor): Flat and old, with a dent the shape of somebody small and round. Any Nubbin may sit in it. Clover says Fossil would want it sat on, not kept.
- Blue-and-gold ribbon (cosmetic): Sparks dyed it twice to match a ball nobody had seen before. The two colours turn round each other in the light.

Sets on completion: `{"s30Done":true}`

**Learning Grove swaps**

- `s30-drizzle#8` → There. That’s a bit better. Not mended. Better.
- `s30-rim#4` → Crying slows me right down, so I can feel it properly. I think that’s what it’s for.

**Notes:** THEME. Sadness has a job: it shows the people who love you where to find you. Hush kept the valley’s griefs (S1), and her stone says most things are both, a ring with a line through it. Fidget is “safe is not the same as well” in a small, funny, domestic key. Dimple is never fixed: she ends smiling most of the time, and now she can be sad too. SIGNATURE CHOICE (Tier 3, in the line). s30-ring (step 2) is paid off nine outline quests later. broke or sat: Drizzle comes on the search (s30-gone), climbs down unasked (s30-hollow), sits on the hill (s30-hill) and has her mark in the rubbing (s30-complete). helped: Drizzle stays home, and you take her place in all four (the -helped variants). broke and sat share scenes; the difference is felt in step 2 only. VARIANTS (all in SIDE_STORY_S30_VARIANTS; Scene has no conditions). s30-wall-s1 replaces s30-wall when S1’s wall memory has been seen (outline: s1Done; see SIM). s30-hole-youngest plays straight after s30-drizzle when s5Done. s30-gone-helped replaces s30-gone when s30-ring = helped. s30-trail-s5 replaces s30-trail when s5Done (Dimple walks Fossil’s last walk in reverse: water, tree, cliff). s30-hollow-helped replaces s30-hollow when s30-ring = helped. s30-hollow-youngest plays straight after s30-stopped when s5Done. s30-hill-helped replaces s30-hill, and s30-complete-helped replaces s30-complete, when s30-ring = helped. Everything else is shared and written so that it works with either companion (“leaning on somebody”, “anyone small who sat with her”). AFTERWARDS (Tier 2). Once s30Done is set, s30-after-clover, s30-after-dusty, s30-after-byte and s30-after-sparks join those Keepers’ ordinary talk lines (each plays once as new talk, then sits in their rotation like any other line). Clover’s household gains Fidget, who now shuts the shed only halfway; Dimple and Drizzle share the sill; Dimple’s ball sometimes shows blue and gold together (a rare idle state, never a stat). TIMING. Step 1 opens on the first morning after c6-close (the morning after the rain). Steps 1–6 run in chapter 6. Step 7’s scene waits until c7-stillness-3 has played (chapter 7), then plays the next time the player is in the Grove at dawn. Steps 8–9 follow in chapter 7 (the first frost week; c7-open is the first frost). Step 10’s scene waits for chapter 8 (after c8-open and its add-ons) and plays on a morning when the player is home, never during the Hollow expedition. Steps 10–14 and the complete scene are one day and play in one sitting. If chapter 9 opens before step 10 has begun, S30 closes unfinished with no rewards and no s30Done. SIM: (1) once step 10 has begun, hold chapter 9’s opening until S30 completes (same backlog rule as S5 and S27). (2) s1Done is the outline’s read for s30-wall-s1, but S1 only completes at chapter 9’s end (its last step checks chapter:9:end), so it is always false in this window. Read “S1 step s1-step2 complete” (s1-hush has played) instead; until the engine can, s30-wall plays for everyone and nothing breaks. (3) “Your youngest Nubbin” in the s5Done variants is the Nubbin that got S5’s gift (its stats sheet says “A gift from Fossil”), not whoever is youngest now. (4) Hush’s memory page: one page per Nubbin you own when S30 completes, and for each one hatched later. Rings, lines and both are drawn from save events: first hatch (ring), a farewell or a grey scar (line), a rebirth or Fossil’s gift (both), a bond with Byte’s shoelace joke or a race win (ring). It reads the save and writes nothing; no task, no timer. CHECKS. visit:s30-clover-cottage (enter Clover’s cottage); watch:s30-acorn-ring (go out to the rain butt; the choice then plays); minigame:s30-bounce-party (three short party games, no fail); watch:s30-dimple:five-things (five observation points, any order: sill, yard puddle, pond with Sparks’s racers, Byte, the bedtime hum after dusk); wait:s30-dusty-hole (stand near the hole for about twenty seconds without digging); walk:s30-hill-path:night (any night after midnight, Luna walks with you; carrying a lit lantern sends Luna to ask you to put it out); inspect:s30-hush-stone; watch:s30-shed:dusk; puzzle:s30-shed-door (hang blanket, place bucket on hem; pulling the bucket away makes Fidget squeak and drag it back, no fail); find:s30-dimple-trail (follow the dropped toys); sit:s30-spoon-hole and sit:s30-fossils-flower (hold Sit nearby; standing up early just restarts the wait); wait:s30-spoon-hole:clover-story (met when the story ends); carry:s30-basket:spoon-hole-rim (a hand-over-hand rope pull). STAGING. Mood balls carry the story: Dimple’s ball goes pale and thin in step 1, sits at Byte’s “two” at the party, goes dark on the night path, deep blue in the hollow, then blue and gold together (new two-colour swirl). Nubbin crying is a thin high whistle and a full-body shake (new SFX and anim). Fidget’s ball whirls when she is frightened and settles to a small warm orange when someone sits with her. Your Nubbin copies Dimple: the smile in step 1, the bounces in step 3, the sitting in step 14. Hush’s stone needs a close-up: hundreds of fine scratched rings and lines. Clover’s cottage needs a deep sill with the dented cushion, a shed with an outward door, a rain butt, a bucket and a washing line. CARE. No clinical words. Nobody tells Dimple to cheer up as a lesson; Sparks’s party is kind, and Ember’s and Byte’s readings are the Keepers’ own ways of seeing. The hollow is waist-deep on a person, and Dusty’s rope is there from the start. Hazel’s third rule is shown, never quoted or named. LEARNING GROVE. The outline asks for an edu version (a face and a feeling can be different; sadness helps people know you need them; sitting with a sad friend without fixing them; worries that keep us too safe; naming feelings). It is not part of this file. The two quoted lines have childLines here.

## S31. The Great Gourd

*It’s the Great Pumpkin, Charlie Brown (Peanuts, CBS 1966; Peanuts Worldwide LLC): a small boy who believes what nobody else does, and waits all night in a cold field. Theme and shape, plus three listed one-sentence quotes.* · audience: all · where: Hazel’s Grove: Tam on the Grove wall by the gate · when: Chapter 7, after c7-stillness (early autumn). Gourd Night is the first dusk after step 6. Closes when chapter 8 opens; unfinished if Gourd Night has not come.

**Cast:** Tam (Linus van Pelt (Peanuts: It’s the Great Pumpkin, Charlie Brown)), Ivy (Lucy van Pelt (Peanuts: It’s the Great Pumpkin, Charlie Brown)), Ned (Charlie Brown (Peanuts: It’s the Great Pumpkin, Charlie Brown)), Nettie (Sally Brown (Peanuts: It’s the Great Pumpkin, Charlie Brown)), Granny Brock

### Step 1: Help Tam spell his letter to the Great Gourd.

Hint: Tam is on the Grove wall by the gate. Fix the words he asks about; he will argue about some of them. · check: `puzzle:s31-letter:spelling`

#### Scene `s31-wall`

_The plums have gone soft, and the wind smells of bonfires two gardens away. Autumn is coming in at the edges._  
_A small boy is sitting on your Grove wall, wrapped in a patchwork quilt, writing very hard with his tongue out._  
_The quilt goes right down to his boots. It has been patched so often that some of the patches have patches._  
**Tam**: Don’t look. It’s private. It’s a letter.  
**Tam** _(think)_: …You can look a bit. I need a speller.  
**Tam**: I’m Tam. I live in the lane. I’m seven, and I’m writing to the Great Gourd.  
**You**: The what?  
**Tam** _(happy)_: The Great Gourd. Every Gourd Night he rises out of the most sincere gourd patch in the valley and flies over, bringing toys.  
**Tam**: Not the biggest patch. Not the tidiest. The most sincere one. He can tell.  
**Tam**: Nobody else believes in him. That’s all right. He doesn’t need everybody. He only needs one patch.  
_He holds out the letter. It begins DEAR GRAET GORD, and it has been rubbed out so hard in three places that there are holes._  

#### Done `s31-letter`

_Between you, GRAET becomes GREAT. Tam keeps GORD for a while on principle, then gives it up._  
**Tam** _(think)_: Now the important bit. What to ask for. You choose. You’re a grown-up; he might take you more seriously.  

**Choices**

- **Toys. It’s what he brings. Ask for toys.** — sets `{"s31-letter":"toys"}`
    - **You**: Ask for toys. He’s bringing them anyway.
    - **Tam**: Toys. Yes. Not greedy ones. Medium toys.
    - He writes A MEDIUM AMOUNT OF TOYS PLEASE, and underlines PLEASE twice.
- **Something for the strays in the Hollow.** — sets `{"s31-letter":"strays"}`
    - **Tam**: The strays?
    - **You**: The ones past the falls. Nobody brings them anything.
    - Tam thinks about this with his whole face.
    - **Tam**: He’d like that. He’s flying over anyway.
    - He writes SOMETHING FOR THE STRAYS IN THE HOLLOW. Then, smaller: THEY ARE NOT GREEDY.
- **Tell him, very politely, that he needn’t come if he’s busy.** — sets `{"s31-letter":"busy"}`
    - **Tam**: Not come?
    - **You**: Very politely. In case he’s busy. It’s a long night.
    - **Tam**: …That’s very thoughtful. Nobody ever thinks he might be tired.
    - He writes YOU NEEDN’T COME IF YOU ARE BUSY, WE WILL UNDERSTAND. Then, after a long think: MOSTLY.

### Step 2: Go with Tam and the lane children to Fern’s gourd patch.

Hint: Fern’s patch is over the wall next door, past the hens. Follow Ivy; she walks fast on purpose. · check: `visit:s31-gourd-patch`

#### Scene `s31-creed`

_Tam folds the letter into four and posts it down the front of his quilt, for safekeeping._  
_Two more children come down the lane: a girl with a straight fringe and a clipboard, and a boy with a round, worried face._  
**Ivy** _(angry)_: Tam. Are you writing to that pumpkin again?  
**Tam**: Gourd. He’s a gourd. A pumpkin is only one kind of gourd. Fern told me.  
**Ivy**: This is my brother. I’m Ivy. I’m ten, and I’m sorry about him in advance.  
**Ned** _(think)_: I’m Ned. Hello. Is it true he flies? Ivy says it isn’t. I’m only asking.  
_Ivy laughs. Ned laughs too, a beat late because Ivy is, and then looks as if he wishes he hadn’t._  
_Tam gets down off the wall. He draws himself up, quilt and all, to very nearly the height of the gatepost._  
**Tam**: There are three things that I’ve learned never to discuss with people: religion, politics, and the Great Gourd.  
_A very small girl has come down the lane behind them, and is listening hard._  
**Nettie** _(happy)_: I believe him. I’m Nettie. I’m six. I believe everything Tam says.  
**Ivy**: You believed the hens could read. Come on. Mum wants the biggest gourd in Fern’s patch, and Tam’s carrying it.  
_Ivy marches Nettie off by the hand. Tam follows with great dignity, holding his quilt up off the mud like a train._  

#### Done `s31-patch`

_Fern’s gourd patch is vines going in every direction at once. The gourds are lumpy and leaning, and several are warty._  
**Fern**: Sorry. Sorry about the patch. I grow them badly on purpose. It’s a style.  
_Ivy walks straight to the biggest gourd there is: orange, enormous, and the shape of a sat-on cushion._  
**Ivy**: That one. Tam, lift.  
**Tam**: It’s bigger than me.  
**Ivy**: Then you’ll have to be bigger than it. Go on.  

### Step 3: Carve a gourd lantern with Tam: a smaller, kinder one.

Hint: Pick a small gourd from Fern’s patch and carve it on the cottage step. A kind face takes longer than a fierce one. · check: `craft:s31-gourd-lantern`

#### Scene `s31-carve`

_Tam carries the gourd all the way up the lane, very slowly, like a man carrying a sleeping relative._  
_At the cottage step, Ivy takes it off him, sets it down, and fetches a big knife from the kitchen._  
**Tam**: What’s that for?  
**Ivy**: It’s nearly Gourd Night. You carve them. Everyone carves them. You put a candle in.  
_She cuts the top off in one go. Tam makes a noise like a kettle._  
**Tam** _(shock)_: Nobody told me. Nobody TOLD me you cut them.  
**Tam** _(shock)_: What if it was a relative of his?  
**Ivy**: It’s a gourd, Tam. It hasn’t got relatives. It’s got seeds.  
_She carves a face with jagged teeth and one eyebrow higher than the other. It looks a lot like Ivy._  
_Tam sits on the step with his quilt over his head. Nettie sits beside him with a tea towel over hers, to be fair._  

#### Done `s31-kinder`

_The gourd you chose is the most lopsided one in Fern’s patch. You carve it slowly, on the step beside the quilt._  
_Round eyes, no teeth, and a mouth that is mostly thinking about smiling._  
_After a while, one corner of the quilt lifts._  
**Tam** _(soft)_: …That one’s kind. He won’t mind that one.  
**Tam**: You keep it. Put it somewhere he’ll see it from the air.  

### Step 4: Witness Ivy’s promise, and watch Ned take his kick.

Hint: Ned is on the green at the end of the lane with a ball and a great deal of hope. · check: `watch:s31-ned:kick`

#### Scene `s31-ball`

_Next morning, on the green at the end of the lane, Ivy is holding a ball upright on the grass with one finger._  
**Ivy**: Ned. I’ll hold it. You run up and kick it. I won’t pull it away.  
**Ned** _(think)_: You pulled it away last time.  
**Ivy**: That was last time. This time I’ve written it down.  
_She holds out the clipboard: I PROMISE NOT TO PULL THE BALL AWAY, SIGNED IVY. Underneath is a line marked WITNESS._  
**Ned** _(soft)_: Will you witness it? Please. A grown-up. Then it counts.  

**Choices**

- **Sign as witness.**
    - You sign. Ned looks at your name on the line as if it were a bridge over a river.
    - **Ned**: Right. Right. This is it.
- **Sign, and tell Ned quietly to watch her finger.**
    - You sign, and tell Ned quietly to watch her finger.
    - **Ned**: I will. I’ll watch it the whole way.
    - **Ivy**: Witnesses aren’t allowed to coach.
- **Don’t sign. Tell Ned not to do it.**
    - **You**: Don’t do it, Ned.
    - **Ned**: But she’s written it down.
    - He signs the witness line himself, in very small letters, and backs off for his run-up.

#### Done `s31-kick`

_Ned runs with everything he has. At the very last moment Ivy lifts her finger, and the ball rolls gently away._  
_Ned kicks the air, goes up, and comes down flat on his back with a sound like a dropped sack._  
**Ned** _(sad)_: …You promised. It was witnessed.  
**Ivy**: By a biased party. Look at them. They’re obviously on your side. It doesn’t count.  
_She tucks the clipboard under her arm and walks off. Ned lies on the grass and looks at the sky, which at least stays put._  

### Step 5: Find out whether Ned is really invited to Sparks’s party.

Hint: Sparks is up a ladder outside her workshop at the top of the lane. You will hear her before you see her. · check: `talk:sparks:s31-lists`

#### Scene `s31-lists`

_Ned is still on the green when you come back past. He is holding a card cut in the shape of a cog._  
**Ned** _(happy)_: Sparks asked me to her Gourd Night party. Me. On a card.  
**Ivy**: Sparks keeps two lists. Everybody knows. One of who to ask, and one of who not to ask.  
**Ivy**: She’ll have muddled them up. You’re on the wrong one. I’m only saying it to help.  
_Ned looks at the card. The card looks back, cheerfully, being a cog._  
**Ned** _(sad)_: I didn’t think it was a mistake. I thought it was a card.  
**Tam**: Ask her. That’s what you do. You ask.  
**Ned**: I can’t ask. What if she says?  
_He holds the card out to you very carefully, as if it might go off._  

#### Done `s31-everybody`

_Sparks is up a ladder with a string of bulbs in her teeth. She takes them out to hear the question, and goes red._  
**Sparks** _(shock)_: TWO LISTS? Who said two lists? There’s one list. ONE.  
_She points at her workshop door, where it is chalked in letters a foot high: GOURD NIGHT, EVERYBODY._  
**Sparks** _(happy)_: I wrote it on the door so I couldn’t lose it. Tell Ned. No, I’ll tell Ned. NED!  
_Down the lane, a small figure on the green sits up very fast._  

### Step 6: Help Tam put up his sign in the patch he has chosen.

Hint: Tam has chosen the long grass by your fourth lantern post. The hammer is in the cottage shed. · check: `build:s31-welcome-sign`

#### Scene `s31-sincere`

_Tam has been all round the valley looking at gourd patches. He comes back with his quilt full of burrs and his mind made up._  
_He walks past three lantern posts, lit and neat, and stops at the fourth._  
_The fourth post stands dark at the edge of the long grass. There isn’t a gourd anywhere near it._  
**Tam**: This one.  
**You**: There aren’t any gourds here.  
**Tam** _(think)_: No. But it isn’t pretending. Everywhere else is tidy, for people. This one’s just itself.  
**Tam**: He’ll like that. The most sincere patch. I’ve got a feeling for sincere.  
_His sign is painted on the back of a tea tray: WELCOME GREAT GOURD. The R is backwards, on purpose, for luck._  
_He also has a toffee apple in a hanky. It’s for the Great Gourd, in case he’s hungry from flying._  

#### Done `s31-moss`

_You knock the sign in by the dark post. Tam sets the toffee apple on top and looks at it all the way people look at cathedrals._  
_Moss comes past along the hill path, slowly, the grass hardly bending under him. He stops to read the sign._  
**Tam**: Moss. You’re very old. Is the Great Gourd real?  
**Moss** _(think)_: I have never seen it. I have not seen a great many things that are real.  
_Tam nods slowly, as if that settles it in his favour. Moss goes on up the hill, and you think he might be smiling._  
_Then Clover comes over the stile with three folded blankets, puts them down by the sign, and turns straight round._  
**Clover**: I’m not discussing it. Three. One each and a spare. Don’t look at me like that.  

### Step 7: Get your Nubbin ready for Gourd Night in Sparks’s goggles and scarf.

Hint: Tap your Nubbin and choose Dress. The goggles go on top; the scarf goes round twice and still trails. · check: `place:cosmetic:s31-ace-goggles`

#### Scene `s31-dusk`

_Gourd Night. The sun goes down orange behind the hill, and all down the lane the gourds light up on the steps, one by one._  
_Ivy comes out in a pointed hat and a black cloak, with a broom she is clearly going to hit people with._  
**Ivy**: I’m a witch. A costume should be the opposite of your personality. Everyone knows that.  
_Ned is a ghost. His sheet has so many eyeholes that it is more hole than sheet._  
**Ned** _(sad)_: I had some trouble with the scissors. I kept thinking I’d got them in the wrong place.  
**Ned** _(happy)_: I can see really well, though.  
_Sparks runs down the lane with a pair of old welding goggles and a long red scarf, and drops them in your arms._  
**Sparks** _(happy)_: For your Nubbin! Every Gourd Night needs a flying ace. I wore those for twelve years. They’ve seen things.  
_Tam is already in the long grass in his quilt, sitting very straight beside his sign. Nettie sits beside him, holding his hand._  

#### Done `s31-ace`

_The goggles go on top. The scarf goes round twice and still trails on the ground._  
_Your Nubbin looks up at the darkening sky, very slowly, as if it has a score to settle with it._  

### Step 8: Decide where you’ll spend Gourd Night.

Hint: Ivy is waiting in the lane, Sparks at her workshop, and Tam in the long grass. Choose one; the others will manage. · check: `choose:s31-gourd-night`

#### Scene `s31-long-grass`

_The guisers stop at the edge of the long grass on their way out: a witch, a ghost mostly made of holes, and three small bats._  
**Ivy**: Tam. You’re going to sit in a field all night, in the cold, for a pumpkin.  
**Tam**: Gourd. And it’s a patch, not a field.  
**Ned** _(soft)_: You could come guising. There are toffee apples. You could come back after.  
**Tam**: If I’m not here when he rises, he’ll think nobody meant it. I mean it.  
_Nettie looks at the guisers, and at the bag in Ivy’s hand. Then she sits back down, firmly, next to Tam._  
**Nettie**: I’m staying. I mean it too. I think.  
**Ivy**: Fine. I’m fetching you both at four. I’ve told Mum. I’ve told Clover. Clover’s told everyone.  
_Up the lane, Clover’s window has a lamp in it. It stays lit all night._  
_Granny Brock comes down the lane with a basket, and stops at the gate to look at the sign._  
**Granny Brock**: Waiting for a gourd. In the cold. Hm.  
_She hangs a second quilt over the gate and sets a flask of soup on the wall. She says nothing else._  
_Tam skips on the spot to keep warm, to a rhyme you almost know. He gets the words wrong in a new way._  
**Ivy**: Keeper. I need a grown-up for the lane. Some of the bats are very small.  
_At the top of the lane, Sparks waves both arms at you. Behind her the workshop glows, and somebody is playing a pianola badly._  
**Sparks** _(happy)_: KEEPER! PARTY! You’re on the list! Everyone is!  
_Tam doesn’t ask you anything. He just looks at the dark post, and waits._  

**Choices**

- **Go guising with Ivy. Somebody has to mind the bats.** — sets `{"s31-night":"guise"}`
    - You take the smallest bat’s hand. It is sticky already.
    - **Tam**: Go on. When he comes, I’ll tell him you were busy. He’ll understand. Probably.
    - From the lane you look back once: two small shapes in the long grass, a sign, and the dark post.
- **Go to Sparks’s party. You did say you would.** — sets `{"s31-night":"party"}`
    - **Sparks**: YES! The Keeper’s coming! Pixel, play something faster!
    - **Tam**: Bring Nettie a sandwich. She won’t ask. She’ll want one.
    - You go up the lane towards the light. The long grass behind you gets darker as the music gets louder.
- **Sit down next to Tam and wait.** — sets `{"s31-night":"sat"}`
    - You sit down in the long grass beside Tam. It is wet, and it is going to stay wet.
    - **You**: My gran had a rule for this. Her third one.
    - **You**: When you don’t know what to do, sit down next to them and wait.
    - **Tam**: That’s a rule? Good. I like rules.
    - Ivy looks at the three of you in the long grass, opens her mouth, and shuts it again.
    - **Ivy**: Fine. Four o’clock.
    - The guisers go off down the lane. Sparks waves once more, a bit sadly, and goes in.

### Step 9: Find out how the guising went.

Hint: The guisers go door to door down the lane and back. Ned will want to show you what he got. · check: `talk:s31-ned:rock`

#### Scene `s31-guising`

_The lane is all lit gourds and cold noses. At every door the guisers tell a joke, and hold out their bags._  
_At the first door, Ivy gets a toffee apple and the bats get nuts. Ned looks in his bag for a long moment, then holds it open for you._  
_There is a rock in it._  
_At the second door: pennies and toffee for everyone, and for Ned, a rock._  
**Ned** _(think)_: …Maybe they’re running out of things.  
_At Fern’s door there are honey cakes. Fern gives everyone a honey cake, then looks at Ned, and gives him a rock._  
**Fern** _(shock)_: I’m so sorry. I panicked. It seemed to be the custom.  
_At Byte’s door, Byte looks at the witch and the bats and the ghost with too many holes, and goes back in for something._  
_He comes out with a rock. It has a little paper label tied on with string._  
_Ned doesn’t look in his bag any more. He just holds it open, and waits for the clunk._  

#### Done `s31-rock`

_Ned holds up the rock with the label, so you can read it. In Byte’s small square capitals: BASALT._  
**Ned**: I got a rock.  
**Ned** _(soft)_: But Byte wrote what it is. Nobody ever writes down what my things are.  
**Ned** _(soft)_: You have it. For being the witness. I know it didn’t count.  
_It is heavy and cold and very ordinary. You put it in your pocket, and find you keep checking it’s there._  

### Step 10: Your Nubbin is having its own night. Guide it home from the war.

Hint: Keep low along the hedges and out of the frosty grass. The lit windows of Sparks’s workshop are friendly lines. · check: `minigame:s31-flying-ace`

#### Scene `s31-ace-roof`

_Meanwhile, on the roof of the end cottage, a small figure in goggles and a long red scarf is at war._  
_Your Nubbin is the flying ace. It stands on the ridge tiles and stares into the dark at an enemy only it can see._  
_It ducks and weaves and shakes one paw at the sky. Then something only it can hear goes wrong with its engine._  
_It spins three times on the tiles, slides down the slates, and drops into the water butt with a small, heroic splash._  
_Shot down, behind the lines. It climbs out, wrings its scarf out with great dignity, and looks about for cover._  
_Far off across the hedges, Sparks’s workshop glows like a field hospital._  

#### Done `s31-ace-party`

_Your Nubbin crawls the last hedge on its belly, slips into the party between everyone’s boots, and hides in the apple-bobbing tub._  
_Ivy, back from guising, puts her hands behind her back and plunges her face in for an apple. She comes up with a lick instead._  
**Ivy** _(shock)_: Something LICKED me. Something in the TUB licked me.  
_Pixel plays old tunes on the pianola. Your Nubbin, dripping in its goggles, is merry for a jig, tragic for a slow one, and merry again._  
_It sits by the stove gazing into the middle distance, like a veteran. Then it howls, once, by accident, and looks shocked at itself._  
_Before anyone can say anything, it slinks out into the dark, scarf trailing._  

### Step 11: Midnight: Tam is still waiting in the long grass.

Hint: There’s nothing to do but wait. That is the hard part. · check: `wait:s31-long-grass:midnight`

#### Scene `s31-if`

_Nearly midnight. Three lanterns burn steady across the Grove, and the fourth post stands dark over the long grass._  
_The cold has come up out of the ground. Your knees have stopped being knees and become opinions._  
_The spare blanket stays folded on top of the sign. Tam says it’s for the Great Gourd, in case he’s cold from flying._  
**Nettie** _(sad)_: Tam. My feet have gone.  
**Nettie**: Tam. What if he isn’t coming?  
**Tam**: He’s coming. And when he comes, I’ll put in a good word for everybody. You. Ivy, even. The Keeper.  
**Tam** _(happy)_: If he comes, I’ll ask him to—  
_He stops. He heard it too._  
**Tam** _(sad)_: …If.  
**Tam** _(sad)_: I said “if”. Out loud. In the patch. He’ll have heard it. Hearing things is the whole point of him.  
_He pulls the quilt up over his head. From under it comes a small sniff, and then a much bigger one._  

**Choices**

- **“Everyone says ‘if’. Even about things they’re sure of.”**
    - **You**: Everyone says “if”, Tam. Even about things they’re sure of.
    - **Tam**: …Even you?
    - **You**: Especially me.
    - The quilt comes down as far as his eyes.
- **Say nothing. Shift a little closer.**
    - You say nothing. You shift a little closer, until your shoulder is against the quilt.
    - After a while, the quilt leans back.

#### Done `s31-midnight`

_Midnight comes and goes. Nothing rises out of the long grass except the cold._  
_An owl goes over, very low, and has a look at the sign._  
**Nettie** _(shock)_: Is that him?  
**Tam** _(sad)_: That’s an owl.  

### Step 12: Before dawn, the frost comes in over the long grass.

Hint: Nothing to do but wait. Keep your Nubbins at the edge, out of the white grass. · check: `wait:s31-long-grass:frost`

#### Scene `s31-frost`

_In the small hours the frost comes, the way it has every dawn this autumn._  
_It creeps out of the long grass in a long white tongue, straight for the dark post. The grass goes stiff and silver as it passes._  
_Tam is awake. He has been awake all night, out of principle._  
_Nettie is asleep against him with her mouth open and one boot off._  
**Tam** _(shock)_: Here it comes. Here he comes. That’s what the cold is. It’s him, getting near.  
_Out in the white grass, by the post, something moves._  

#### Done `s31-shape`

_A shape rises at the edge of the white grass, where the frost hasn’t reached. Round, and goggled, and trailing a scarf._  
_Tam takes one look, says “Oh,” very politely, and faints clean away._  
_It is your Nubbin, home from the war. It climbs into your lap, still damp from the water butt, and falls asleep at once._  
**Nettie**: He does that. He fainted at a goose once. Leave him; he likes it.  

### Step 13: See Tam safely home, then wait for the light.

Hint: Ivy is coming at four, as promised. After that, stay a while in the long grass until it gets light. · check: `wait:s31-long-grass:first-light`

#### Scene `s31-four`

_At four o’clock exactly, a lantern comes bobbing down the lane, grumbling._  
**Ivy** _(angry)_: Four o’clock. I said four. Look at the state of you all.  
_She looks at Tam, fainted in his quilt, and doesn’t laugh. She hands her lantern to Nettie and lifts him, quilt and all._  
_He doesn’t wake. His boots hang down, and she hitches him up and doesn’t complain once._  
_She has a blanket over her arm, brought for him. He doesn’t need it; he has the quilt._  
_On the way past, she drops it on you without looking._  
**Ivy**: You look frozen. It’s very stupid, sitting out all night. I’m not saying anything nice about it.  
_For Ivy, that is something nice._  
_She goes off up the lane with Tam, and Nettie trots after with the lantern, yawning, very small._  

#### Done `s31-light`

_Up the lane, a light goes on in the end cottage. Ivy gets Tam up the stairs, takes his boots off, and tucks him in._  
_Then she walks back to her own room and shuts the door. Nobody ever hears about it from her._  
_In the long grass it gets light, slowly. The frost comes off the post in a thin steam._  
_Tam’s sign is still standing, WELCOME GREAT GOURD, with the R still backwards for luck._  

### Step 14: Find Tam at the Grove wall.

Hint: He’s on the wall by the gate, where he wrote the letter. He has slept until lunchtime. · check: `talk:s31-tam:next-year`

#### Scene `s31-next-year`

_Tam sleeps until lunchtime. Then he is back on the Grove wall in his quilt, looking at the long grass, with his chin out._  
_Ned is sitting beside him, with a bit of ghost sheet still tucked into his collar, trying to help._  
**Ned** _(soft)_: I’ve done loads of silly things. I tried to kick a ball Ivy was holding. Nine times. Ten, now.  
**Ned**: So you mustn’t feel bad. About waiting in a patch all night for nothing. Everyone does silly things.  
**Tam** _(angry)_: Silly?  
**Tam** _(angry)_: He didn’t come because somebody said “if”. That’s a technical problem. That isn’t silly.  
**Ned**: And Ivy’s told everybody. You must feel awful.  
_Along the lane, Nettie walks past the end of the wall with her nose in the air, and does not look at Tam at all._  
**Tam**: Well, that’s nothing compared to the fury of a woman who’s been cheated out of guising!  
**Tam** _(happy)_: He’ll come next year. Next Gourd Night. I’ll find a patch so sincere he can’t help himself.  
_He goes on saying so for some time. Ned, who is kind, listens to all of it._  

#### Done `s31-promise`

_When Ned has gone home, Tam turns round on the wall, quilt and all, and looks at you._  
**Tam**: You sat. All night. Even the cold bit.  
**Tam** _(soft)_: Next year. Will you wait with me again?  

**Choices**

- **“Yes.”**
    - **You**: Yes.
    - **Tam**: Good. I’ll put you on the list. There’s only one list. It says everybody, but you’re at the top.
